## ⚠️ SCOPE CORRECTION (read this first)
The user's goal was misread for most of the build. It is NOT a causal growth thesis.
**Actual goal:** a present-day investment screen — find hexes that are already walkable and
populated but MISSING one obvious daily-needs business (one that comparable areas normally
have), because that missing business is the opportunity to open TODAY. Per-hex geography.
Ranked by resident population for now ("people affected" = walking-catchment population is a
deferred refinement).

**The deliverable is `analysis.hex_gaps` + `loci gaps` + the map "The Missing Business"**
(https://claude.ai/code/artifact/0a87f73d-8b24-4c10-8418-aaea04d2811a): filter by business,
every walkable area missing it lights up. 726 gap hexes (was 648 before SNAP/SLA/FSQ anchors; see session 8). Top gap types: hardware (237),
bodega/convenience (166), clinic (132), fitness (120), childcare (62), laundry (27), pharmacy (22) (counts as of the 648 run; re-tabulate).

Method: a category counts as a conspicuous gap only if its prevalence ≥80% among walkable areas
(so a missing tailor — 40% — is noise, a missing grocery — 97% — is real). Hex qualifies if
≥12/15 categories present yet missing ≥1 expected business.

**The earlier E3 causal work (supply model, residual, growth regression) answered the WRONG
question** and is retained only as honest negative science, NOT the product. The residual/growth
framing (D1) was my reframing, not the user's hypothesis. Do not present it as the deliverable.

## How to resume

```bash
cd ~/Documents/Projects/abenmayor/loci
claude --continue
```

A **fresh session is required** to pick up MCP servers connected mid-session — this is how
the Linear tools become available.

Then say: **"read docs/CHECKPOINT.md and continue"**.

**Canonical pipeline order (D78 — MN+BK only; never `--borough ALL`):**

```bash
uv run loci street-frame            # D84: refresh the street points parquet (address-gaps consumes it; --no-street-frame to skip)
uv run loci address-gaps            # default MNBK; --borough MN then --borough BK if it runs long (~10 min each)
uv run loci address-demand --borough MNBK
uv run loci pipeline && uv run loci storefronts && uv run loci age-fit apply --category all
uv run loci supply-ratio --boroughs MN,BK        # NO --fit-baseline
uv run loci address-access --boroughs MN,BK      # ~9 min; address-gaps NULLs the seven access columns
uv run loci transit-profile --boroughs MN,BK --re-sweep   # re-apply after address-gaps; --re-sweep required after any frame change (D84)
uv run loci citibike address-measures --boroughs MN,BK
uv run loci address-character build --boroughs MN,BK && uv run loci storefront-pipeline openings --boroughs MN,BK
uv run loci dot-counts address-context
uv run loci address-legality build   # D97/D104: PLUTO zoning legality; re-apply after every address-gaps re-run
uv run loci revenue --boroughs MN,BK
uv run loci export-webmap
```

**Seed spec for D97-D100 (Ouroboros, immutable):** `.ouroboros/seed.yaml` — 23 acceptance criteria, deliver in order zoning → closure → search → report.

  directions) — csr_matrix SUMS duplicates, so every distance was 2×. Fixed (min per pair).

**CAUSAL TEST DONE — the naive thesis is empirically REJECTED (honest null/reversal).**
P2 β=+0.069 (wrong sign, p=5e-16); pre-trend breaks parallel trends (gap = momentum, not latent
demand); placebo clean. Loci is a valid descriptive tool; the investment inference is not
supported. Recorded as CONTEXT §0. Done: GTM-36/37/40/41/42. This is the project's central result.

  claimed by a question. Warns on load-bearing E3/E4 tickets with no question behind them.
  Wired into `make check`.
- **`loci` CLI** — `init-db`, `sources`, `check-sources`, `check-questions`, `gen-tickets` all work.
  `ingest`/`grid`/`score`/`model`/`export` raise `NotImplementedError` pointing at their
  epic.
- **Tickets** — 6 milestones, 61 issues (two exploratory E3 tests added in session 4), 219 points, generated by `loci gen-tickets` and
  **pushed to Linear** (GTM-5 … GTM-62). Verified: all 58 assigned to a milestone, none
  orphaned. E0 sits at 71% (5 of 7 done).
  Project: https://linear.app/avi-benmayor/project/loci-723fa10296fb

## Decision log

Append-only. Each entry says what was decided **and why**, so a future session doesn't
relitigate it.

**Collapse rule (2026-09-15).** Entries dated **on or before 2026-09-11** (more than five
sessions back) that ran five lines or longer and no longer state a rule the current phase must
obey have been collapsed to a single `**Dnn — … → archive` line. Their **full original text,
unchanged**, lives in **`docs/CHECKPOINT-ARCHIVE.md`** under the same D-ids. The archive is
**append-only** and is **not read at session start** — the briefing agent reads this file only.
Open the archive when a stub's reasoning is actually needed, or when a stub is about to be
relitigated. Anything still load-bearing (scope rulings, live gates, traps the database will not
enforce) was kept verbatim here regardless of age; everything dated 2026-09-13 or later is
verbatim.

**D1 — The thesis is a residual, not a raw count; the causal framing later failed and was scope-corrected away.** *(2026-09-01)* → archive

**D2 — LODES WAC is the panel backbone; license-date reconstruction deferred to E5.** *(2026-09-01)* → archive

**D3 — Commercial zoning capacity (PLUTO CommFAR/ZoneDist) is a required supply-model control.** *(2026-09-01)* → archive

**D4 — Access is one multi-source Dijkstra per category, not ~7,400 per-hex isochrones.** *(2026-09-01)* → archive

**D5 — Docker CLI symlinked at user level to avoid sudo; moot for Loci since D9.** *(2026-09-01)* → archive

**D6 — Epics are Linear Milestones, not Projects.** *(2026-09-01)*
The Loci project already exists in Linear; mapping epics to Projects would have created six
competing projects. Milestones nest correctly and drive the overview progress bars.

**D7 — DNCI is a weighted geometric mean.** *(2026-09-01)*
An arithmetic mean lets a hex with fifty restaurants and no grocery, pharmacy or laundromat
score well — exactly the failure the project exists to detect. Mandatory adversarial unit
test in E2.

**D8 — h3-pg is not a dependency; superseded by D9, DuckDB's community h3 extension.** *(2026-09-01, superseded by D9)* → archive

**D9 — DuckDB replaces PostGIS.** *(2026-09-01)*
The only available PostGIS image ran **emulated** (amd64 on arm64), and the "service path"
rationale for Postgres was speculative. DuckDB removes the container, the daemon and the
emulation penalty. Verified before committing: `spatial` + community `h3` extensions,
RTREE indexes, `ST_Read`, `read_parquet`, schemas, composite PKs, FKs, CHECK constraints,
JSON and GEOMETRY columns all work.
**Caveat the database will not enforce:** DuckDB `GEOMETRY` carries **no SRID**. Everything
stored is EPSG:4326 by convention; metric work must reproject explicitly. Hold this in code.

**D10 — Three root directories named by audience: docs/, src/, data/; scripts became CLI subcommands.** *(2026-09-01)* → archive

**D11 — Linear label taxonomy collapsed 32 to 14 via LABEL_MAP in tickets.py.** *(2026-09-01)* → archive

**D12 — LODES panel proceeds without a crosswalk; pre-2020 years are retro-allocated, leakage filed as GTM-63.** *(2026-09-01)* → archive

**D13 — DCWP contributes zero daily-needs POIs; laundromats come from OSM/Overture instead.** *(2026-09-01)* → archive

**D13 — docs/QUESTIONS.md is a sanctioned fourth document with its own lifetime and drift check.** *(2026-09-01)* → archive

**D14 — Grid boundary is the 2020 NTA file; cells labelled by maximum areal overlap (hex era, see D38).** *(2026-09-01)* → archive

**D15 — OSM Overpass needs mirror rotation and must fail loud.** *(2026-09-01)*
The main overpass-api.de endpoint 504s frequently; the adapter rotates to lz4.overpass-api.de
first, then main, then kumi. Critically, it now RAISES if every mirror is down rather than
returning 0 — a silent 0-row ingest for a core source would be invisible corruption. General
rule for any live-API source: fail loud on total failure, never ingest a silent zero.

**D16 — DuckDB `ST_Distance_Sphere` reads POINT(x, y) as (latitude, longitude).** *(2026-09-02)*
Our geometry is stored (lon, lat) per D9, so the raw call computes every distance at the
equator: 1° of longitude at NYC came back as 111 km instead of 84 km, and a gap hex's nearest
hardware store as 359 m instead of the true 1,228 m. Caught by a sanity check after an
aggregate disagreed with a walk-graph sample. **Rule:** never call the function on stored
geometry directly; wrap both arguments in `ST_FlipCoordinates` (the `METRES` expression in
`validation/sample.py`, pinned by a unit test). Same family as the no-SRID caveat: the
database will not tell you.

**D16 — SNAP and SLA are the coverage anchors for the bottom-biased tiers.** *(2026-09-02)* → archive

**D17 — Manhattan has no validated gaps (hex era); superseded by D48's Manhattan+Brooklyn scope.** *(2026-09-02)* → archive

**D18 — "Best investment" is a feasibility-gated Investability Index (Axis 1), not homes-affected.** *(2026-09-02)* → archive

**D19 — Trajectory ("Rising") is a second axis the owner added, kept separate from D1.** *(2026-09-02)* → archive

**D20 — Real ACS income momentum replaces the LODES job-growth proxy in Rising.** *(2026-09-02)* → archive

**D21 — East New York sized as a rooftops play; its value-gym call was overturned by D23.** *(2026-09-02)* → archive

**D22 — Premium/destination amenities are Axis 3, an inverted trade-area method, not the daily-needs screen.** *(2026-09-02)* → archive

**D22 — Hosted read-only query app for sharing the dataset; built, then taken down (GTM-85).** *(2026-09-02)* → archive

**D23 — Maturity curve and 2033 projection are Axis 4, not a revival of D1; see D25.** *(2026-09-02)* → archive

**D23 — Gym/ENY recommendation overturned; gaps need category-radius and walkability-demand gating.** *(2026-09-02)* → archive

**D25 — The 2033 maturity projection fails its 10-year backtest; ships as ranking and scenario only.** *(2026-09-02, overnight — numbered D25 because a parallel session already used D22/D23 above)* → archive

**D26 — Ignition is exogenous: a curated catalyst layer, not zoning or trajectory (Axis 4b).** *(2026-09-02)* → archive

**D27 — Catalysts run on two clocks: construction 5-9 years, demographic tip 10-20 years and only if market-rate.** *(2026-09-02)* → archive

**D28 — Axis 3 v1 built; padel has zero supply rows; premium demand must be steeply top-weighted.** *(2026-09-02)* → archive

**D28 — Scope arc: one corrected pivot, then deliberate expansion toward a where/what/when investment dataset.** *(2026-09-02)* → archive

**D29 — Google "found at least one" is not a coverage hole; split every result three ways (see D90).** *(2026-09-03)* → archive

**D30 — Clinic is excluded from headline gap claims until redefined.** *(2026-09-03)*
Why: loci's clinic category deliberately excludes doctor's offices (foursquare_places.py) and no
external source reproduces that exclusion, so the clinic gap is unfalsifiable by construction.
Google's `doctor` type saturates at the API cap (median 20 results/hex). Decision: re-anchor to
licensed urgent care (NYS Article 28 / DOHMH) rather than drop; until then clinic is not cited.

**D31 — The walk threshold is not one citywide constant; refined by D33, settled by D41.** *(2026-09-03)* → archive

**D32 — Validator bug fixed (commit 877d162): canonical deduplicated local counts, and .env now loads.** *(2026-09-03)* → archive

**D33 — The "missing" rule is definitionally broken, not mistuned; monotonicity is the acceptance test.** *(2026-09-03)* → archive

**D34 — Reach rule lands the monotonicity fix but the p80 calibration is rejected; not publishable.** *(2026-09-05)* → archive

**D35 — Spacing and p80 reach calibrations rejected; external walk-time tiers recommended, adopted by D41.** *(2026-09-05)* → archive

**D36 — 0 m same-type spacing is mostly a snapping artifact; the real duplication is DOHMH turnover.** *(2026-09-05)* → archive

**D37 — Income enters the gap screen as an annotation, never as a filter (Meltzer & Schuetz 2012).** *(2026-09-05)*
Why: Meltzer & Schuetz (EDQ 26(1):73–94, 208 NYC ZIPs, ZBP 1998–2007) show low-income NYC neighborhoods (<80% of citywide mean HH income) are NOT under-supplied by count in necessity categories — groceries are MORE numerous per acre there (0.051 vs 0.036) though smaller — while food service, gyms (0.29 vs 1.04 per ZIP), bars and upscale chains concentrate in higher-income areas. So a missing gym or cafe in a low-income hex is plausibly demand-following, not conspicuous; a missing grocery or pharmacy there is a real gap. Loci's gap rule used no income at all. Decision: (a) new package data `src/loci/demand.yaml` classifies each of the 15 categories as `necessity` or `discretionary` with an `evidence: paper|assumed` field — paper-backed: grocery/convenience/pharmacy necessity, restaurant/cafe_bakery/bar/fitness discretionary; the other eight are owner priors marked `assumed` and must survive contrarian review before the flag is shown on the map; (b) every gap row now carries `median_hh_income`, `renter_share`, `income_class` (low if below 0.80 × citywide population-weighted mean over inhabited hexes) and `demand_caveat` = discretionary ∧ low-income, plus a `caveated_missing` list; (c) the lead-missing category prefers a non-caveated gap; (d) the annotation NEVER changes which hexes or (hex, category) pairs are gaps — tests/test_demand_caveat.py proves set equality with a demand-unaware re-derivation for both rules, and the monotonicity battery is untouched. Result on real data: window rule 726 hexes (unchanged), 987 pairs, 91 caveated (9.2%) — fitness 42% (58/138), bar 36%, all necessity categories 0%; reach rule 1,707 hexes (unchanged), 6,189 pairs, 918 caveated (14.8%) — tailor_repair 42%, fitness 41%, bar 38%, nails_beauty 35%, cafe_bakery 33%, restaurant 29%. Reading: nearly half of the fitness "gaps" sit in low-income hexes where the literature says gyms follow income, which weakens fitness as a headline category the same way D29/D30 weakened clinic. Why annotation not filter: filtering would bake demand-side assumptions into the gap set and hide the D8 calibration problem behind them; and the same paper warns that race predicts retail net of income (X6), so an income-only filter could launder under-provision as "no demand". Also recorded from the paper: transit and retail floor space do NOT explain the income disparity (low-income ZIPs have more of both), so subway access must not be used as an expected-supply covariate; count-based reach is one-dimensional vs size/diversity (D9); reusable robustness battery (exclude Manhattan, exclude tiny units, alternate cutoffs) and symmetric growth rate. Side fixes: `build_gaps` now inserts by column name not position; `gaps-sweep` read `missing_expected` by index and was fixed to `r[-1]`; stray `__write_probe__.txt` deleted.

**D38 — The gap screen's geography is the residential address, not the hex.** *(2026-09-05, owner)*
Why: the owner stated it plainly this session — "no more hexes." The address-level screen already
exists (`model/address_gaps.py`, committed 2026-09-02 with the Railway webmap: per PLUTO residential
lot, one multi-source Dijkstra per category, same-gap buildings clustered) but it was never recorded
as the geography of record, so the 2026-09-04/05 sessions built D6/D33–D35 on hexes. What carries
over unchanged: the reach rule's logic (a location is a gap for c iff its nearest c is beyond
reach(c); eligibility is per-category reach too), the monotonicity acceptance test, the D35 verdict
that spacing and p80 calibrations are unusable and external walk-time tiers are the candidate, the
D36 DOHMH duplication fix, and the conveniences engine (`compute_address_convenience`) which already
yields per-address nearest distance for all 15 categories. What does not carry over and must be
redone at address level: the D34 acceptance-battery numbers (gap counts, list stability, lead
distribution, coverage correlation), the eligibility gate's K-of-15 threshold, and the clustering
step that turns same-gap buildings into an actionable site. address_gaps.py's current rule (800 m
window + ≥80% expected) has exactly the D33 defect and must move to the reach rule. Hex tables
(analysis.hex_gaps, hex_gaps_reach) stay in place as history; nothing new is built on them. The
address-level conveniences check (owner-set norms) and the address-level gap screen (reach rule) are
now the same geography; whether they remain two claims or merge is decided with the D8 tier source.

**D39 — Address-level reach screen needs a fixed gate for monotonicity; ranking must be continuous, not exactly-one.** *(2026-09-05)* → archive

**D40 — Census ZIP Business Patterns added as a validation-and-calibration source, never a gap-flag input.** *(2026-09-05)*
Why: Meltzer & Schuetz measured NYC retail with ZBP establishment counts by NAICS; Loci had no establishment count independent of its POI feeds. Built: `census_zbp` registry entry, `src/loci/zbp_naics.yaml` (NAICS 2017 crosswalk for the 15 categories, tailor_repair single medium-confidence code), `sources/universal/census_zbp.py`, `loci ingest-zbp` → `analysis.zip_establishments` (37,804 rows, 6-digit NAICS, 217 NYC PLUTO ZIPs, CBP 2023) and `analysis.zip_category_establishments` (size bands <5 / 5–9 / 10–19 / 20+), `loci zbp-compare` → `analysis.zip_coverage_check`; tests/test_zbp.py (8 passing; full suite 80 passed + the known test_comps failure). Probe facts: the standalone `zbp` API stops at 2018; from 2019 ZIP data lives in the `cbp` endpoint; a comma-separated ZIP list works in one call (~14 s); `in=state` is rejected; suppressed cells are absent rows, not zeros. FINDING (QUESTIONS M9): Loci POI counts exceed ZBP establishments by a median 2–8× in most categories, and the overcount is concentrated in the license-registry-anchored categories (restaurant 3.2×, cafe 3.5×, hair 2.8×, nails 8.0×, bar 6.0×) plus fitness 5.9×, while OSM/Overture-only categories sit near 1 (childcare 0.85, clinic 0.95, laundry 1.3, pharmacy 1.5). Part of this is CBP's employer-only universe, but bank 2.2× and hardware 3.2× are not sole-proprietor trades, so POI duplication or stale records is the working hypothesis — the same mechanism D36 found in DOHMH. Consequence: every revealed-spacing reach value is biased tight in those categories and their gaps are under-reported; the D8 tier work should treat license-anchored categories as suspect until the per-source rerun lands. Geography: ZIP is coarse; POI→ZIP is a majority-vote hex→ZIP crosswalk from PLUTO and ZIP population is summed dasymetric hex population, so the ratios are diagnostic, not load-bearing. Hex tables are used here only as a crosswalk, not as gap geography (D38 respected).

**D41 — Reach table adopted: cited walk thresholds where they exist, owner norms elsewhere;
ranking is continuous.** *(2026-09-05, owner)*
Why: D35 showed external walk-time tiers are the only calibration whose per-category counts carry
information, D39 showed the tier assignment is load-bearing and any binary "exactly one missing"
list is a knife-edge, and the tier-source research found only 4 of 15 categories have a citable
walk threshold (grocery 800 m — USDA FARA 0.5 mi urban / NYC FRESH / Portland 20-min; pharmacy
800 m — Guadamuz & Qato 2021 low-income threshold; restaurant and cafe_bakery 400 m — Walk Score
full-credit radius), 3 have analogs (convenience 400, bar 400, fitness 1,200 vs CDC's 1 mi), and 8
have no walk-scale literature (laundry, hair_barber, nails_beauty, tailor_repair, childcare, clinic,
bank, hardware; clinic and bank standards are drive-based). Owner adopted `src/loci/reach_tiers.yaml`
as the reach table — cited values where they exist, the owner's conveniences.yaml norms for the
rest, every row carrying its basis and citation — and a CONTINUOUS ranking: per address,
ratio(c) = nearest_m(c) / reach(c), gap_score = max ratio, lead = argmax, n_missing = count of
ratio > 1. Tier edges therefore decide only the gap flag, never the order. The eligibility gate
stays fixed and reach-independent (≤800 m for ≥12/15; D39) so monotonicity holds. Units are
capped at 500 per lot for any unit-weighted ranking (D39 mega-lot artifact). reach.yaml (p80)
stays available as `--reach p80` for comparison only. Implementation: `model/address_gaps.py`
rewritten, `loci address-gaps`, `analysis.address_gaps`, tests/test_address_gaps.py — see Session
10. Caveats carried: the −0.6 coverage correlation (M1) is untouched by any of this; 8 of 15 reach
values rest on the owner's judgment, and pharmacy's 800 m is the one cited value that moves the
most gaps.

**D42 — NAICS 2022 adopted as the category-taxonomy anchor via `src/loci/categories.yaml`.** *(2026-09-05)*
The 15 category slugs (`src/loci/categories.py`) were ad hoc keys scattered across
reach.yaml/conveniences.yaml/demand.yaml/benchmarks.yaml/spend.yaml, the OSM tag map, and
NYC/NYS license-type maps, with no single place stating what each slug means in NAICS
terms — the vocabulary every downstream comparison (ZBP/CBP, SBA, LODES, Meltzer & Schuetz)
actually speaks. `categories.yaml` is now that pivot: one entry per slug with `naics_2022`,
`naics_2017`, `label`, and each adapter's native tag (`osm`, `overture`, `dohmh`, `nys_dos`,
`dcwp`, `nys_sla`). It does NOT classify individual POIs — no POI source publishes NAICS —
it only anchors definitions. NAICS 2022 reference files (Structure, Descriptions, and both
concordance directions) were fetched from census.gov/naics (direct file paths; the `/naics/`
landing and search-style URLs are Cloudflare-blocked at 403) and reduced to
`src/loci/naics/naics_2022.csv` (2,122 rows, 2–6 digit) so nothing needs to open an xlsx at
runtime. `tests/test_categories_anchor.py` checks every slug in use is anchored, every
naics_2022 code is real, and no two slugs share a 6-digit code.

Two corrections the crosswalk surfaced, neither caught before because nothing previously
joined these categories against a verified NAICS 2022 code:
1. **The retail-tier 2017→2022 recode moved three of our codes**, not just the two the task
   anticipated: convenience 445120→445131, pharmacy 446110→456110, **and** hardware
   444130→444140 (all real, confirmed against the concordance file — `naics_2017` in
   categories.yaml keeps the pre-recode codes for panel years before 2022).
2. **`registry.yaml`'s `lodes_wac.naics` block was internally inconsistent**: it listed
   4-digit codes as if LODES WAC published NAICS, but WAC only ever publishes 20 fixed
   sector columns (CNS01–CNS20, e.g. CNS07 = all of Retail Trade 44-45) — no WAC column is
   narrower than a full sector. The 4-digit codes there are aspirational targets, realizable
   only from ZBP/CBP; the block is now annotated to say so explicitly (recoded to 2022,
   pharmacy's prefix corrected 4461→4561) rather than silently implying a join that can't
   happen.

**D43 — The adopted tiers are a floor standard; mature Manhattan's market standard is 3-7x tighter.** *(2026-09-05)* → archive

**D44 — Address-level reach screen shipped citywide; ratio ranking dominated by tailor_repair and laundry (scope drift, see D48).** *(2026-09-05)* → archive

**D45 — The universal layer is universal in logic, NYC-hardcoded in constants (GTM-89 verified on London).** *(2026-09-05)*
Why: E8 assumed the universal core runs outside NYC; CLAUDE.md says verify before designing around a dependency. A Sonnet spike ran the real pipeline pieces on a central-London bbox with live Overture S3, Overpass and OSMnx calls, writing only to scratchpad. Category mapping populated all 15 categories (Overture 7,591 + OSM 4,334 normalized POIs, plausible: Waitrose/Boots/London pubs), `score/dedup.py` ran unmodified (9,688 clusters), and the walk-graph builder is already parameterized (16,051 nodes). But the Overture and Overpass adapters expose BBOX/CACHE_PATH only as module constants (monkeypatch needed), and `grid/build.py` hard-requires `nyc_nta2020.geojson` + EPSG:2263, so the H3 grid step is not runnable for another city today even though bare H3 clipping works. Anchor loss is the real second-city cost: 7 of 15 categories (restaurant, cafe_bakery, bar, hair_barber, nails_beauty, grocery, convenience) lose their near-census anchor (DOHMH, SLA, NYS DOS, SNAP) and fall back to raw Overture/OSM confidence — and D40/M9 shows those anchors are themselves inflating NYC counts, so a second city may be closer to truth in count and worse in coverage. Decision: GTM-89 closed as verified; one E8 ticket carries the six constant-level fixes (adapter constructor args, parameterized build_grid, generic region columns, injectable anchor map in source_rank). No E8 design work before it lands. No `sources.cities.nyc` import leaks into universal/, model/ or score/ — the isolation rule is holding.

**D46 — NYC only, for a long time.** *(2026-09-05, owner)*
Why: the owner said so plainly after the GTM-89 result. The second-city milestone (E8) is parked: the parameterization ticket from D45 is filed at Low priority and blocks nothing, and no session should propose E8 work or design for city-agnosticism beyond what the isolation rule already gives for free. Depth in NYC — address-level screen, anchor quality (D36/D40), demand-side annotation (D37), and the Axis 3/4 extensions — is the whole roadmap. Revisit only when the owner raises it.

**D47 — The POI overcount is mostly uncorroborated single-source records, not stale licenses; fitness and hardware have no license anchor (corrects part of D40).** *(2026-09-05)*
Why: `loci zbp-compare --by-source` (GTM-108) split M9's ratios by the canonical record's source and by whether any second feed corroborates the cluster. Only six sources are loaded (Overture, Foursquare, DOHMH, SLA, NYS DOS, SNAP); OSM Overpass, FDIC, NYS pharmacy and DOHMH childcare are still `planned` with zero rows, so D40's grouping of fitness (and hardware) with the license-anchored categories was wrong — both are Overture+Foursquare only. Dropping every single-source record moves restaurant 3.4→1.09, cafe_bakery 3.55→1.12, hardware 3.37→0.86, hair_barber 2.58→0.65 (so the corroborated core already matches or undershoots ZBP), while bar 5.85→1.54, fitness 6.8→1.74 and nails_beauty 8.9→1.77 keep a residual. Staleness is ruled out where testable (SLA: 0 of 3,199 bar licenses expired before 2024) and untestable elsewhere because the DOHMH adapter never fetches inspection/grade dates and the NYS DOS adapter never fetches `license_expiration_date` despite documenting it; SLA misclassification is ruled out because the adapter already keeps on-premises classes only; DOS booth-renter fragmentation is 1.8% of nails rows. Worst nails ZIPs include Elmhurst (37×) and Williamsburg (24×), not just CBDs, so it is not a daytime-population artifact. Reading: the residual in bar/fitness/nails is most likely dedup misses (registry owner-name vs storefront-name, and the 40 m match radius), i.e. the same mechanism as D36 but not confined to DOHMH. Decisions: (a) for the reach-tier calibration, run the supply set both ways — all canonical POIs vs corroborated-only (≥2 sources, or anchor+1) — and report the reach values under each; if they diverge materially, corroborated-only is the default; (b) the DOHMH and NYS DOS adapters should fetch their date fields so the staleness test can be run at all (feeds the D36 active-filter work); (c) M9 stays in-progress until the dedup-miss hypothesis is tested by name-matching a sample of nails clusters by hand or via Google.

**D48 — Scope is Manhattan + Brooklyn (dense, walking-dominant); leads are graded, not filtered; the D44 citywide run was drift.** *(2026-09-08, owner)*
Three owner rulings in session 13, made when the assistant presented the D44 lead-viability candidates as "which rule knocks laundry out of the top 200." (1) Scope: Manhattan and Brooklyn, as it has been throughout. The `--borough ALL` run in D44 was drift; Staten Island and Queens results should not surface. Density IS a factor, but the model optimizes for dense, walking-dominant areas first so it stays simple; driving-dominant areas are a future expansion, not a current constraint to design around. CONTEXT.md's "five boroughs" scope line is updated accordingly. (2) Method: refine the logic, never optimize for an outcome. Tabulating candidate rules by the category mix they produce in the top 200 is outcome-tuning and is rejected as a way to choose. (3) Shape: Loci is a gradient, not a filter. The output is a GRADED recommendation; when the grade is high enough the recommendation is to act, at a fair price (hand-off to the comps/fair-value work, O7/O8). Hard filters — a supply floor, a fixed excess minimum as a cutoff — are the wrong shape. The excess-minimum idea (D44 candidate b) is sound as a grade INPUT: absolute excess meters carry information the ratio loses. Numbers from the session-13 tabulation kept only as description: the citywide top 200 by max-ratio is entirely laundry, 186 of them on Staten Island, because laundry's 320 m reach is the tightest of the 15 categories; Manhattan+Brooklyn-only description is being produced separately. Supersedes the "lead-viability rule" framing of D44's next step; the next step is a grade design (data-scientist proposal, then contrarian), described on Manhattan+Brooklyn only.

**D49 — demand.yaml's necessity/discretionary split is derived from spend.yaml's BLS CEX income elasticity (cut: > 0.35 → discretionary), not owner priors; the 0.80 cutoff denominator is citywide MEAN household income; the caveat is continuous and MOE-gated.** *(2026-09-08, owner, on contrarian review GTM-109)*

Why: the eight `assumed` rows contradicted a sourced file three directories away — spend.yaml already carried a continuous BLS CEX elasticity per category, and no cut on that scale reproduced the hand-coded binary (tailor_repair 0.30 = hair_barber, classed opposite; nails_beauty 0.35 = grocery, classed opposite). The 0.35 cut reproduces all seven Meltzer & Schuetz paper-backed rows exactly and is the only cut in {0.30, 0.34, 0.35, 0.40, 0.45} that does, so it is identified, not chosen. It sends all eight former priors to necessity; nails_beauty and tailor_repair flip. The old denominator was a population-weighted mean of tract medians ($88,154, cutoff $70,523); the paper's quantity is citywide mean household income, household-weighted — ACS 2023 5-year B19025/B11001 over the five counties gives $127,894 ± $988 (cutoff $102,315), cached at data/interim/citywide_mean_hh_income.json and exposed as `loci citywide-income`. Median income MOE is 27% of the estimate and more than half of gap hexes sit within one MOE of the line, so the binary badge is retired: gaps.py now emits `income_ratio`, `income_ratio_moe`, `income_indeterminate`, `demand_caveat_text` (X6 disclaimer appended), and `demand_caveat`/`lead_missing` key off the MOE-confident test (ratio + moe < 0.80). Clinic is excluded from the annotation (D30). The `evidence` vocabulary now refuses `assumed`. Caveated pairs: window 91 → 119, reach 918 → 765 (stored D37 tables; the review's intermediate prediction of 582 under old denominator reproduced exactly). Annotation verified non-filtering by a test that runs the screen with it forced off and asserts identical gap sets. Not yet ported to addresses (GTM-110). Renderers must not truncate `demand_caveat_text` — the disclaimer is its tail.

**D50 — Google Places validation harness fixed (primary-type filtering, 20-result cap recorded, returned types persisted) and the type map corrected per the GTM-105 audit; clinic removed from the Google map; existing clinic and fitness validation rows are not usable as counts.** *(2026-09-08, GTM-105)*

Why: both call sites (validation/google_places.py, webmap/server.js) filtered on `includedTypes`, which matches any of a place's types, while loci assigns one category per place — a bakery-cafe counted under restaurant and cafe_bakery, a bodega under grocery and convenience, a salon under both hair and nails. Switched to `includedPrimaryTypes`. Nearby Search (New) caps at 20 results with no pagination, and `nearby_count` returned `len(places)`, so counts were right-censored: clinic hit the cap in 867/990 sampled hexes (87.6%), fitness in 151/990 (15.3%), hardware in 0. New columns `n_ground_truth_at_cap` and `ground_truth_types` (primary-type histogram, JSON) on analysis.coverage_validation; existing rows are NULL (unknown), not false. Mapping fixes: beauty_salon moved from nails_beauty to hair_barber (it is NAICS 812112, hair's anchor); cafe_bakery gains donut/bagel/ice-cream/juice/dessert/tea (loci's own DOHMH keywords already include them, so Google was narrower than loci and biased toward "gap survives"); fitness gains yoga_studio and sports_club; restaurant gains the Table A cuisine family under primary filtering (39 types, under the 50 cap, pinned by test); bar drops bar_and_grill; clinic removed (no Table A type isolates 621493; GTM-107 is the only path). tailor_repair keeps `tailor` but is marked not usable for headline claims (Table A has no shoe_repair). Drift test added between GOOGLE_TYPES and the JS map (JS was missing preschool, medical_lab, fitness_center). `loci validate` skips unmapped categories with a notice. Ledger stands at 2,978/3,000 calls; a corrected 15-category re-run at 990 hexes is ~14,850 calls (~$315) and needs a fresh budget before GTM-48 can run. D29's fitness "21% true hole" is unreliable in both directions until then.

**D51 — Trust factor dropped from the grade; all of Brooklyn in scope; in-building laundry a supply input.** *(2026-09-08, owner)* → archive

**D52 — Supply-set principle adopted; stale licences ruled out; booth-renter dedup rule; alcohol is a map overlay, not a category; pipeline re-run approved.** *(2026-09-08, owner)*
Findings (data-engineer, session 13, MN+BK, ZBP 2023): the ACTIVE filter is a no-op — NYS DOS has 0 expired licences statewide (min expiry = today), NYS SLA 0 expired, and a DOHMH "not inspected in 24 months" rule removes 0.4% of canonical POIs; this closes D47(b): licence staleness does not explain the overcount for nails/hair. CORROBORATED (≥2 sources) brings restaurant 3.62→1.13×, cafe 3.73→1.20×, hardware 3.72→0.99×, tailor 3.88→1.12× ZBP, but collapses convenience to 0.16×, childcare 0.15×, clinic 0.10× because their anchors are not loaded — corroboration measures feed overlap, which is thickest in dense/rich areas, so applied globally it manufactures gaps. Dedup radius is not the cause (40→100 m changes counts <1%); the causes are aggregator-only rows (≈30% of canonical bar and fitness are Foursquare-only) and booth renters (996 DOS nails licences, 20%, at a shared ~11 m coordinate under different names).
Rulings: (a) the supply set is chosen per category by a principle, not a table: where a category has a registry anchor loaded whose own coverage is ≥0.7 of ZBP, a lone aggregator record (cluster whose only member is Overture or Foursquare) is not evidence of a business and is excluded; where no qualifying anchor is loaded, all canonical records stay and loading the anchor is the fix. The 0.7 threshold is a module constant with its reasoning in the docstring. Categories currently without a qualifying anchor: laundry (DCWP wired but empty), bank (FDIC planned), pharmacy (NYS planned), childcare (DOHMH planned), clinic, fitness, hardware, tailor, convenience. (b) Booth-renter rule: same source (NYS DOS), same category, ≤15 m, name-blind collapse; nails −12.5%, hair −0.9%; explains ≈1/8 of the nails overcount; caveat that it also collapses genuine stacked storefronts in multi-tenant retail. (c) Alcohol: the NYS SLA feed already covers every licence that authorizes selling alcohol; it becomes a map OVERLAY (on-premises / off-premises liquor / off-premises beer, toggle + legend, MN+BK), not a 16th category. (d) Pipeline re-run approved after (a) and (b) are in code: dedup → distances → address-gaps MN+BK → export-webmap, with supply_set and supply_hash recorded in provenance; before/after reported as description per category, never as a top-N list.
Why: two defensible supply sets differed 6× and rank-correlated at 0.19; the principle applies corroboration only where corroboration means something, and it turns each unanchored category into a concrete plumbing task rather than a judgment call.

**D53 — The Google coverage validator's radius is now the gap screen's 800 m NETWORK threshold divided by NYC's MEASURED circuity (1.233), i.e. 649 m straight-line; the radius is config, is derived in code, and is recorded on every row.** *(2026-09-08, QUESTIONS M8, GTM-105 finding D)*

Why: (Ids D53/D54 are allocated because the concurrent ENY session took D51/D52 the same day; the two entries below are this session's.) M8 offered two fixes for the D29 geometry artifact. Option (a) — reuse analysis.hex_poi_distance at validation time — was rejected as not implementable and actively harmful: Google Nearby Search accepts only a circular locationRestriction, so (a) could only change loci's side, comparing a network-shaped set against a disc of the same nominal size and guaranteeing a one-sided loci undercount. Re-measuring Google's returns on the walk network is the only coherent version and cannot reuse hex_poi_distance (which holds loci's own POIs only); it would mean routing to every returned place. Option (b) keeps BOTH sides a disc and chooses the disc that best approximates the network-800 set. Circuity was MEASURED on loci's own data rather than taken from the literature: read-only over analysis.hex_poi_distance (16.2M pairs) joined to hex centroids and staging.poi geometry, the 949,334 pairs whose network_m falls in the 700–900 m band give median network_m/straight_m = 1.2332 (IQR 1.122–1.348); 1.2341 restricted to populated hexes. By borough: Brooklyn 1.226, Manhattan 1.232, Queens 1.233, Bronx 1.248, Staten Island 1.279 — a single citywide constant is used because the four dense boroughs sit within 1%. Cross-check: median straight-line distance among pairs at network 780–820 m is 648.8 m = 800/1.2332 to the metre. The value is at the low end of the literature's 1.25–1.30 for gridded US cities, as a dense grid should be. 800/1.233 = 649 m. Radius sweep against the network-800 set (recall / precision): 600 m 0.825/0.928, 640 m 0.893/0.881, 660 m 0.919/0.853, 800 m 0.992/0.629 — 649 m is the balance point with ~12% symmetric error, while the pre-D53 800 m disc put 37% of what the validator counted beyond the reach it was validating, one-sided toward "loci looks under-covered." Second benefit (D50 finding D): the disc's area falls to 66% of the 800 m disc, reducing the 20-result cap censoring. Implementation: src/loci/reach_tiers.yaml gains a `validation:` block (network_threshold_m 800, circuity 1.233, derived_radius_m 649, measured_on, circuity_basis, provenance and the sweep); loci.reach.load_validation_geometry/validation_radius_m derive radius = round(threshold/circuity) and refuse a circuity below 1.0 or a pinned derived_radius_m that contradicts the derivation; validation/google_places.py, validation/sample.py, plan(), and webmap/server.js (which reads derived_radius_m from the same yaml) all resolve to that one value. analysis.coverage_validation gains a nullable radius_m column: NULL means a pre-D53 row measured at 800 m, and NULL and non-NULL rows must never be pooled. sample.recount_local re-measures each row at its own COALESCE(radius_m, 800) — recounting an 800 m row at 649 m would have manufactured an undercount. tests/test_validation_radius.py (10 tests) pins the derivation and fails on any yaml/code drift across all call sites including server.js. No Google calls spent; ledger still 2,978/3,000. Existing coverage_validation rows are 800 m rows and stay usable only against each other; the corrected re-run (D50, ~14,850 calls) will be the first at 649 m.

**D54 — ACS vehicle-ownership ingest lands (B08201 primary, B25044 tenure-split cross-check); renter_share finally gets its MOE. The D7 renter_share pre-test was negative.** *(2026-09-08, CHECKPOINT next action 10, QUESTIONS H-L7)*

Why: next action 10 required testing renter_share before the vehicle ingest. The test (read-only, MN+BK, hexes with population ≥ 100, n = 1,875): renter_share's partial Spearman with reach outcomes net of population density is 0.13 (POI/km²), 0.07 (present_count), −0.07 (median nearest m) and −0.02 (max_ratio, p = 0.37); it adds under 2 points of R² over log density; in Manhattan the sign flips (renter-heavy hexes walk slightly farther at fixed density). Renter_share proxies density (ρ = 0.62 citywide) but not mode (ρ = 0.09 within Manhattan), and its ACS MOE (17% of estimate; hex median 0.072) leaves 30% of MN+BK hexes indeterminate at a 0.70 cut. H-L7's owner-occupancy finding replicates citywide and dissolves under a density control. So the ingest is not optional. src/loci/grid/acs.py::build_acs now pulls B08201_001E/M and _002E/M → `zero_vehicle_hh_share[_moe]`; B25044_002/003/009/010 E/M → `zero_vehicle_owner_share[_moe]` / `zero_vehicle_renter_share[_moe]`; and B25003_001M/_003M → `renter_share_moe`. All cells verified against the 2023 ACS variables.json, plus a runtime cross-table check (B25044 owner+renter totals vs B25003 total; raises above 2% aggregate discrepancy). Propagation: extensive quantities apportioned tract→hex by PLUTO unit share exactly as population/households already were; MOEs use root-sum-of-squares for apportioned sums, then the ACS-handbook proportion-MOE formula (`_moe_proportion`, with the documented negative-subtraction fallback). Raw response cached at data/raw/acs/tracts_2023.json (one call per vintage). Schema: 7 new nullable columns via ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS; registry.yaml mirrors the variables. Rebuild: 5,978 hexes, row count unchanged. Headline: citywide median zero_vehicle_hh_share 0.321 (MOE 27.8% of estimate), MN+BK median 0.585 (MOE 14.2%, n = 1,960). Within MN+BK, pop ≥ 100: Spearman(zero_vehicle_hh_share, pop density) = 0.519, Spearman(zero_vehicle_hh_share, renter_share) = 0.712 — it correlates MORE with renter_share than with density, so whether it is a genuinely distinct mode signal is next session's question; no threshold or class was built. Caveat the database cannot enforce: proportion MOEs on hexes with a near-zero apportioned housing-unit denominator can exceed 1 (one hex at households ≈ 1.8 shows renter_share_moe 5.3); any consumer sorting or filtering on these MOE columns must also gate on a minimum apportioned denominator. Tests: tests/test_acs.py 8 (6 new).

**D55 — Plan to add the missing daily-needs categories; anchor laundry first via DCWP; LL84 and listings become laundry inputs.** *(2026-09-08, owner)*
Owner: "lets plan to add in all of those missing categories. for now lets anchor laundry." Candidates (from the session-13 gap list): liquor & wine stores, dry cleaners (if not inside laundry), dentists & eye care, veterinarians & pet supply, shipping & mail, specialty food (butcher/fish/produce/non-cafe bakery), dollar & variety stores, florists/bookstores/phone repair, auto repair & gas (likely out of scope for a dense walking screen). A planning agent drafts per-category NAICS, anchor source, aggregator vocab, reach basis, demand class, ZBP mapping and risk, with an ordering and a drop list, and adds tickets under a new milestone in src/loci/tickets.py; nothing is pushed to Linear until the owner reviews the plan. Laundry is anchored first: the DCWP "Legally Operating Businesses" adapter exists but its ingest was empty; it is being fixed to emit laundromats into a pending table, merged into staging.poi after the D52 pipeline run finishes, then a laundry-scoped re-run. Laundry supply inputs now on disk: LL84/LL133 benchmarking laundry hookups (staging.ll84_laundry, analysis.address_laundry; 30,966 BBLs; clears 710 of 43,726 MN+BK laundry leads = 1.6%, because 92% of laundry leads are <6-unit buildings which LL84's 25,000 sq ft threshold never covers; field names resolved by display name because Socrata transposes the in-unit/common-area columns across vintages), and advertised laundry from StreetEasy listing pages via Tavily (registry `streeteasy_listings`, verified; pilot in progress). NYCHVS 2023 says 74% of 1-unit and 50% of 2-unit MN+BK dwellings have an in-unit washer: for small buildings in-building laundry can only ever be a rate, and it belongs in the grade as a demand haircut, not as supply.
Why: the laundry category's dominance is substantially small buildings being counted as laundromat demand; anchoring supply and adding the demand rate are the two logic fixes, and neither is a filter.

**D56 — `analysis.address_demographics` lands: address-grain ACS income, tenure and vehicle ownership, taken directly from each PLUTO lot's 2020 census tract via a BBL lookup — no spatial join, no apportionment. It replaces `analysis.hex_demographics` as the demographic carrier under D38; hex_demographics is frozen history.** *(2026-09-09, owner: "do it, no more hex work")*

Why: the owner's 2026-09-09 correction — "why do we keep talking about hexes?" — was that D49/D53/D54 had all landed on hex tables the address screen never reads, so none of it reached anything the owner looks at. The missing piece was an address-level demographic carrier. An address sits in exactly one tract, so the tract's ACS values and their MOEs are taken as-is: no apportionment, no interpolation, the least-modelled input possible. Tract assignment is PLUTO's own `bct2020` field on the lot (the same field `grid/acs.py` already trusted for the hex weights), so address → tract is a BBL lookup against the raw PLUTO extract, not a point-in-polygon join; no TIGER download. Columns: tract GEOID, median_hh_income + moe (B19013), renter_share + moe (B25003, ACS-handbook proportion MOE via `_moe_proportion`), zero_vehicle_hh_share + moe (B08201), zero_vehicle_owner_share / zero_vehicle_renter_share + moe (B25044), population and households (B01003/B11001) for reference, acs_year. Built from the existing raw ACS cache (no new Census call). Results: 767,337 rows, 767,331 with a tract (99.9992%; the 6 without get NULLs, never dropped); every address sharing a tract carries an identical income (0 tracts with more than one distinct value — proves lookup, not apportionment); 2,225 distinct tracts. Citywide median household income $91,389, MN+BK $83,083; median zero-vehicle share 31.2% citywide, 52.4% MN+BK; median income MOE 25.7% of estimate; 53.0% of addresses sit within one MOE of the D49 threshold ($102,315). Caveat the database cannot enforce: population/households are the tract's FULL count repeated on every address in it — summing them across addresses is an N-times overcount; rates are safe per address or as medians. Implementation: src/loci/model/address_demographics.py, DDL in 002_schema.sql, `loci address-demographics [--dry-run]`; tests/test_address_demographics.py 6 tests. Note: the concurrent session's migration 008 separately put a hex-interpolated median_hh_income onto address_gaps the same day; the annotation in D57 deliberately reads the tract-direct value here instead.

**D57 — GTM-110 done: the D49 demand annotation now exists ONLY at the address grain, as a sibling table that cannot filter. The hex_gaps annotation is frozen history.** *(2026-09-09, GTM-110)*

Why: D49 built the continuous, MOE-gated demand annotation on model/gaps.py's hex screen and left "not yet ported to addresses" as its own open item; D38 had already made the residential address the unit of analysis, so the annotation was living on the one geography the project no longer uses. The port is a NEW table, analysis.address_demand, not a column set on analysis.address_gaps — D48 says the output is graded, never filtered, and a sibling table keyed by address_id makes that mechanical rather than conventional: model/address_demand.py opens address_gaps read-only and has no write path to it, so nothing it computes can move gap_score, lead_category, n_missing or membership in the missing set. Verified on the real run: address_gaps' row count (767,337; 281,842 MN+BK) and hash-sum checksums of gap_score, lead_category, n_missing and eligible are byte-identical before and after the build. Grain: one row per (address_id, category, run key), the run key being (reach_hash, supply_hash) carried from the address_gaps run being annotated — the two inputs that decide what "missing" and "nearest" mean (D41 reach table, D52 supply set), so an annotation row detached from them is uninterpretable; the delete-then-insert is on the run key, not the borough. Rows are emitted for every category whose ratio > 1 (the continuous D39/D41 reading of "missing") plus the address's lead_category always; lead rows are the only ones that can carry ratio <= 1. Columns mirror D49 exactly: demand_class (derived from spend.yaml's BLS CEX elasticity at demand.yaml's 0.35 cut, never hand-coded), income_ratio, income_ratio_moe, income_indeterminate, demand_caveat, demand_caveat_text, acs_year, run_at. The caveat fires only on the MOE-confident test (income_ratio + income_ratio_moe < 0.80) AND a discretionary, annotatable category; unknown MOE means no assertion, and clinic can never be caveated (D30). The numerator is the address's TRACT median household income from analysis.address_demographics (D56), deliberately NOT the hex-interpolated median_hh_income that migration 008 added to address_gaps the same day. MN+BK run (reach_hash f6339bf9d842 tiers, supply_hash c4b847b82ffd principled): 627,439 rows over 281,842 addresses; 107,009 caveated (17.1%); 286,159 income-indeterminate (45.6% of rows — the same "more than a coin flip" fact that retired the binary badge in D49, reproduced at address grain); caveated rows by category bar 65,874, cafe_bakery 33,440, restaurant 6,955, fitness 740 (the entire caveat-eligible set); 48,888 of 267,114 eligible addresses (18.3%) have a caveated LEAD category. Rows with ratio > 1 among eligible addresses total 421,218, exactly sum(n_missing) in address_gaps — the missing set is reproduced, not reinterpreted. 3,740 rows have no income and 6,257 no MOE; all fail closed. Consequence: model/gaps.py and the hex_gaps/hex_gaps_reach demand columns are FROZEN HISTORY; gaps.py keeps its private _ratio_moe and _caveat_text rather than being rewired (D38: the hex path takes no edits); loci.demand.ratio_moe is the shared home of the formula and tests pin the two implementations and the two caveat wordings against each other. Renderers must not truncate demand_caveat_text — the X6 disclaimer is its tail. NOT yet rendered: viz/webmap_export.py does not read address_demand; the remaining step is one join on (address_id, reach_hash, supply_hash), filtered to is_lead. CLI: `loci address-demand [--borough MNBK|MN|BK|ALL] [--dry-run]`, default MN+BK per D48. Tests: tests/test_address_demand.py 12; suite 297 passing.

**D58 — The Google coverage validator's sampling frame is gap ADDRESSES, not hex centroids; the 2,970 existing coverage_validation rows are the frozen pre-D38 hex frame and must never be pooled with address rows.** *(2026-09-09, GTM-48)*

Why: D38 made the address the unit of analysis and froze the hex tables, but validation/sample.py still drew hex centroids stratified by income decile from analysis.hex_demographics — so GTM-48 would have spent ~$300 of Google budget validating coverage at points no lead refers to (a hex centroid can sit in a park, a rail yard or mid-block), against a screen that no longer speaks in hexes. draw_sample now draws rows of analysis.address_gaps, restricted to MN+BK (D48) and to eligible lots, stratified income decile × missing-this-category. The income decile is NTILE(10) over analysis.address_demographics.median_hh_income — the tract's own ACS median looked up directly on the lot's BBL (D56), no apportionment. The missing/present split is the point: measuring Google's enumeration only where loci says a category is missing cannot separate coverage bias from a real hole (D29 / CONTEXT 7.1 P3), so the present half of each stratum is the control. Both sides of every row are measured at the lot's own lon/lat at the D53 649 m radius, so the Google count and the local canonical count are like-for-like by construction. The sample is address-weighted by default (one lot, one draw); `--unit-weighted` draws proportional to units_capped (Efraimidis–Spirakis, without replacement) for reading the sample as a statement about households behind a gap. analysis.coverage_validation gains nullable address_id and borough; h3_index stays and is NULL on address rows. The hex-era PRIMARY KEY (h3_index, category) had to be removed — a DuckDB PK column is implicitly NOT NULL and there is no ALTER TABLE DROP CONSTRAINT, so the table physically could not hold an address row; sample.ensure_address_frame() migrates an existing database (rename → re-apply 002_schema.sql → copy rows back by column name; guarded, no-op once migrated, never fires on --dry-run) and run() maintains uniqueness itself by deleting the row's own key before inserting. The 2,970 rows written 2026-09-02/03 are the pre-D38 hex frame, all at the legacy 800 m disc (radius_m NULL): different unit, different geometry, different radius. They stay for the D29 hardware/fitness/clinic split and are usable only against each other — every statistic must filter on one frame (h3_index IS NOT NULL or address_id IS NOT NULL), never pool. Code only: no Google call made, ledger still 2,978/3,000. Plan on the current data (20/stratum, 14 mapped categories, clinic skipped): 5,185 addresses over 265 strata = 5,277 calls at 649 m. Two facts the plan surfaced that GTM-48's budget request must carry: grocery has ZERO missing addresses in the eligible MN+BK frame (200 control calls buying no gap observations — drop it or accept it as a pure control), and fitness/pharmacy/nails_beauty/hardware have income deciles with no missing address at all, so their missing arm is thinner than 10 strata. Also: plan()'s est_cost_usd treats the 5,000 free calls as untouched; 2,978 are already spent, so the true incremental cost of a 5,277-call run is nearer $170 than the printed $8.86 — budget off the ledger, not that number. CLI: `loci validate --per-stratum N --boroughs MN,BK [--unit-weighted] [--seed S] [--dry-run]`. Tests: tests/test_sample.py 16 (11 new); suite 297.

**D59 — Supply-set principle applied and re-run; laundry anchored via DCWP; alcohol is an overlay, not a category.** *(2026-09-09, owner)* → archive

**D60 — ACS age, race/ethnicity, education and household size added, and the tract-direct address table is the ONLY address-demographics source; the hex-copied columns are gone.** *(2026-09-09, owner, GTM-129)*
Owner asked for age, race/ethnicity, education and household size. grid/acs.py now pulls 118 E+M variables (every cell verified against the live 2023 variables.json; fetched in 3 chunks of ≤48 because the Census API caps a request at 50): median age (B01002); under-18, 18–34 and 65+ shares from B01001 summed across sexes, with 35–64 deliberately left as the residual so a fourth column cannot read as independent; white, Black and Asian non-Hispanic and Hispanic-any-race shares (B03002); bachelor's-or-higher share of 25+ (B15003); average household size (B25010) and one-person-household share (B11016). MOEs travel with every field: RSS for apportioned and multi-cell sums, the handbook proportion formula for shares, the incumbent weighted mean for the two intensive fields. A B01001-vs-B01003 per-tract population cross-check (>2% aggregate raises) joins the D54 B25044/B25003 one. Population-weighted hex figures against known NYC values: white NH 31.4% (~31), Black NH 20.7% (~22), Asian NH 14.6% (~15), Hispanic 28.5% (~29), college 39.7% (~40). Why the decision matters: these landed first on hex_demographics and were then COPIED onto address_gaps by containing hex — doubly modelled (tract→hex by unit share, hex→address by containment) and a step function at hex edges — while a parallel session built D56's tract-direct analysis.address_demographics the same day. Two tables answered one question. Ruling: the tract-direct table is canonical because a lot sits in exactly one tract and there is nothing to apportion; it was extended to the same 20 columns by IMPORTING acs.py's cell lists and helpers, the 36 hex-copied columns were stripped from the gap table, and a drift test fails if any hex demographic lacks an address twin. Unit-weighted citywide from the canonical table: white NH 35.0%, Black NH 19.1%, Asian NH 14.4%, Hispanic 26.7%, college 44.6%, median age 39.0, avg household 2.47 (units, not people, are the weight, so these differ from the hex figures by construction). Also caught: `loci address-demographics` had never been re-run after the extension landed, so every new column was NULL on all 767,337 rows until this session. Caveat the DB cannot enforce: share MOEs exceed 1 on a handful of near-empty denominators; gate on population before filtering on any *_moe. A catchment-weighted variant (demographics over the walk-shed rather than the lot's tract) is logged in QUESTIONS.md, not built.

**D61 — Warehouse consolidated 34 → 26 objects: address long/wide split with address_gaps as a generated VIEW, hex screen outputs dropped, prototypes and orphans removed, laundry evidence merged; zip_coverage_check deliberately kept as a table.** *(2026-09-09, owner, GTM-129)*
Owner, on seeing 34 objects: "we need to really watch for proliferation of tables." Inventory showed the sprawl in the address layer — wide per-category tables that were pivots of an existing long table, a 200-row prototype (address_convenience) beside the table that replaced it, two orphans nothing wrote (address_gaps_prev, main.p), four hex screen outputs frozen since D38 (hex_gaps, hex_gaps_reach, hex_dnci, empty hex_outcomes), and two laundry-evidence tables with two views. Six items approved; five done, one rejected with reasons. Done: (1) one address-demographics source (D60); (2) orphans dropped; (3) hex screen outputs dropped, their writers and CLI commands retired under D38, the foundation hex tables kept because the address path still reads them for transit, jobs and reach; (4) `loci conveniences` is a read-only query over the long table — no second Dijkstra pass, nothing persisted; (5) the principled shape: analysis.address (one row per address: identity, summary, provenance stamped ONCE) + analysis.address_category (767,337 × 15 = 11,510,055 rows: nearest_m, ratio, and the D57 demand-annotation columns, which address_demand.py now writes by UPDATE only, with a pinned test asserting its SET-list is disjoint from the screen's own columns) and analysis.address_gaps as a VIEW generated from categories.yaml carrying every old column including the six provenance ones, so webmap_export, sql/004–006 and the supply-set tests work unchanged; (6a) address_laundry_evidence(bbl, source) replaces the two laundry tables and two views with one of each. REJECTED (6b): turning zip_coverage_check into a view over zip_coverage_by_source would silently drop 12 of 2,034 rows — the poi_count=0 "Census sees establishments, Loci found none" rows, the table's strongest undercoverage signal — because the by-source table inner-joins onto canonical POIs; reasoning is in the 002_schema.sql header. The bug the work surfaced: address_gaps_view_sql() had been designed but never wired into db.init_schema(), so a fresh test DB crashed at 004 with "address_gaps does not exist" (58 failed / 20 errors, reported by a parallel session and reproduced exactly); fixed by creating the view immediately after 002, since DuckDB resolves a view's query at CREATE time. Full pipeline rebuilt for all five boroughs on the live DB after a backup; non-filtering proof exact on the rebuild (count{ratio>1, eligible} = sum(n_missing) = 1,389,900); supply_hash unchanged (e2b7ef55e607). Full suite 333 passed. Rule that now lives in code rather than table boundaries: analysis.address + address_category hold exactly ONE run; comparing supply sets means a parquet snapshot between runs, never two copies in the warehouse. Standing rule from the owner: before creating a table, run the inventory; pivots and subsets become views, new measures extend the existing grain, provenance is stamped once on the parent.

**D62 — Residential development pipeline built from DCP Housing Database + DOB certificates of occupancy: analysis.dev_pipeline at job grain, 12 pipeline-exposure columns on analysis.address by UPDATE, pipeline overlay on the webmap.** *(2026-09-09, owner)*
Owner asked (2026-09-09) "build the pipeline layer with DOB permits and CO dates", after the density-shock investigation (QUESTIONS T7) found Loci had no way to say which gap addresses are about to gain residents. Source decision: the DCP Housing Database project-level file is br6q-ssj3 (NOT hg8x-zxpr, which is HPD affordable production) — version 25Q4, data as of 2026-01-21, 109,076 jobs with unique job_number, 100% geocoded with BBL/BIN on MN+BK; completion is CO-derived by DCP; datepermit present on 454/457 MN+BK new-building jobs ≥10 units, so DOB NOW filings (w9ak-ipjd) and Permit Issuance (ipu4-2q9a) were NOT ingested. Both DOB CO feeds (bs8b-p36w BIS, pkdm-hqz6 DOB NOW) refresh daily and were joined on job_number to override DCP's stale status: 436 MN+BK jobs / 11,407 units are still "Permitted" in DCP but hold a CO issued after DCP's cutoff. Traps: pkdm-hqz6 CO date is text (MM/DD/YY h:mm:ss AM); bs8b-p36w contains a literal 2105-11-05. Inventory before building (D61 rule): 26 objects, none at job grain; ignition.py's 198k DOB filings were never persisted. So ONE new table, analysis.dev_pipeline (one row per DOB job: a genuinely new grain), 48,762 MN+BK jobs (BK complete 21,979 / permitted 5,124 / filed 5,222 / withdrawn 3,807; MN complete 6,923 / permitted 2,236 / filed 2,068 / withdrawn 1,399). Measures extend analysis.address by UPDATE only — units_permitted_400m, units_completed_24mo_400m, units_completed_60mo_400m, nearest_large_project_id/_units/_stage/_date, pipeline_asof etc. (12 columns), reusing score/access network-distance helpers; NOT address_category, because exposure is category-independent and would write 15 identical copies. Pinned test: PIPELINE_COLUMNS ∩ ADDRESS_SCREEN_COLUMNS = ∅ and ADDRESS_SCREEN_COLUMNS == address_gaps.ADDRESS_COLUMNS. Bug found and fixed: db.init_schema() builds the address_gaps view right after 002 and before 011, so on the live DB the view bound to columns that did not exist and every connection raised BinderException; the ALTERs now live at the tail of 002 (same class as the D61 bug). Live run after backup (data/loci.duckdb.bak.20260909-182444): 281,842 addresses annotated in 2m45s; non-filtering proof exact before and after (422,904). Of 177,078 eligible gap addresses, 9,591 have ≥100 units permitted within 400 m and 2,198 have ≥300; top 15 are FiDi–Battery Park City and Carroll Gardens–Gowanus, lead category laundry. Ground truth passes: Gowanus max permitted 2,102; Downtown BK–DUMBO max completed-60mo 5,099; Greenpoint 3,234; largest jobs name 111 Wall St (1,569u), 175 3rd St Gowanus (1,071u), 260 South St Two Bridges (666u), 25 Water St (1,320u complete 2025-02). Two honest misses: Two Bridges has only 5 gap addresses; Park Slope's Fourth Avenue build-out peaked 2016–21 so only its tail falls in the 60-month window. Webmap: "Development pipeline" overlay (net_units ≥ 50, stages permitted / partially complete / complete ≤60 mo; filed and withdrawn never drawn): 589 jobs (MN 210, BK 379; 108,528 homes), sized by band 50–99/100–299/300+, coloured by stage, hollow ring = temporary CO; gap popups carry the exposure; a min-units-permitted selector filters the gap layer; legend derives the as-of line from the data (max filed/permit date 2026-01-20). Data dir 42 → 50.6 MB; nearest project dictionary-encoded per file. Suite 333 → 364. CLI: loci ingest-dcp-housing, loci pipeline, loci export-webmap, all --dry-run. Caveats the DB cannot enforce: (1) DCP vintage lag — the forward pipeline is a FLOOR; (2) TCO ≠ finished building, co_type is the filter; (3) zombie permits: 1,348 jobs / 9,468 units (23% of permitted units) permitted >5 yr ago and never completed — units_permitted_400m is exposure, not a forecast; (4) withdrawn-after-permit kept in the table, excluded from measures; (5) negative net_units (demolitions, alterations) ingested, measures count only ≥1; (6) units_complete is gross, net_units net — never ratio them; (7) 24mo ⊂ 60mo, never add; (8) a job is not a masterplan (Greenpoint Landing, Pacific Park are several points); (9) no under_construction stage, deliberately; (10) `loci check-sources --urls` not yet run for br6q-ssj3. Also logged this session: the registry describes lodes_wac as annual 2002–2023 but analysis.hex_panel holds only 2002/2013/2023 — registry drift, fix the temporal line.

Fixes: (1) listings.py `_post` timeout 240→90 s and a network error is now a recorded failed call that backs off instead of crashing the run (commit 72a5f8d); (2) supervise.sh v2 kills an attempt with no progress for 15 min; (3) sweep PAUSED at 09:12 and scheduled to resume at 16:30 with `--pace 5 --cooldown 120` (resume.pid) — flushed runs show extract failure 17–26%, the stalled attempts never flushed a part so their rate is unknown. Remaining: 1,659 addresses. Merge still automatic on completion (merge.log).
- **2026-09-11 14:48: StreetEasy sweep of the ≥6-unit stratum COMPLETE and merged** (all 2,828 targets; 4,813 credits logged across every attempt ≈ $38 at $0.008; paused 04:07–10:26 after four overnight hangs; the 09:00 auto-resume did not fire and was started by hand). Landed: staging.listings 3,724 listings over 1,210 BBLs (90 active / 3,634 past; 1,812 in-building, 1,087 in-unit, 6 explicit none, 1,310 silent); analysis.address_laundry_evidence source='listing' rows for 1,210 BBLs beside 30,966 'll84' rows. Coverage: 1,192 of 2,835 eligible MN+BK ≥6-unit laundry leads (42%) now carry advertised-laundry evidence. The <6-unit strata were deliberately NOT swept (owner ruling 2026-09-09: a rate from NYCHVS, never a per-address fact). Ops lessons for any future sweep: Tavily trickles at night (≈25 s/call vs 8 s by day) and ~hourly hangs slip past the read timeout — add a hard per-call deadline; the fetch log flushes per part, so a hang before the first part re-spends up to 24 searches per relaunch. Next use: the laundry demand haircut in the grade (LL84 + listings + NYCHVS rates).

**D63 — Age informs the RANKING of bar gaps through a market-revealed New York curve, not the CEX survey: `age_fit_bar` from the SLA composition specification, non-filtering, with its own F2 gate enforced in code.** *(2026-09-10, owner, GTM-131)*
Owner, 2026-09-10, restating the request `docs/age_demand_fit.md` had refused: "a bar gap in the Upper East Side where avg age is 60+ vs a bar gap in the East Village where avg age is 25 — the East Village bar should score higher." The BLS CEX route stays rejected on its own numbers: alcohol spend is $643 per household nationally and $851 in the NY metro, but those are means over households *including non-drinkers*, alcohol is among the most under-reported lines in the survey, there is no age-by-metro table so the metro level and the age shape cannot be crossed, and the household shape peaks at reference-person 45–54 — the budget of a household, not the behaviour of a drinker; the owner's own gloss, "I spend $100 for 1 date", is exactly the distance between the two, and the resulting multiplier had a p10–p90 spread of 0.013 against a median MOE of 0.140 (dispersion 0.10) and scored the UES *above* the East Village. The replacement is measured on this warehouse: the median East Village address has 197 on-premises SLA licences, 55 bar-type licences and 87 canonical bar POIs within 400 m against Carnegie Hill's 49, 11 and 14 — 4.0×, 5.0× and 6.2× — so the market has already answered the question at 6× on the category-specific measures. Of the specifications `docs/bar_age_nyc.md` tested, exactly one was shipped: the COMPOSITION spec, outcome log(1+bar-type) − log(1+on-premises) within 400 m of the tract's residential centroid, on adult 18–34 and 65+ shares plus log units, log(1+CNS07 retail jobs), log walk-to-subway, log median income, renter share and borough FE, Conley 2 km spatial-HAC SEs over 1,065 MN+BK tracts; it reproduces at b(w18) = +0.404 (Conley se 0.203, t +1.99) and b(w65) = −0.484 (t −1.74), Carnegie Hill → East Village 1.298 [1.123, 1.500]. It was chosen because it is the ONLY specification whose two age coefficients both carry the owner's sign — every count specification has w65 *positive*, because the UES is old, rich and dense — and the only one significant in Brooklyn (b18 +0.596, b65 −0.662, t +2.44, ratio 1.451 [1.196, 1.761]), where 98.2% of the bar-lead gap set actually lives; the bar-POI count's Brooklyn CI grazes 1 at [1.011, 2.178] and Manhattan's composition effect is null at 1.124 [0.950, 1.331], so pooling alone would have calibrated where the signal is and shipped where it isn't. That criterion (F2) is now enforced in code rather than documented: `loci age-fit fit` recomputes the Brooklyn-only Conley CI and the dispersion gate and exits non-zero writing nothing if the CI includes 1.0 or the spread fails to clear its own median MOE — currently 3.61× (spread 0.200 against 0.055), a 36× improvement on the test that retired the predecessor. The caveat ships with the column and must render untruncated: it is SUPPLY-REVEALED (fitted on where bars already are, so demand and residential sorting are not separated), a low value is never evidence a neighbourhood does not want the service, and resident age is a proxy for a bundle — spatial-block CV says age adds +3.2–3.6% out of sample over a density-only model in Brooklyn but ≈0% once income, renter share, walk-to-subway and retail jobs are measured directly, and it transfers *negatively* (−1.1 to −1.7%) from Brooklyn to Manhattan. Stated honestly, the effect on the output: over the 67,223 bar-lead gap addresses in 82 clusters the multiplier runs p10/p50/p90 = 0.899/0.962/1.038, cluster Spearman 0.955 and address Spearman 0.983, and the top-50 cluster list changes by four clusters (Jaccard 0.923, two in and two out, max rank shift 20) — enough to answer the owner's question, little enough that gap_score still dominates, spread 1.66 against 0.20. Two data findings logged on the way: `analysis.hex_controls.subway_riders_2024` was NULL on all 8,321 rows for the plain reason that nothing ever wrote it — the column landed ahead of its loader and the registry entry was still `planned` — now fixed by `grid/mta.build_subway_ridership` chunking the ~110M-row hourly feed by calendar month because a whole-year server-side aggregation times out (8,321 hexes, 424 complexes; Staten Island correctly NULL, its nearest station being SIR); and `analysis.hex_panel` is not total LODES employment but exactly two NAICS sectors, CNS07 retail (303,835 jobs, 2023) and CNS18 accommodation-and-food-services (332,020), of which CNS18 must NEVER be a control for bars because food-service payroll *is* the outcome in employee form. Built: `age_fit`/`age_fit_moe`/`age_fit_source` on `analysis.address_category` (bar rows only; NULL, never 1.0, elsewhere — "no curve" is not "a neutral curve") and `age_fit_lead`/`age_fit_lead_moe`/`gap_score_fit` on `analysis.address`, all by UPDATE with both SET lists asserted disjoint from the screen's own columns before either statement runs; the generated `address_gaps` view exposes the three address-grain columns, appended; `loci age-fit fit` and `loci age-fit apply`, both with `--dry-run`; 20 tests in `tests/test_age_fit.py` (suite 364 → 384) covering the non-filtering proof (re-verified on the live DB: Σgap_score 938,319.24, Σn_missing 1,389,900, ratio>1 count 3,044,584 all unchanged), the disjointness pin, the NULL-vs-1.0 pin, the F2/F3 refusal-to-write and its non-zero exit code, and East Village 1.117 ± 0.090 > Carnegie Hill 0.834 ± 0.083 on the live curve.

**D64 — `age_fit` extends to a REGISTRY of per-category curves, and childcare's curve is refused by the same gate that admitted bar: the composition spec fails F2 in Brooklyn at 0.767 [0.545, 1.078], so childcare does NOT ship.** *(2026-09-10, owner, GTM-134)*
Owner extended age_fit to childcare per QUESTIONS D15, on the reasoning that the supply-revealed placebo independently reproduced the CEX note's two passing categories with the same signs from a different source — the strongest available evidence that b(w18) is category-specific rather than generic urbanity — with the F2/F3 gate applied without relaxation, because two sources agreeing on a sign is corroboration and not a licence to skip the test. The spec mirrors bar deliberately: composition rather than count, `log(1 + canonical childcare POIs within 640 m) − log(1 + ALL canonical POIs within 640 m)` off the tract's residential centroid at the category's own reach tier, so that "this is a dense commercial strip" cancels out of numerator and denominator mechanically the way on-premises licences cancelled it for bar, with `under_18_share` as the primary demand regressor (children, not the age of adults) and bar's two adult shares retained so the placebo b(w18) stays comparable; controls, Conley 2 km SEs and the 1,065-tract MN+BK sample are identical to bar's. It failed. On 1,065 tracts (R² 0.551) b(under_18_share) is −0.205 pooled (Conley se 0.388, t −0.53) and −0.769 in Brooklyn (se 0.393, t −1.96), giving a Brooklyn contrast of 0.767 [0.545, 1.078] on East Village → Borough Park — the interval includes 1.0 and the point estimate is on the wrong side of it — while Manhattan runs the opposite way at +2.199 (t +2.98, ratio 2.586 [1.505, 4.446]), so the two boroughs are on opposite mechanisms even more sharply than bar; F3 passed comfortably at 1.49× (spread 0.117 against median MOE 0.078), which makes F2 the sole binding failure, exactly as the note predicted it would be. The contrast endpoints were derived from the panel rather than chosen — 79 MN+BK NTAs carry ≥500 addresses, and the extremes on unit-weighted under-18 share are East Village 0.069 (1,927 addresses) and Borough Park 0.464 (7,434) — and the count specification, fitted as a reported robustness check, is significantly wrong-signed at −1.862 in Brooklyn (t −4.52, ratio 0.542 [0.396, 0.741]), a CI that excludes 1.0 by rejecting the hypothesis rather than confirming it; F2 now tests the SIGN as well as the interval so that trap is closed in code, and nothing was switched. Two readings of the negative sign both forbid shipping: Borough Park's median address sits 375 m from the nearest canonical childcare against East Village's 205 m despite nine times the child share, which is either aggregator under-coverage of Hasidic home-based and yeshiva daycare — M1 wearing a costume, and childcare has NO registry anchor at all (`category_anchor` coverage 0.000; all 4,302 POIs are Overture plus Foursquare, so `in_principled` degrades to `in_all` and the outcome is built from the same supply the screen reads) — or a real desert, in which case the multiplier would rank the neighbourhoods with the most children DOWN, the D49/X6 hazard verbatim. Even had it passed it would have moved nothing: the 10,222 childcare-lead gap addresses sit in only 11 clusters, the multiplier runs p10/p50/p90 = 0.920/0.989/1.062 against a median MOE of 0.086, and cluster Spearman is 1.000. The under-5 robustness regressor, computed at tract level from B01001_003E + _027E in the raw ACS cache, enters at +0.783 and drives under_18_share more negative (Brooklyn −0.975, ratio 0.708 [0.513, 0.978]) — under-5s raise childcare composition, 5–17s lower it, net under-18 negative — so `under_5_share` and its MOE should be added to `analysis.address_demographics` in a future ACS pass, deliberately not in this one. Bar is untouched and proved so twice: the refit after the registry refactor reproduces every committed coefficient and the inputs hash `5b1374406fea` exactly, and applying both curves leaves all 281,839 bar rows bit-identical (digest `5bec24a6ed2c8d14`), with Σgap_score 938,319.24, Σn_missing 1,389,900 and 3,044,584 ratio>1 pairs unchanged; pharmacy remains open. Built: `CURVES`/`CategorySpec`/`ContrastSpec` in `model/age_fit.py` with radii read from `reach_tiers.yaml`, a k-term multiplier and delta-method MOE that raises rather than defaulting when a regressor has no ACS margin, per-category stale hashes and fit files, `loci age-fit fit|apply --category {bar,childcare,all}` with a reset pass scoped to what was requested so a gate-failed category is NULLed rather than left stale, and 12 new tests (suite 384 → 396, one deliberate skip: the childcare ordering test skips because no curve exists, so a future re-fit cannot ship without clearing it). Prerequisite to re-test childcare: load DOHMH child-care-centre inspections (dsg6-ifza) as the category's anchor.

**D65 — The childcare anchor lands and overturns D64: Borough Park was aggregator under-coverage, not a desert; the re-fitted childcare gate passes; under_5_share added; two rulings pending (D52 floor-anchor veto, screen re-run).** *(2026-09-10, GTM-135)*

The dataset D64's next action named, `dsg6-ifza`, turned out to be titled "(Historical)" with a portal note saying it reflects data as of 2019-05-14, a max inspection date of 2023-04-24 and no coordinates at all — the D55 trap again — so the source was repointed at the live DOHMH roster `gy3q-4tzp` ("Active NYC Health Code Regulated Child Care Programs", rowsUpdatedAt 2026-09-09): 2,755 programs, `dcid` 1:1 with rows, 99.56% geocoded by the publisher, ACTIVE applied at source because preliminary, suspended and closed programs are excluded from the file, so no D36/D47 staleness proxy is needed or even possible. It covers group settings only — Article 47 GCC and Article 43 school-based, the latter kept deliberately because it carries the yeshiva and parochial pre-K — while home-based Family and Group Family Day Care is licensed by NYS OCFS and appears in no NYC feed, which makes this anchor a FLOOR on childcare supply and gives the asymmetry that matters: a gap it closes was under-coverage, a gap that survives it is still not proof of a desert. Canonical childcare went 4,302 → 6,066 (1,035 of the 2,743 anchor centres sat within 40 m of an existing POI, 1,708 were new) and `anchor_coverage` went 0.000 → 0.85 vs ZBP, so childcare now qualifies; a required fix on the way was `score/dedup.py::source_rank`, which had no childcare branch, so the registry source had fallen to the tail rank behind Overture and an aggregator's geometry would have stayed canonical for every anchored cluster. Borough Park's median address moved from 298 m to 104 m from the nearest childcare with 3 → 31 sites inside 640 m, against East Village's 144 m → 140 m and 14 → 18: the 375 m D64 reported was the aggregators missing 151 Borough Park programmes across 97 buildings, and the re-fitted gate now PASSES with b(under_18_share) flipping from −0.769 to +3.299 in Brooklyn (pooled +3.753, Conley t +7.23), a contrast of 3.556 [2.416, 5.235] and F3 dispersion 3.33× — Manhattan and Brooklyn now agree instead of running opposite.
