The childcare curve is fitted but NOT applied, pending the owner. `under_5_share` (+MOE) was added to both demographic tables from cells already in GETVARS, so no ACS refresh was needed; Borough Park is 0.1438 against East Village 0.0253 on a citywide unit-weighted 0.0570, it enters the childcare fit at BK ratio 2.832 as a reported robustness check, and it is a strict subset of under_18_share, so the four age shares do not partition and must never be summed (test pinned). Two things need a ruling before the screen is re-run: because childcare now qualifies, D52's veto switched on and `in_principled` fell 4,302 → 2,657, which deletes lone-aggregator records with no registry able to corroborate them in exactly the neighbourhoods where OCFS home-based care dominates — `score/supply.py`'s own rule says a category landing in 0.5–0.9 (childcare is at 0.85, the first ever in that band) is the signal to load a better anchor, not to move the threshold, but no better anchor exists in any feed, so the proposal is a per-category `anchor_is_floor` flag under which lone-aggregator records are retained; and address-gaps / address-demand were deliberately NOT re-run (they would clear the age_fit columns), so the screen still reads the pre-anchor supply — a re-run would cut Brooklyn eligible addresses beyond 640 m of childcare from 2,237 to 1,605 (−28%). Suite 443 passed / 1 deliberate skip.

**D66 — Pharmacy clears the F2/F3 gate on the 18–34 coefficient rather than its own primary regressor; applied under the gate as written, with a ruling requested to tighten F2 to the primary regressor; age-fit provenance re-keyed to the live supply hash.** *(2026-09-10, owner, GTM-136)*

Tested under the D64 gate with no relaxation: the same composition shape at pharmacy's own 800 m reach tier, outcome log(1 + canonical pharmacy POIs within 800 m) − log(1 + ALL canonical POIs), `age_65_plus_share` as the primary demand regressor with `age_18_34_share` beside it — both raw bands, because a renormalized twin of the same variable in one spec is a collinearity trap and a test now refuses it — on bar's identical controls, 1,065 MN+BK tracts and Conley 2 km SEs. It clears F2 in Brooklyn at 1.585 [1.236, 2.032] on a contrast the panel derived (79 NTAs with ≥500 addresses; Williamsburg 0.059 unit-weighted 65+ → UES–Carnegie Hill 0.295) and clears F3 at 3.01× (spread 0.264 against median MOE 0.088), R² 0.436 — but b(age_65_plus_share) is −0.300 pooled (Conley se 0.319, t −0.94), +0.264 in Brooklyn (t +0.90) and −0.424 in Manhattan (t −1.40), insignificant everywhere and wrong-signed in two of three, while b(age_18_34_share) is −1.128 pooled (t −3.71) and −1.234 in Brooklyn (t −3.02), so roughly five-sixths of the Brooklyn log-contrast is "fewer 18–34s" and one-sixth is "more 65+". That is a real, category-specific finding — pharmacy is the exact mirror of bar on the same tracts and controls, the §5c placebo ordering reproduced inside the shipped registry — but it is NOT evidence that pharmacy demand rises with age, and a column that passes on a variable other than its stated demand driver is the GTM-109 defect: a number wearing a label it did not earn. The count spec agrees rather than conflicting (Brooklyn 1.687 [1.329, 2.142]), so unlike childcare nothing was switched; it was applied to the live DB under the gate as written, where it changes almost nothing — 1,527 pharmacy-lead gap addresses in 2 clusters, multiplier p10/p50/p90 = 0.935/1.030/1.211, top-50 cluster Jaccard 1.000 — and F4 clears (Williamsburg 0.868 ± 0.091 vs Carnegie Hill 1.115 ± 0.133) with bar bit-identical at digest `5bec24a6ed2c8d14` and Σgap_score 938,319.24, Σn_missing 1,389,900, 3,044,584 ratio>1 pairs unchanged. RULING REQUESTED, proposed by the session: tighten F2 so the primary demand regressor's own Brooklyn Conley CI must exclude zero with the stated sign — bar (w18 t +2.44 in Brooklyn) and the re-tested childcare (under_18 t +7.23) survive, pharmacy does not and would be un-applied with these numbers recorded. Pharmacy has no registry anchor (anchor_coverage 0.000 vs 1,330 ZBP; 3,540 of 5,536 canonical POIs single-source, 63.9%), and the NYS Board of Pharmacy registry remains the planned fix. Found and fixed on the way: `model/age_fit.py` read its `supply_hash` off `analysis.address`'s screen stamp instead of recomputing it, so after the D65 anchor rebuild a fit file recorded a supply (e2b7ef55e607) it was not estimated on (live f1b12ab55878) and the next address-gaps run would have tripped a FALSE staleness alarm on bar; the fit now records `score.supply.supply_hash(con)` live with the screen stamp kept as an informational `screen_supply_hash`, `check_fit_is_current` compares against the live hash, three tests pin it, and all three fit files were re-stamped with identical evidence (bar coefficients reproduce to full precision; n_target 2,299 / 5,536 / 2,657 unchanged). Built: `PHARMACY_SPEC` in `CURVES`, raw ACS bands in `AGE_MOE_COLUMNS` and the tract panel, an `adult_shares` robustness refit so b(w18) is placebo-comparable, CLI `--category` help derived from the registry, 7 pharmacy tests + 3 provenance tests; suite 443 passed / 1 deliberate skip.

**D67 — NYC DOF Storefront Registry ingested: analysis.storefront at storefront×filing grain, analysis.storefront_latest view, seven vacancy-exposure columns on analysis.address by UPDATE; no rent data exists at address grain anywhere public.** *(2026-09-10, owner, GTM-137)*
Owner asked whether Loci has vacant-storefront and asking-rent data. Research pass: the only address-level public vacancy source is the DOF Storefront Registry (Local Law 157, NYC Open Data 92iy-9c3n); asking rents exist only at corridor grain (REBNY/CBRE PDFs) or inside marketplaces (LoopNet/CoStar, Crexi) whose terms forbid scraping; HUD USPS vacancy is tract-grain and gated. Probe findings that changed the design: "Reporting Year" is a text label — the real key is Filing Due Date (11 filings), five of them vacant-only supplements; pooling them with the annual full-universe filing reads as 100% vacancy. vacant_6_30_or_date_sold is not mixed (only YES exists; sale date separate). Y/YES and N/NO are a form change. No storefront identifier; unit blank 87% of rows; a premises files four rows/year legitimately — deduped on ingest. Tax Class 1 essentially absent (0.27% MN+BK), so rowhouse corner stores under-covered. 4,780 MN+BK rows are (0,0). Lease expiry published 2024-06-03 filing only, then dropped. Inventory (D61 first: 26 objects): ONE new table analysis.storefront, one row per storefront per filing (253,519 MN+BK rows, 31,797 premises, premises_id = BBL|unit stable); derived per-premises fields in VIEW analysis.storefront_latest; seven measures by UPDATE only on analysis.address (vacant_storefronts_400m, storefronts_400m as denominator so absence/no-filing distinguishable, nearest_vacant_storefront_m/_id/_business, nearest_vacant_lease_expired, storefront_asof), disjoint from screen columns and D62 pipeline columns; network metres via D62 helper. Snapshot = one filing (full universe wins), pinned by test. Two double-count bugs caught in live run (backup data/loci.duckdb.bak.20260910-165510): pooled 2025-06-03 annual with 2025-02-15 supplement (13.79% vacancy), fixed to one filing (9.93%); same in storefront_latest. Snapshot 2024-12-31 — MN 23,411 storefronts / 2,607 vacant (11.14%); BK 14,907 / 1,200 (8.05%); 38,318 / 3,807 total. Of 177,078 eligible MN+BK gap addresses, 109,311 (61.7%) have ≥1 vacant storefront within 400 m and 44,048 (24.9%) have ≥3; non-filtering proof byte-identical. Ground truth: 335 E 54 St bodega cluster → 26 vacant within 400 m network; Fulton Mall 15+ within 300 m; Gowanus/Carroll Gardens up to 14. Suite 443 passed. Caveats DB cannot enforce: self-reported, non-filing invisible; annual snapshot not a listing; NO rent and NO square footage anywhere public; primary_business_activity on vacant is "NO BUSINESS ACTIVITY IDENTIFIED" — prior use 84% recoverable by ARG_MAX over filings, premises-level only; construction_reported counted vacant; vacant_storefronts_400m ⊂ storefronts_400m, never add; only 36 of 3,806 vacant carry lease expiry. Webmap "Vacant storefronts" overlay shipped the same day: 2,750 vacant premises drawn (MN 1,857, BK 893; 1,708 vacant two-plus consecutive years; 61 construction-reported), legend denominators MN 2,607/23,411 (11.1%) and BK 1,200/14,907 (8.0%) recomputed from the data, snapshot 2024-12-31 (full filing 2025-06-03, DOF vintage 2026-04-09) derived not hard-coded; gap popups carry vacant/registered within 400 m and the nearest vacant storefront with prior use (unknown for 16%) and lease expiry ("not reported" for all but 36); a filter for gap addresses with ≥1/≥3 vacant storefronts nearby. Laundry gap layer: 33,198 of 73,335 addresses have ≥1 vacant storefront within 400 m, 9,958 have ≥3, and 9,769 have ZERO registered storefronts nearby — the reason the denominator ships in every popup. Data dir 50.6 → 58.4 MB; storefronts.json 195 KB. Suite 443 → 463 (one pipeline test had been silently shadowed by a duplicate name, revealed by ruff F811). Caveat: the overlay's consecutive-year run is anchored at the snapshot year, whereas storefront_latest anchors at each premises' own last observation (often the 2025 supplement, which has no denominator).

**D68 — Headroom (slack) has no predictive skill; bars, cafés and restaurants cluster rather than saturate; the grade carries two regimes per category, and headroom is descriptive only, never a forecast.** *(2026-09-10, owner, GTM-138)*
Owner hypothesis (Gowanus): the category exists but thinly and thousands of residents are arriving, so "more of the same type" could work — intensive-margin headroom question meant to feed fair-price/grade design; owner asked to check Greenpoint too. Backtest same day (data-scientist, Opus; report scratchpad headroom/headroom_backtest.md): headroom_2013 = norm density × pop_2013 − estab_2013 on ZBP ZIP × category (MN+BK, ZIPs with pop ≥5k; ZCTA ACS 2013/2023), outcome Δestab 2013→2023. Result: no out-of-sample skill — leave-one-ZIP-out, 1 of 14 categories beat persistence/drift/population-only, and that one (convenience) is 2017 NAICS 445110→445120 reclassification (grocery −25%, convenience +242%, sum flat). Structurally headroom is affine recombination of population and count, adds nothing conditional on them. For bar (Spearman −0.26) and cafe_bakery (t −4.23) the sign is NEGATIVE: ZIPs above norm added more — clustering, not slack. Greenpoint 11222 read "full" on bars/cafés/restaurants 2013 (−13.7/−3.2/−13.4), added +12/+27/+49. Forward headroom Gowanus ZIPs (11215/11217/11231) with 25,584 pipeline residents (2.0/unit): none for bars/cafés on headcount norm, +9–13 restaurants, +8–18 laundries, +10–17 hair, +5–8 pharmacies; Greenpoint +11 laundries, +4 pharmacies. Age-scaled norm (per 18–34 resident) flips Greenpoint restaurants −13.4→+33.7 (2013), −41.5→+10.9 (today). Ruling for grade: two regimes per category — chore categories (laundry/grocery/pharmacy/hair) behave like demand÷incumbents and pipeline adds directly; social categories (bar/café/restaurant) reward incumbent proximity and cap at spend per resident by age/income, not headcount. Headroom ships as descriptive ratio with interval (mature-ZIP bar density 0.00–1.84/1,000 around 0.33), in ONE supply universe (never ZBP norm vs Loci count; D40 gap 2–8×), age-scaled for bars — never a forecast. Next increment GTM-138: per-category clustering-vs-saturation coefficient.

**D69 — Three owner rulings land together: a per-category `anchor_is_floor` exception to D52 for childcare, F2 tightened to the primary demand regressor, and the screen re-run on the resulting supply — childcare ships, pharmacy is un-applied.** *(2026-09-11, owner, GTM-139)*
Ruling 1 amends D52 rather than relaxing it: `childcare` now carries `anchor_is_floor: true` in `categories.yaml`, because D52's veto rests on the premise that a dense registry's silence is evidence, and for childcare that premise is false by construction — the DOHMH roster `gy3q-4tzp` covers Article 47 and Article 43 group settings only, while OCFS-licensed home-based Family and Group Family Day Care is published by no NYC feed, so the registry could not corroborate a home-based provider however real it is, and the veto was deleting supply precisely where home-based care dominates (`in_principled` had fallen 6,066 → 2,657 the moment the anchor qualified at coverage 0.846). The flag suspends only the lone-aggregator veto: the anchor still ranks first for canonical geometry and name, still feeds `anchor_coverage`, still qualifies, and `in_principled` for childcare returned to 6,066 — equal to `in_all` — moving citywide principled supply 138,403 → 141,812 and the live `supply_hash` f1b12ab55878 → 06bb6f357cc1; the flag is persisted on `analysis.category_anchor` and read by a new append-only migration `sql/013_floor_anchor.sql` so the view stays self-contained SQL, and it is folded into `supply_hash` so a rule change that moved nothing today still changes the stamp. Three tests pin the semantics — flag off, the veto still fires on the identical fixture; flag on, the lone aggregator is retained and `in_principled == in_all` for that category with the CORROBORATED ⊂ PRINCIPLED ⊂ ALL nesting intact — plus a drift check that refuses a floor flag on any category with no registry source measured in it, because a floor is a floor *under* a roster and flagging a category whose anchor was never ingested would be silently wrong the day one landed.

Ruling 2 splits F2 into F2a (the Brooklyn contrast CI excludes 1.0 with the stated sign, unchanged) and F2b (the primary demand regressor's own Brooklyn Conley CI excludes zero with that same sign), because F2a is a contrast over the whole age block and pharmacy cleared it in D66 at 1.585 [1.236, 2.032] while `b(age_65_plus_share)` carried a t of +0.90 — five-sixths of the contrast was "fewer 18–34s" — which is the GTM-109 defect, a number wearing a label it did not earn; the gate message now names which sub-test failed, a fit missing its Brooklyn SEs fails closed rather than skipping, and a fixture in which the contrast passes and the primary CI includes zero is refused by test. On the new supply, bar reproduces D63 exactly and passes (w18 +0.404 pooled, +0.596 Brooklyn t +2.44, contrast 1.451 [1.196, 1.761], dispersion 3.61×); childcare passes on materially stronger coefficients than D65's, `b(under_18_share)` +2.744 pooled (Conley se 0.446, t +6.15) and +2.302 in Brooklyn, East Village → Borough Park 2.990 [2.125, 4.208] pooled and 2.353 [1.662, 3.330] in Brooklyn, R² 0.601, dispersion 3.24× — different from D65's 3.556 because the floor anchor changed both the numerator and the all-POI denominator of the composition outcome; pharmacy is REFUSED at `b(age_65_plus_share)` +0.396, se 0.284, t +1.39, CI [−0.161, +0.954], and the D66 figure of +0.90 does not reproduce because it predates the anchor — the point estimate moves with the denominator, the verdict does not.

Ruling 3 applied both surviving curves and retired pharmacy's: its stale fit JSON was moved out of `data/interim` (a gate failure leaves yesterday's file in place by design, and `apply` then refuses on staleness rather than proceeding), pharmacy's 281,839 `age_fit` rows are NULL, childcare's 281,839 are populated with MOEs, and bar's 281,839 are unchanged in count. The screen was then re-run citywide in the canonical order — address-gaps ALL, address-demand ALL, pipeline, storefronts, age-fit apply — stamping supply_hash 06bb6f357cc1 on all 767,337 addresses; eligible rose 587,408 → 593,930 and Σn_missing 1,389,900 → 1,398,892, with the non-filtering identity exact on the new run (count{ratio>1, eligible} = 1,398,892 = Σn_missing), childcare-lead gaps falling 20,949 addresses in 45 clusters to 9,289 in 17, and bar-lead rising 144,731/230 to 148,221/240. That last number is the session's surprise and worth recording: eligibility is `present_count ≥ 12 of 15`, so +3,409 childcare POIs pushed 6,522 addresses over the gate and *raised* every other category's gap count on a run whose only supply change was childcare — D51's "the eligibility gate does no work" holds inside MN+BK and not outside it. The ranking barely moves: the top-50 MN+BK cluster list is identical by gap_score and gap_score_fit (Jaccard 1.000), bar-lead-only is 0.961 against D63's 0.923, and childcare's F4 ordering clears on the live curve with Borough Park at 2.306 ± 0.811 against East Village 0.718 ± 0.120 — passing, but with a band three times bar's, so childcare's multiplier must never render without its MOE. The UPDATE-only layers the rebuild cleared were re-applied and verified identical (pipeline 281,842/278,909; storefronts 281,842/281,835/9,625), `age_fit_lead` and `gap_score_fit` cover 267,327 addresses, the webmap was re-exported on the new gap set (142 files), and the D64 childcare live-ordering test that had been a deliberate skip now runs and passes: suite 503 passed, 1 skipped, that skip being pharmacy's live-ordering test, which is the state the ruling intends. The fix for pharmacy remains the NYS Board of Pharmacy registry, not a relaxed gate. *Correction (D71):* the "bar-lead 144,731 → 148,221" figures here count eligible addresses with bar as lead, including 17,329 that are not gaps; the bar-lead GAP count is 130,892, the delta +3,267, of which 1,832 came through the eligibility gate and the rest is childcare losing the argmax at already-eligible addresses, which is the ranking behaving correctly; see docs/eligibility_gate.md.

**D70 — Per-category density elasticity shipped as package data: eight daily-needs categories saturate, none cluster; supersedes D68's "bars/cafés cluster" reading; two gate rules added (unanimous-sign rejection; regime requirement relaxed).** *(2026-09-11, owner, GTM-138)*
Owner: "lets do it" on GTM-138. Built src/loci/model/density_elasticity.py + density_elasticity.yaml (config lives with the code), `loci density-elasticity fit [--dry-run] [--bandwidth-km] | show`, tests/test_density_elasticity.py (29 tests); suite 501 passed. Panel: 79 MN+BK ZIPs with 2013 pop ≥5k, ZBP 2013→2023 via analysis.zip_category_establishments, ZCTA ACS 2013/2023 population and income cached under data/raw/acs/ (same Census API as acs.py). Estimand per category: Δestab_2013→2023 on 2013 density (estab per 1,000 residents) with Δlog pop, log pop_2013, log income_2013, Δlog income, borough FE; HC3 and Conley 3 km SEs; Moran's I on residuals; leave-one-ZIP-out sign stability; standardized cross-category placebo (real |β_std| must exceed the 90th percentile of |β_std| from regressing the category's growth on OTHER categories' 2013 density). Classification: saturating / clustering / no_signal by |t_Conley| ≥ 1.96 AND LOZO sign stability ≥ 0.90 AND placebo. Result: NOTHING clusters. Saturating (8): childcare β −14.73 (t −2.10; only category with spatially autocorrelated residuals, Moran +0.40), clinic −13.57 (−4.77), laundry −10.82 (−4.20), grocery −8.79 (−4.76), tailor_repair −8.74 (−5.74), fitness −7.37 (−4.42), nails_beauty −6.58 (−3.80), bank −4.49 (−4.95). No signal (8): bar +3.47 (t +1.63, fails significance), cafe_bakery +6.20 (t +4.83, LOZO 1.00, but FAILS THE PLACEBO: standardized 5.354 vs p90 5.820 — 2013 restaurant density (6.18) and hardware density (5.88) predict café growth better than café's own, i.e. generic gentrification), convenience, hardware, food_retail (fails placebo by a hair, 2.726 vs 2.804), restaurant, pharmacy. Supersedes D68's reading that bars/cafés cluster; D68's chore-category conclusions stand and are strengthened. Two gate rules: (1) the log (proportional-growth) form Δlog(estab) was refused by REJECT_UNANIMOUS_SIGN — all 16 βs negative, and its only three "significant" verdicts (convenience, tailor_repair, childcare) were exactly the three series with base-year definitional breaks; the base count sits in its own denominator, so the form measures its own transform; the rule is symmetric (an all-positive table fails identically) and forces no sign on any category; the level form was adopted and the choice recorded in the YAML. (2) The original gate required a category in EVERY regime including clustering — a spec error that forces a sign at the portfolio level; relaxed 2026-09-11 to: placebo ran for every category, at least one category carries a signal of either sign, every regime in the allowed set; documented in the module docstring and YAML header, pinned by test. Grade usage recorded in the YAML header: saturating → demand pool ÷ incumbents, pipeline residents add directly; no_signal → residents only, incumbent count enters in neither direction; no category is scored with incumbents in its favour. Caveats: ZIP grain (11215 mixes Park Slope and Gowanus; 11249 merged into 11211); ZBP employer-only and noise-infused 2017+; grocery/convenience reclassification 2017 (also fitted combined as food_retail); revealed-supply panel; cafe_bakery and food_retail are hair's-breadth placebo calls to re-read if the panel is ever extended below ZIP grain. Gowanus reading: the chore stack (laundry, hair, nails, grocery) is supported; "more bars/cafés because the pipeline is coming" has no support at ZIP grain.

**D71 — Pharmacy anchor lands as the NYS Medicaid roster because the Board of Pharmacy has no bulk form; coverage 0.000 → 0.903, the pharmacy age-fit stays refused on its own regressor; the webmap renders age-fit with its MOE; the eligibility gate is measured and its D69 numbers corrected.** *(2026-09-11, GTM-140)*
Three D69 next actions closed in one pass. The pharmacy registry D66/D69 named — NYSED Office of the Professions establishment registrations — was probed and found to have no bulk form at all: a one-record verification page, no export, no API, `op.nysed.gov/data` 404s, and nothing on data.ny.gov or health.data.ny.gov, so the registry entry is now `excluded` with the probe result written into it; CMS NPPES taxonomy 3336C0003X was probed and rejected too (no coordinates, a 1,200-record enumeration cap that truncates Brooklyn, `status='A'` on 100% of returns against an active count 1.6–2× lower — the D47 stale-licence trap with a federal badge). The anchor is `keti-qx5t`, the NYS Medicaid Enrolled Provider Listing filtered to `profession_or_service='PHARMACY'`, defensible as a pharmacy roster in New York specifically because NYRx (2023-04-01) carved the pharmacy benefit for ALL Medicaid members into fee-for-service, so any pharmacy serving any Medicaid member must enrol and enrolment requires a current NYSED registration; weekly refresh, lat/lon published by the State on 100% of rows. Two silent bugs were caught by probing before building: `mmis_id` is per-PROVIDER not per-storefront (Ideal Care Pharmacy enrols 1621 and 811 Avenue U, 670 m apart), so the key is `mmis_id@address-slug`; and 8 rows carry a (0,0) null-island coordinate, dropped and counted rather than guessed. 2,416 records staged citywide (MN 448, BK 757, QN 719, BX 397, SI 95), hospital (1,270), clinic (142), specialty (11) and supervising-pharmacist (2,911) enrolments excluded by a closed vocabulary that raises on a sixth value and on a blank; `score/dedup.py` got the pharmacy branch (the D65 trap). `anchor_coverage` 0.000 → 0.903 against 1,330 MN+BK ZBP establishments, pharmacy qualifies; canonical pharmacy 5,536 → 7,105 `in_all` and 5,536 → 3,959 `in_principled` as D52's veto removed 3,146 lone-aggregator clusters (MN 534, BK 564, QN 599, BX 300, SI 94, 1,055 outside the res-9 grid); live `supply_hash` 06bb6f357cc1 → 767b28674e30, every other category bit-identical. `anchor_is_floor` deliberately left OFF: at 0.903 the file is above the "load a better anchor" band, the slice it misses skews affluent — the opposite of the bias that makes a veto dangerous — and unlike OCFS home-based care the true roster exists and is merely not downloadable. Caveat the database cannot enforce: `mmis_name` is the legal entity and the aggregators carry the awning, so 516 corroborated aggregator clusters within 40 m of a registry cluster are counted twice (~13% of principled pharmacy), under-stating pharmacy gaps; the fix is the DBA in NPPES `other_names` joined on the `npi` already stored, not a wider `MATCH_METERS`. The pharmacy age-fit on the new supply is still refused, more decisively — `b(age_65_plus_share)` Brooklyn +0.148, Conley se 0.307, t +0.48, CI [−0.454, +0.749] (D66 +0.90 → D69 +1.39 → +0.48): the anchor removed the excuse rather than rescuing the curve; and because +1,569 pharmacy POIs enter the ALL-POI denominator of every composition outcome, the bar and childcare fit files (stamped 06bb6f357cc1) are correctly stale and need genuine re-estimation before the next apply. The webmap now renders the age-fit: the gap layer carries `age_fit_lead`, `age_fit_lead_moe`, `gap_score_fit`, `gap_score` and the lead's `age_fit_source` in parallel arrays, with three states drawn distinctly — NULL (never applied), 1.0 with NULL MOE and source (identity: the lead has no curve, drawn unranked and never folded into the ramp), and a fitted value with ± always shown — behind a "supply-revealed; see caveat" expander that renders `AGE_FIT_DISCLAIMER` untruncated, plus a default-off toggle to reshade by `gap_score_fit` that is disabled with an explanatory tooltip for categories with no curve; `meta.json` gains an `ageFit` block listing exactly the categories with a fit file on disk, their timestamps, supply hash, median MOE, the caveat verbatim and a staleness warning (`loci export-webmap` does not yet echo that warning to the terminal). The eligibility-gate investigation (`docs/eligibility_gate.md`, read-only) found the gate binds nowhere in Manhattan, on 5.8% of Brooklyn addresses and on 20–60% of QN/BX/SI; four categories have reach longer than the gate's fixed 800 m window (tailor 960, clinic 960, hardware 960, fitness 1,200), so the gate's "present" and the screen's "present" are different predicates sharing a word; it corrects D69's record — the bar-lead 148,221 included 17,329 non-gaps, the gap count is 130,892, the delta +3,267 of which only 1,832 came through the gate, and inside MN+BK the whole event is 359 addresses — and it shows the gate's importance to the deliverable is 0.93 by units and 0.32 by gap score, so the cluster-ranking choice is the larger open question. Recommendation, pending the owner: long-term a POI-free built-form gate (PLUTO retail floor area within 800 m; AUC 0.873 vs 0.856 against the DOF Storefront Registry, with a pre-registered NTA-holdout test and an externally derived threshold), interim the gate computed on `in_all` (one predicate; would have made D69's crossing exactly zero; moves the MN+BK deliverable under 2%). Suite 536 passed / 1 deliberate skip; check-sources ok at 32 sources. The screen has NOT been re-run on supply 767b28674e30; that second pass waits on the gate ruling.

**D72 — Construction-activity evidence on the development pipeline: permitted splits into active / lapsed / stalled from DOB permit issuance and DOB NOW approved permits; units_active_400m and units_stalled_400m ride `loci pipeline`; Gowanus red-team downgrades the earlier reading.** *(2026-09-11, owner, GTM-141)*
Owner: "our goal is to take this model and start to make recommendations. close the gap." A contrarian pass on the Gowanus reading (same day) graded the load-bearing claims: arriving homes A on record / D on timing (14 of 20 permitted jobs vested before the 2022-06-15 421-a deadline, none with a CO 40 months later); laundry-as-lead-gap D (of 17,628 homes at laundry-lead addresses in the wide box, 59% sit in 51+-unit buildings that carry in-building laundry and most of the rest are 1–5-unit rowhouses where NYCHVS says 50–74% have in-unit washers; LL84/listings evidence covers 1.9% and confirms laundry on 3 of the 12 largest flagged buildings; the DCWP anchor vetoes 25 of 83 candidate laundries as lone-aggregator); the supply counts the owner was shown were in_all not principled (restaurant 886→443, laundry 83→58; the chore-vs-social ratio 7.6:1 is at par with MN+BK 7.9:1 and survives only in the Bond–4th Ave / Douglass–9th St core, where pharmacy 0.07×, convenience 0.14×, grocery 0.29×, hair 0.33× baseline are all thinner than laundry 0.28× and bar is 1.12×); the "restaurant/bar takeover on a below-market lease" line had no data behind it and is withdrawn as a data claim; D68's forward headroom numbers had been quoted forward despite D68 ruling them descriptive only — the rejected-predictor pattern, flagged. Web research (Tavily) on the pipeline: 499 President (350u, Union Market grocery, Paulie Gee's, a PT clinic), 420 Carroll, 251 Douglass (occupied 2026-02) and 267 Bond (517u) are delivered/leasing; 300 Nevins (654u) and 545 Sackett (255u) are building; 175 Third St (1,071u, Life Time signed) closed financing only in June 2026 with first move-in ~2028; Gowanus Green (950 affordable) is stalled on remediation; 18 Gowanus projects use the state PILOT workaround for the lapsed 421-a; the rezoning's active-use mandate adds ~100,000 sq ft of storefront; Gowanus new-build retail asks ~$70/SF vs Court St $115 and Smith St $90 (REBNY H1 2025, both softening); CSO tanks online early 2029. Build (data-engineer): sources/cities/nyc/dob_permits.py using ipu4-2q9a (BIS permit issuance; TEXT dates in two formats in one column, byte-identical duplicate rows, parsed both ways, aggregates min/max) and rbx6-tga4 (DOB NOW approved permits; job number = 9-char prefix of job_filing_number); w9ak-ipjd rejected (coarser duplicates). No construction-start date exists in any feed — job_start_date is the declared start copied onto every renewal — so the column is activity_status (active = permit issued/renewed within 12 months of asof or expiration in the future; lapsed = expired 0–12 months, no CO; stalled = expired >12 months or permitted >5 years with no activity; complete; n/a when no permit row matched, never stalled), NOT an invented under_construction stage. sql/014_dev_pipeline_activity.sql: UPDATE-only columns on analysis.dev_pipeline (last_permit_issued, last_permit_expires, permit_evidence_source, activity_status); `loci pipeline-activity` writes dev_pipeline only; units_active_400m / units_stalled_400m added to PIPELINE_COLUMNS so the concurrent session's canonical re-apply order (`loci pipeline` → `loci storefronts` → age-fit) is unchanged; NULL not 0 when no evidence. Bug surfaced: dcp_housing.write_dev_pipeline used a positional INSERT…SELECT that the new columns broke (would have silently mis-mapped type-compatible columns); now a named column list. Live (asof 2026-09-11, backup data/loci.duckdb.bak.20260911-142920): MN+BK 7,364 permitted/partial jobs → active 2,244 (29,657u), lapsed 616 (1,382u), stalled 4,454 (9,195u), complete 4 (714u), n/a 46 (353u); stalled = 22.3% of permitted units, independently reproducing D62 caveat 3's 23%; validation: unit totals reconcile exactly, 0 addresses with active+stalled > permitted. Gowanus box: 13 active jobs / 3,433u, 7 stalled / 1,673u (33% of units; four never renewed once; B00625252 + B00649561 share BBL 3004710200 — one site, not two risks); 175 Third St B00680917 active (renewed 2026-03); Gowanus laundry-lead gap addresses: 80% mostly-active exposure. Webmap: the existing pipeline overlay should style dots by dev_pipeline.activity_status (not done; another session held webmap_export.py/index.html); the two gap-layer fields registered in PIPELINE_NOT_EXPORTED. Caveats: a renewal is not a shovel (separates abandoned from not-abandoned only); active + stalled ≤ permitted, never equal (lapsed and n/a are in neither); last_permit_expires is a MAX over work types; DCP-filed jobs that permitted after 2026-01-21 still read n/a (D62 caveat 1); rbx6-tga4 permit_status 'Signed-off' exists per work type and is deliberately not ingested (ticket it). The `pipeline-activity` CLI block was carried in the concurrent session's commit cdfb46c; the rest landed in this session's commit. Suite 554 passed. Standing ruling for recommendations from here: a recommendation card must grade each load-bearing claim (demand, arriving homes by activity, supply ratio in the principled universe, addressable households, space, economics) and may not say "act" while any load-bearing claim is at grade D.

**D73 — Supply ratio vs the MN+BK baseline per address×category on the principled set (400 m network), homes_400m, and a laundry addressable-homes haircut: laundry is NOT thin in the Gowanus core (0.94×); pharmacy (0.00×), convenience (0.40×) and hardware (0.72×) are.** *(2026-09-11, owner, GTM-143)*
Built by a data-scientist agent from the D72 red-team's three findings (in_all counts shown to the owner; "is there a gap" is the wrong statistic for a present-but-thin category; the laundry pool is mostly unaddressable). src/loci/model/supply_ratio.py + `loci supply-ratio [--boroughs] [--radius-m 400] [--fit-baseline] [--dry-run]`: for every eligible address × category, supply_400m = principled POIs within 400 m NETWORK distance (87 s for MN+BK by sourcing Dijkstra from the 39,083 distinct graph nodes that 282k addresses collapse onto, 611 calls; graph symmetry licenses the reversal, pinned by test); homes_400m on analysis.address (category-independent, so not duplicated 15× on address_category per D61); supply_per_1k and supply_ratio_vs_base on analysis.address_category by UPDATE only, SET lists disjoint. Baseline per category = MN+BK median supply_per_1k over 267,329 eligible addresses, shipped as src/loci/model/supply_baseline.yaml stamped with supply_hash 767b28674e30 (note: not the 06bb6f357cc1 of D69 — the set moved with D71's pharmacy anchor), aggregate estimator recorded alongside; tailor_repair's median is 0 so it uses the aggregate, stamped. `--fit-baseline` is the only path that rewrites the YAML and is deliberately NOT in the re-apply order. src/loci/model/laundry_haircut.yaml (hand-maintained, separate from the generated baseline): addressable_homes_400m_laundry = Σ units × (1 − p_in-home): 1-unit 0.74 and 2-unit 0.50 from NYCHVS 2023, 3–5 units 0.35, 6–50 units 0.15, 51+ units 0.85 as owner-adjustable priors; address_laundry_evidence overrides to 1.0. Re-apply command handed to the concurrent session: `uv run loci supply-ratio --boroughs MN,BK` at the end of the canonical order; re-run verified byte-identical (sum homes_400m 801,269,217; sum supply_400m 25,246,768), screen untouched. Gowanus core (Bond–4th Ave, Douglass–9th St; 1,831 eligible addresses, median 3,646 homes within 400 m): pharmacy 0.00× (961 core addresses have zero principled pharmacies within 400 m; 18 principled vs 29 in_all in the wide box), tailor 0.00×, convenience 0.40×, hardware 0.72×, laundry 0.94×, childcare 0.94×, hair 0.98×, grocery 1.07×, clinic 1.33×, nails 1.75×, restaurant 1.84×, bank 1.97×, cafe 2.89×, fitness 3.39×, bar 5.58×. Wide Gowanus box and Williamsburg both rank convenience and pharmacy thinnest too. The red-team's laundry 0.28× reproduces only on the laundry-gap subset, which is selected on laundry being absent — circular; unconditioned, laundry is normal for this city. Addressability: the median core laundry-gap address keeps 718 of its 5,622 nearby homes (13%) after the haircut; zero core gap addresses reach 1,500 addressable homes; 69 buildings of 51+ units hold 14,740 of the wide box's 40,275 homes. Ruling: the recommendation ranks categories by supply_ratio_vs_base on the principled set, never by gap count, and laundry is withdrawn as the Gowanus lead; pharmacy, convenience and hardware lead. Caveats: baseline is revealed supply (D6 circularity) — 1.0× means "normal for this city", never "correctly provisioned"; poi_supply is a view, hash stamped per row and in the YAML with a drift test; median vs aggregate estimators differ 15–50% (bar 5.58× vs 1.89×), both ship and the choice is the largest lever; three of five haircut priors are judgement, the 51+ prior drives the laundry result; only MN+BK written, other boroughs NULL. 46 tests; suite 600 passed / 1 skipped.

**D74 — `loci recommend` shipped: an evidence-graded recommendation card per area × category whose verdict cannot say "act" while any load-bearing claim is grade D; the first Gowanus card says "do not act on this data" for 14 of 15 categories, all on economics.** *(2026-09-11, owner, GTM-144)*
Built by a developer agent on the D72 ruling. src/loci/model/recommend.py + recommend_grades.yaml (owner-adjustable thresholds, load_bearing/context split, cheapest_check text) + `loci recommend --area NAME --bbox lat0,lon0,lat1,lon1 [--nta] [--category c]... [--format md|json] [--out PATH]`; docs/recommendations/ holds the cards. Seven sections per category, each graded A–D with the rule and the reason: (1) demand now (homes_400m, income vs MN+BK with MOE, 18–34 share; A if MOE < 20% else B; context); (2) arriving homes (units_permitted_400m split active/stalled/other from D72; B ≥ 70% active, C 40–70%, D < 40% or NULL; takes the WORSE of exposure-weighted and median-address share); (3) supply thinness (median supply_ratio_vs_base on the principled set with baseline and supply_hash; A if hash matches and n ≥ 200, B 50–199, else C; plus the D70 regime); (4) addressable demand (laundry haircut B on priors, A only when evidence coverage > 50%; other categories C "no haircut yet"); (5) space (vacant/registered storefronts within 400 m; B; D if nobody filed; context); (6) economics (comps_for: A ≥ 5 neighbourhood comps with cash flow, B ≥ 5 borough, C thin, D no cash-flow data); (7) coverage (anchor qualification, Google validation in the bbox; A validated, B anchored registry, C aggregator-only). Verdict = min grade over the load-bearing sections 2, 3, 4, 6, 7: "act" ≥ B, "diligence" C, "do not act on this data" any D, and every D section prints the cheapest check that would change it (site walk; Google check; 3–5 P&Ls). Never emits an expected profit; economics reports supportable rent only. Hash drift between supply_baseline.yaml and the live poi_supply warns and caps section 3 at C rather than refusing the card — argued (six sections never read the supply view; C already makes "act" unreachable; the cap can never raise a grade) and pinned. Tests brute-force all 4^7 grade combinations to prove "act" is unreachable with a load-bearing D and that context sections never move the verdict; synthetic in-memory warehouse fixture; 38 tests, suite 638 passed / 1 skipped. First card, Gowanus core (bbox 40.670,-73.995,40.682,-73.982): 14 of 15 categories "do not act on this data", all on economics (no cash-flow comps anywhere); hardware and tailor_repair reach "diligence" only because the listings CSV holds a couple of cash-flow rows; arriving homes C not B (72% of exposure-weighted permitted units active, median address 50% — the "80% mostly-active" line in D72 flattered the typical doorway); demand-now B (income $160,652 ± $35,726, MOE 22%); supply ratios per D73. What closes the gap from here, in order of leverage: (a) 3–5 P&Ls per category from businesses for sale in the area — BizQuest/BizBuySell block automated and browser access and that block is honoured, so this is a human collection into data/benchmarks/bizbuysell_nyc_listings.csv; (b) the Google coverage check on the core for pharmacy/convenience/hardware (GTM-48 budget, ~$170, owner approval pending); (c) a site walk or per-work-type sign-off (GTM-142) on the 20 permitted jobs. The map overlay styling by activity_status remains a follow-up.

**D75 — The eligibility gate is removed by owner ruling; every address is in the universe, and the cap_m censoring it was hiding is now flagged rather than smoothed.** *(2026-09-13, owner, GTM-146)*
The owner's words (2026-09-13): "I 100% vehemently disagree with 'which addresses count at all'. If an address is truly in a super underdeveloped area, this would completely not count it." The gate existed for exactly one reason — D39 found that the literal port, eligible iff within reach of ≥12 of 15 categories, violated monotonicity because tightening a reach dropped addresses out of eligibility faster than they gained gaps, and a FIXED, reach-independent presence test (≤800 m for ≥12/15) restored it — so retiring it needed a replacement for that role, and with no gate at all the role is filled trivially: the gap set is `ratio > 1` read off each address's own nearest_m against a reach that is fixed input, so tightening any reach can only ADD pairs, and the test now asserts reach × 0.8 removes exactly zero pairs over the whole universe rather than inside a mask. `eligible` survives as an always-TRUE column for schema and query compatibility (`WHERE eligible` in new code is now a bug, and the three surviving `eligible_only` parameters — sample, recommend, supply-ratio's baseline fit — are documented no-ops that exist only so a restored pre-D75 snapshot is not pooled with a gate-free one); `present_count` survives as the descriptive count it always was and is a candidate ranking feature, while the feasibility question the gate was reaching for stays where it belongs, in model/invest.py's PLUTO CommFAR/RetailArea build-or-lease test, with nothing folded across. D51's `cap_m` = 2,400 m censoring artifact, which the gate had been accidentally suppressing, is now fully exposed and is handled in the open by a flag and not a rule: `gap_score` and `cap_m` are both unchanged, because a censored ratio is still the smallest value that ratio could take and leaving it in keeps the ranking monotone, continuous and conservative, while `analysis.address_category.censored` and `analysis.address.lead_censored` carry the fact through the `address_gaps` view into the webmap's gap layer and popup, which now says "nearest X beyond 2,400 m — the distance was not measured" instead of printing the cap as a walk; 143,973 (address, category) pairs sit at the cap and 55,971 addresses have a censored LEAD (SI 20,864 / 18.6%, QN 17,511, BK 11,852, BX 5,734, MN 10), tailor_repair alone accounting for 111,512 of the pairs. The screen was then re-run citywide on supply 767b28674e30 — the second pass D71 deferred, and a genuine re-estimation because the pharmacy anchor moved every composition denominator: bar and childcare both clear their gates on the new denominator (bar w18 +0.404, Conley se 0.203, dispersion 3.61×; childcare under_18_share +2.762, se 0.447, dispersion 3.23×) and pharmacy is refused again on its own primary regressor at t +0.48, CI [−0.454, +0.749]. Over all 767,337 addresses the non-filtering identity is exact — count{ratio > 1} = 3,012,430 = Σ n_missing — against 1,398,892 on the gated D69 run; gap addresses rise 452,491 → 626,145, bar-lead gaps 130,892 → 180,480 in 240 → 264 clusters and childcare-lead gaps 1,342 → 1,775 in 17 → 18 (correcting D69's "childcare 9,289" the same way D71 corrected its bar figure: both counted eligible addresses whose lead was that category, non-gaps included). Every one of the 173,407 formerly-excluded addresses turns out to be a gap — none was a served address the gate had merely mislabelled — and they are overwhelmingly outer-borough: QN 74,050, SI 69,887, BX 14,957, BK 14,509, MN 4, leading on laundry 57,799, bar 49,613 and convenience 28,717, which is the shape the owner's objection predicted. The canonical order was re-applied in full and verified — pipeline 281,842 / 278,909 with units_active and units_stalled at 281,842, storefronts 281,842 / 281,835, age_fit_lead and gap_score_fit 281,840, homes_400m and supply_ratio 281,842 — and the MN+BK deliverable barely moves: the top-50 cluster list by capped units has member-address Jaccard 0.926 against D69, the same nine clusters lead in the same order, and the F4 orderings hold on the live curves (bar East Village 1.117 ± 0.090 over Carnegie Hill 0.834 ± 0.083; childcare Borough Park 2.316 ± 0.818 over East Village 0.716 ± 0.120, a band still seven times bar's). One consequence needed its own sanctioned exception: `supply_baseline.yaml` was fitted over the 267,329 addresses the gate admitted, so although the supply set itself did not move the baseline's universe did, and it was re-fit once on the gate-free universe (n 267,329 → 281,842, `universe` now recorded as "all addresses with homes_400m > 0", `supply_hash` 767b28674e30 unchanged, asof 2026-09-13) before the final `supply-ratio --boroughs MN,BK`, which raised no drift warning. The webmap was re-exported on the new gap set (145 files, 83.6 MB) with `meta.ageFit` listing bar and childcare on supply 767b28674e30 and `stale: []`, and the suite stands at 794 passed / 1 skipped, that skip still being pharmacy's live-ordering test. The owner also stated two further directions, recorded here and not yet built: sample an address near the middle of every known street in the borough so that undeveloped streets are represented, and rank by density.

**D76 — Foot-traffic proxies land at the address grain (transit_entries_400m, jobs_400m): DOT validation reads ρ +0.79 headline, but the contrarian verdict is card context only, never a grade, never the supply-ratio denominator.** *(2026-09-13, GTM-147)*
Built: MTA hourly turnstile/entry ridership for Jun–Aug 2026 (64 weekdays), entries split evenly across entry-allowed entrances per complex, joined to LODES WAC block centroids for jobs; walk-graph Dijkstra at 400 m network distance on every MN+BK address (281,842 addresses, 0 NULL). Validation against the DOT pedestrian-count program (99 corridor points): all-99 transit ρ +0.793, jobs +0.790, homes_400m +0.372 — transit and jobs both beat homes_400m on the headline number. But the headline does not survive stratification: Manhattan-only homes_400m is ρ −0.209 (wrong sign), Brooklyn-only transit is ρ +0.561 with a 95% CI of [0.14, 0.83] — a floor low enough that the correlation cannot be trusted in the borough that carries most of the gap-address universe. In the lowest tercile of foot traffic, homes_400m (+0.686) beats transit (+0.622) — the proxy this column would ride alongside already does better exactly where a foot-traffic column would matter most. A binary any-station-within-400m flag alone gets ρ +0.547, meaning most of transit's signal is "is there a subway stop nearby at all", not a graded count. Endogeneity: ρ(transit, supply_400m) ranges +0.536 (restaurant) down to +0.236 (childcare) across every category, and ρ(transit, supply_ratio_vs_base) is +0.418 for restaurant — transit correlates with the supply it would be used to explain, the exact structure D1 was written to keep out of the model. Even-split bias: 59.7% of MTA entries land at complexes with ≥6 entrances, where the even split across entrances is least defensible (a single busy entrance vs five quiet ones reads identically).
Ruling: (a) card context only — the recommend.py context sections may show transit_entries_400m/jobs_400m, and a zero renders as "no station within a 5-minute walk", never as "low foot traffic"; (b) not an economics grade section — D74's seven-section grade does not gain an eighth section from this; (c) never enters supply_ratio_vs_base's denominator (D73) — that would let transit's endogeneity with supply contaminate the ratio it is supposed to be independent of. Graduation test before either restriction lifts: ≥100 non-corridor DOT-class validation points, Brooklyn-only ρ ≥ 0.6 with CI lower bound > 0.4, and a non-degenerate variable (800 m radius or distance-decay kernel replacing the binary any-station read).
Registry fix on the way: `68hr-j2j7` and `2de2-6x2h` turned out to be Socrata map VIEWS with no columns, not usable as ingest sources; the tabular ridership ids are `i9wp-a4ja` and `cqsj-cfgu`.
This supersedes CONTEXT.md §3.5's budget assumption that SafeGraph-class foot-traffic data costs $200–400: as of 2026, Advan via Dewey Data is $3,600/yr and academic-only, Placer.ai/Replica/Cuebiq are enterprise-only with no published self-serve price, and MTA turnstile data (the free historical alternative) is discontinued in favor of the hourly ridership feed used here. QUESTIONS C2's budget premise is marked superseded; C2's underlying behavioural-outcome question (does the effect appear in behaviour before residence) stays deferred regardless — free proxies answer "is there foot traffic," not "did foot traffic change first."

**Addendum (2026-09-13, commit 3020947):** owner asked for foot traffic broken out by day and time of day, not just a pooled 400 m count. Built `analysis.address_transit_profile` (long: address × day_type{weekday, saturday, sunday} × daypart{early 0–6, am_peak 6–10, midday 10–15, pm_peak 15–19, evening 19–24}), `analysis.address_entrance` (persisted reachable-entrance set, 457,440 pairs, cutting rebuild from a 5-minute graph run to 8 s), a wide view, and `transit_am_pm_share_400m` on `analysis.address`. Live MN+BK run: 1,654,545 profile rows, 110,303 addresses carry a share. MTA hourly ridership is grouped server-side by complex × day-of-week × hour (SoQL `date_extract_hh`), Jun–Aug 2026; conservation against the pooled D76 total is exact (worst relative error 4.4e-16). Brooklyn: 84.5% of addresses with a share read residential-type (AM/PM > 1, median 1.79); East Village reads 0% (a destination catchment); Gowanus core median 1.03 (mixed). Validation per DOT window: AM +0.620 / midday +0.644 / PM +0.807 (all 99 points), Brooklyn-only +0.583 / +0.403 / +0.568 — but the off-diagonal is sharper than the diagonal: the AM count is ranked better by pm_peak (+0.788) than by am_peak itself, so at DOT points every window is best ranked by whichever daypart is largest, i.e. station size, and the levels carry no time-of-day discrimination — the D76 ruling (card context only, never a grade or the supply-ratio denominator) stands unchanged for the levels. The share, being a ratio, is the object that might actually discriminate residential vs destination catchments and is carried forward as X7, open. Saturday/Sunday profiles are unvalidated (DOT counts weekdays only). Gowanus core weekday p50 by daypart: am_peak 1,868, pm_peak 1,823, evening 694; Saturday midday 1,215.

**D77 — Growing-chain watchlist ships as package data: 121 brands, a 2026-09 detect snapshot, and Tavily press enrichment; the honest growth measure needs a second snapshot.** *(2026-09-13, GTM-148)*
`src/loci/chains/watchlist.yaml` holds the 121-brand watchlist as package data (config lives with the code that reads it; `data/` is gitignored, so a hand-curated watchlist cannot live there without disappearing). `docs/CHAINS.md` is generated, not hand-written. Detect counts are a floor, not a census: only about 60% of brand-locations in the open-data sources carry any first-seen/opened date at all, and DOHMH, Overture, SNAP and DCWP carry none — `last_inspection_date` is deliberately never read as a proxy for first-seen, because a re-inspection of an existing location would silently manufacture a fake opening. Given that, a single snapshot can only report a count, not a trend; the honest growth measure is the month-over-month snapshot delta, which requires a second snapshot (2026-10) that has not been taken yet.
Seed findings from the first snapshot + press pass: Apollo Bagels is at 9 NYC locations (+5), plausible because its Industry City central bakehouse keeps each storefront low-capex (no on-site baking line to duplicate). Vital Climbing Gym is at 4 NYC locations with 0 net new — its last opening was November 2024 and its UES/West Harlem locations were acquired in 2021 — so it reads as a sales/acquisition prospect, not a growth signal, and the watchlist must not conflate "present and expanding" with "present, acquired, and static." Food/drink brands are roughly 80% of confirmed openings in the snapshot. Pharmacy chains show a net −42 locations (Rite Aid alone −35); the vacated boxes are being absorbed by discount grocers, gyms, and LaundryBee (~20 outer-borough laundromats on 20-year leases) rather than by other pharmacies — a category contraction, not a like-for-like replacement. 27 rows imported from an earlier pass were relabelled from `unverified` to `reported` because they carry evidence (a press hit or a corroborating count) behind them.
Press budget is enforced in code, not by convention: 60 queries per run, with a `--dry-run` path that plans the query set and shows the credit cost before any call is made.
Operational note: macOS's case-insensitive filesystem collided `docs/chains.md` with the already-generated `docs/CHAINS.md`, silently overwriting one with the other; the prose process doc is now named `docs/chains-process.md` to avoid the collision permanently, not just avoid it once.

**D78 — The screen scope is enforced, not defaulted: Manhattan and Brooklyn only.** *(2026-09-13, owner, GTM-149)*
The owner restated the scope in one line — "we are still focused on Manhattan and Brooklyn" — and the screen tables had quietly stopped obeying it: D61 ran `address-gaps --borough ALL` to attach the address-grain demographics, every re-run since matched that call out of habit, and `analysis.address` had been carrying all 767,337 addresses with `address_category` at 11.5 M rows ever since, so a reader of the deliverable was reading Queens and Staten Island alongside the two boroughs the project actually screens. A default was not enough, because a default is what D61 overrode: `SCREEN_BOROUGHS = ("MN", "BK")` now lives beside `BOROCODE` in `sources/cities/nyc/addresses.py` (the codes are NYC-specific, so `model/` and `score/` take the scope as an argument and still name no borough), `loci address-gaps` defaults to `MNBK` and REFUSES `--borough ALL` or any borough outside MN+BK before it opens the database, citing D48/D78, unless `--allow-out-of-scope` is passed for a deliberate documented run. Because `write_address_gaps` only delete-then-inserts the boroughs it is handed, a narrower re-run could never clean up after the wider one, so a new idempotent `prune_out_of_scope` removes out-of-scope rows from `address_category` and `address` together on every in-scope write; it took out 485,495 address and 7,282,425 category rows on its first pass and has returned zero ever since. The canonical order was then re-applied in full — address-gaps MN then BK, address-demand, pipeline, storefronts, age-fit apply (fits current on supply 767b28674e30, not re-fitted), supply-ratio, address-access, revenue, export-webmap — leaving `analysis.address` at 281,842 rows and `address_category` at 4,227,630, the non-filtering identity exact at count{ratio>1} = Σn_missing = 531,773 over 190,973 gap addresses, `lead_censored` at MN 10 / BK 11,852, bar-lead gaps 74,114 in 90 clusters and childcare-lead 52 in 1, and the UPDATE-only layers back at 281,842 / 278,909 / 281,835 / 281,840 with `access_run_at` NULL on nobody. The ruling changes the universe of rows and not one number inside it: Σgap_score is byte-identical at 412,007.8556131311, a 1,000-address sample matches the pre-run backup on all eight screen columns with zero differences, and the top-50 cluster list is identical by member addresses with no id relabelled. `analysis.address_demographics` deliberately stays citywide at 767,337 rows — it is the D48 data foundation, along with the raw sources, `poi_dedup`/`poi_supply` and the hex tables, because a POI in Queens is still the nearest pharmacy to an address in Brooklyn; `address_laundry_gaps` is a view and followed on its own. Layers keyed on `analysis.address` rows (the access columns, the transit profile) are rebuilt underneath by address-gaps and must be re-applied in the canonical order. Webmap re-exported (145 files, 83.6 MB) with `meta.ageFit.gapsSupplyHash` 767b28674e30 and `stale []`; suite 857 passed / 1 skipped.

**D79 — Storefront first-seen ledger: every storefront carries the month Loci first saw it, independent of source dates.** *(2026-09-13, owner, GTM-150)*
The owner asked that every storefront carry the month Loci first saw it, because only about 60% of chain locations had a source date at all (D77) — most POIs have none. Built `analysis.poi_presence` + the `analysis.poi_first_seen` view, `loci poi-snapshot --month` and `loci check-presence` (commit 46e50ff, sql/018). Key finding: the dedup `cluster_id` is an input-order artefact, not a stable identity — 0.00% of ids survive a byte-identical row-order shuffle — so the ledger key is instead a content hash of normalized name + rounded lon/lat + category, with the canonical `poi_id` folded in only for the 118 nameless (CJK/Arabic/generic) collisions (0.052%) that the content hash alone cannot separate. Backfill run 2026-09: 227,548 locations, 52.3% carry a real source_date, 47.7% are `backfill_censored`, 0 are `observed`, 100% coverage, and the run is idempotent (re-run mints 0, retires 0). The view deliberately returns a NULL `first_seen_month` for censored rows, so the backfill can never be misreported as a wave of September openings. `chains.brand_location` now keys on the ledger instead of `cluster_id` — two monthly snapshots keyed on `cluster_id` were not comparable to each other, which is exactly the growth-delta problem D77 flagged; `detect` now reports 60% dated-by-source / 0% observed / 40% censored rather than implying a clean count. Rule going forward: a skipped month is a permanent hole (an observation cannot be reconstructed after the fact), and `last_seen_month` falling behind is not evidence of closure. Why this shape and not per-source date columns: sources disagree with each other and most carry no date at all, so one durable ledger at the deduplicated-location grain is the only version that stays comparable across snapshots; consumers read the view, never the table directly.

**D80 — Storefront lifecycle filings and pipeline: stores can be dated from what they must declare to government, for all storefronts not just chains.** *(2026-09-13, owner, GTM-152)*
The owner asked whether stores can be dated from what they must declare to the government, for all storefronts not just chains. Lease signings are private (ACRIS records ~400 leases/yr vs 52,700 deeds; NYS DOS entities are holdcos, not per-store LLCs), so the answer is the filing chain. Stage one (commit 2979cc2): `staging.storefront_filing`, 232,667 filings across 7 feeds × 8 ordered stages (`liquor_application`, `fitout_filing`, `license_application`, `permit_issued`, `sign_permit`, `license_issued`, `liquor_active`, `first_inspection`; `outdoor_dining` reserved), new sources SLA pending (`f8i8-k2gm`), DOB NOW job filings (`w9ak-ipjd`; storefront filter drops 78%: new buildings, 1–3 family, non-GC/sign/PA work), DCWP applications (`ptev-4hud`); DOHMH promoted planned→verified; BBL match 97–99% DOB/DOHMH, 82–84% SLA (no BBL published; PLUTO address / nearest-30 m), DCWP 38% unmatched are out-of-city home-improvement contractors. Stage two (commit 10a33e8): `analysis.storefront_pipeline` at bbl × business_name_key, 135,912 rows (56,571 open / 79,341 not yet), NULL-BBL (24,147) and NULL-name (2,463) rows carried flagged per D75; lead time cross-agency only — fitout_filing → first_inspection strict N=67 median 259 d (p25 178 / p75 378), reconciled N=741 median 221 d; liquor_application → first_inspection N=22 median 73 d; fitout → license_issued N=35 median 183 d; same-agency clocks (DCWP 11 d, SLA pending→active 26 d) excluded because they measure the agency, not the store; reconciliation links 2,613 pairs but 92% are sole-pair-in-BBL so strict is the reference, not the larger reconciled N; ledger gains kind `'gov_filing'` (20,345 locations; censored 108,605 → 91,031) and only `opened_on` may set first-seen, never an application date; `openings_pipeline_400m` / `openings_recent_400m` on address×category as context only (real for restaurant, bar, cafe_bakery, grocery, pharmacy; structurally zero for the other 10 — must never be read as nothing is coming, it is a filing-blind spot); Gowanus core mean restaurant pipeline 0.27 vs East Village 1.78; category mapping is package data (20.6% of filings mapped; DOB descriptions are free text); top brands by government pipeline are wireless carriers, ecoATM, SP+ parking, GameStop, Home Depot, Family Dollar, Staples — i.e. the filings see national retail the press list does not, and vice versa; bugs: `brand_key(NaN)` fixed, `dohmh.classify("Steakhouse")` → `cafe_bakery` pinned as a known bug, not yet fixed (GTM-152). Why this shape: one filing-event staging table plus one pipeline grain so any consumer (ledger, address screen, chains) reads the same lifecycle; lead times reported strict and reconciled side by side so the number cannot outrun its evidence.

**D81 — Site-revenue model v0 shipped: CEX spend pool × fitted capture share, leakage calibrated to Economic Census 2022 county receipts, gated by a leave-one-ZIP-out backtest and a cross-category placebo; restaurant alone passes and now grades C on the recommendation card; nine categories honestly "not modelled".** *(2026-09-13, owner, GTM-150)*
Owner: "would love real data but can we try to predict what a business could make? how could we do that?" then "go ahead, start the build." Anchors verified before designing (Sonnet probe, scripts in the session scratchpad): Economic Census 2022 (api ecnbasic) publishes receipts and establishments by county × 6-digit NAICS — the finest real revenue truth anywhere public (Kings 2022 revenue per establishment: coin laundry $260,818, restaurant $925,896, bar $804,765, café $675,905, convenience $662,354, hair $190,777, nails $182,953, grocery $2,862,292); NAICS 446110 pharmacy is absent from the 2022 vintage at every geography, so EC 2017 ($4,332,756) is used and flagged; CBP/ZBP carry no revenue, and ZIP grain exposes establishments by employment-size band only (the standalone zbp dataset ended in 2018; 2023 ZIP data lives under cbp); CEX gives national spend by income quintile (Table 1101) and a flat NY-metro rebase (Table 3004), no table crosses metro × quintile; NYS DTF taxable sales reports all five boroughs as one row at 4-digit NAICS — unusable for Kings; a circulating $504k/laundromat trade benchmark is inconsistent with its own totals and 2.5× the census figure, so trade benchmarks are not trusted without a census check. Model (data-scientist, Opus; src/loci/model/revenue.py with every equation in the docstring, revenue.yaml spec, revenue_calibration.yaml generated by the gate): spend pool = households within 400/800 m × CEX spend by the tract's income quintile; capture share Huff-style with distance decay β and competition elasticity γ fitted inside every leave-one-ZIP-out fold with the sign free — the textbook negative sign was WRONG for 7 of 10 categories at ZIP grain (incumbents proxy "is this a retail street"), a result, not a bug, disclosed in the YAML with the grid widened after an in-sample ablation; leakage λ calibrated per county so predicted revenue of existing establishments sums exactly to EC receipts (λ_Kings vs λ_NY disagree 1.4–2.6× in every category — no single NYC constant; both carried). Gate: out-of-sample Spearman against CBP employees per establishment must beat a county-average baseline AND a homes-only baseline AND a cross-category placebo (another category's model must not predict this category's size as well as its own); `loci revenue fit` writes the calibration only for passing categories; `loci revenue --boroughs MN,BK` is the idempotent UPDATE of revenue_p25/p50/p75, rent_ceiling (× benchmarks.yaml occupancy ratio), revenue_model_version on analysis.address_category and is the last step of the canonical re-apply order (a no-op for unmodelled categories); recommend.py section 6 grades a modelled category C ("modelled, uncalibrated to local P&Ls"), never higher, so "act" still needs real comps. Result on the MN+BK-only table after D75/D78 (281,842 addresses, supply_hash 767b28674e30, home_rows_citywide stamped so a universe change can never move a calibration invisibly): RESTAURANT alone passes — ρ_oos 0.763, R² 0.577, β 0.50, γ −0.25 (agglomerative, stable in 65 of 73 folds), placebo pass (own 0.80 vs best rival café 0.79); café, grocery, hair, nails and pharmacy beat both baselines but FAIL the placebo — they measure commercial intensity, not category economics — and ship as "not modelled"; laundry, convenience, bar fail the baselines; fitness has 14 ZIPs < 15. Verdicts were identical before and after the rebuild; λ moved (pharmacy Kings −40%, convenience −45% with a sign flip). A σ-band bug was fixed between runs (fold-frequency-weighted RMS, restaurant σ 0.98 → 0.62, halving a spurious 4× band). homes_400m recomputed by this sweep matches D73's stored column on 100% of addresses — two independent sweeps, same answer. Gowanus core (1,831 addresses, median 3,646 homes within 400 m, 14,169 within 800 m): restaurant revenue p25/p50/p75 $1,077,090 / $1,673,663 / $2,657,475 = 1.81× the Kings average, driven by 39 incumbents at γ < 0 — a corridor reading, not an operator forecast; rent ceiling $133,893/yr = $11,158/mo at the generic 8% occupancy ratio vs the one local comp at $7,313/mo (1.53×); docs/recommendations/gowanus-core-2026-09-13.md regenerated with restaurant at C. Caveats the model cannot escape: county anchors blur Park Slope with Gowanus; CEX quintiles are national; the MN+BK-only universe drops real Queens/Bronx households from edge sheds; λ is right by construction and has NO out-of-sample test; the backtest target is establishment size, a revenue proxy only if revenue per employee is flat within category; p25/p75 is a parameter band, not outcome dispersion; a typical operator at a site, nothing about concept quality. 62 tests; suite 884 passed / 1 skipped / 1 lock-induced failure during the concurrent rebuild. Commits efae213 (code; the CLI block and ALTERs were carried in the concurrent session's c9059d8) and d7387dd (calibration + card).

**D82 — Neighborhood character: retail vs corporate vs industrial vs residential.** *(2026-09-13, owner, GTM-154)*
Owner asked to colour neighborhoods by whether they are retail- or corporate-dominated. Built at the address grain (never hex): PLUTO floor areas within 400 m network over ALL lots (`homes_400m`'s residential-only lot set would have called Midtown residential), LODES 2023 jobs split retail-facing (CNS07/17/18/19) vs desk-facing (CNS09–14/20) vs other (sector columns sum exactly to `jobs_400m`), commercial zoning overlays (C1-/C2-, C4–C8) within 100 m, transit AM/PM share as independent corroboration (median corporate 0.31, retail 0.92, industrial 1.08, residential 1.70). First pass (55e2116) passed the CBD sanity list but the urban-planner rejected it: Brighton Beach Ave, Cortelyou, Myrtle Ave, 7th Ave, Graham Ave, Broadway Hamilton Hts, Dyckman, Fulton St, Church Ave, Kings Hwy, 5th Ave Bay Ridge and Pitkin Ave all read ~0 retail_mixed because prewar ground-floor retail is not PLUTO RetailArea and small-shop strips have few payroll jobs; Bay Ridge VA and East Flatbush hospitals read corporate; parks and cemeteries got labels. Revision (1f115d6): zoning-overlay OR-route (≥ 20 commercially zoned lots within 100 m — executive's choice: ≥1 gave BK 52%, floor/share alone 20.5%, ≥20 gives 35%, every corridor flips at every setting, so the threshold buys discrimination not coverage), RETAIL_JOBS_FLOOR 1,000 → 300 and share 0.40 → 0.35, institutional lots (BldgClass I/M/P/W, LandUse 08) excluded from office area and jobs ≥ 5,000 required on both corporate routes, labels suppressed for parks/cemeteries/NTAs < 50 addresses (2,744 addresses; Stuyvesant Town is a casualty), continuous retail_index for the map, character_copy: corporate → "weekday-office catchment" with the caveat that corporate addresses have MORE homes within 400 m (median 5,682 vs 2,284) so daily-needs there are limited by rent, loading and ground-floor supply, not absent customers. Final mix: BK 34.7% retail_mixed / 62.2% residential / 1.9% industrial / 0.2% corporate; MN 58.2 / 25.6 / 0.0 / 15.5. Map (e99c808): single-hue sequential ramp on retail_index with quantile stops, corporate/industrial hatched overlays, suppressed NTAs as not-measured grey, never the pale end of the ramp. Ruling: character is map colour and card context only; it enters no grade. Caveat: LODES codes jobs at the payroll address (Brighton Beach 5,106 median jobs are home-health offices) — jobs_400m is suspect where jobs_other_share is high and homes ordinary. Follow-up ticketed GTM-154: retail_index saturation, the 20-lot threshold against a held-out corridor list, and character copy on the `loci recommend` card.

**D83 — Clusters are ranked by walk-shed density, not by capped units, per the owner's 2026-09-13 ruling.** *(2026-09-13, owner, GTM-155)*
The owner said "rank by density" and confirmed the reading: households per km² inside the walk-shed. What it replaces was never a decision — every cluster list in this project had been sorted by Σ`units_capped` since D39 capped mega-lots, because that was the first plausible weight to hand, and it silently ranked by SIZE, so forty low-rise blocks outranked four towers. The definition is `density_400m` = `homes_400m` ÷ `walkshed_km2_400m` at address grain, both written by the same `loci supply-ratio` sweep off the same Dijkstra rows so numerator and denominator can never be measured over different reachable sets. The walk-shed's area is the convex hull of the graph nodes within 400 m network metres, projected in EPSG:32618 and floored at a 50 m disc: NOT the nominal π·0.4² disc, because dividing by a constant ranks by `homes_400m` under a new name, and convex rather than concave because a concave hull needs an alpha knob that silently moves every area while the convex hull's error runs conservative — it spans water, parks and rail cuts, overstating the shed and understating the density. The measured shed's median is 0.267 km², 0.53× the nominal disc, and the density distribution is MN p10/p50/p90 14,418 / 22,810 / 39,825 against BK 4,218 / 8,444 / 14,020 units per km², carried everywhere with the caveat that this is a PLUTO `UnitsRes` register count with no margin of error and not an ACS household density. A cluster's density is the `units_capped`-weighted MEDIAN of its members' own, never Σhomes/Σarea, because members sit within 200 m of each other so their sheds overlap almost entirely and the pooled ratio counts the same homes and the same land once per member; the unweighted mean ships beside it so skew is visible. The ordering moved almost everything — top-50 Jaccard 0.087 against the old list, borough mix flipping from 32 Brooklyn clusters to 44 Manhattan ones, median cluster size from 912 addresses to 13.5 — while the gap set and `gap_score` are byte-identical by checksum, because this changes an ORDER and nothing else; `--rank-by units` keeps the old order selectable, `loci clusters` lists the ranked table, and the webmap's `meta.rankBy`, `clusters.json` and the NTA index carry the ordering with the caveat. The one open edge is that density ranks a one-address cluster against a 700-address one on equal terms, so nine of the top fifty are single doorways; `loci clusters --min-addresses N` exists for it and the floor is deliberately left unset, as the owner's to choose. Suite 1,044 passed / 1 deliberate skip.

**D84 — The screen now samples TWO frames: the residential tax lot and the street midpoint.** *(2026-09-13, owner, GTM-156)*
The owner's direction was literal — "for addresses, we should be sampling an address near the middle of every known street in the borough" — and the reading confirmed in docs/street_midpoint_frame.md is one scored point per intersection-to-intersection segment, scored by exactly the same engine as an address. The lot frame was built FROM RESIDENTS (D38), so a street nobody lives on yet was not low-scoring, it was absent: the Navy Yard, the Red Hook and East Williamsburg industrial blocks, Spring Creek. The source is NYC Street Centerline `inkn-q76z`, weekly; the keep rule `status='2' AND rw_type=1 AND nonped<>'V' AND at-grade` keeps 32,291 of 41,784 MN+BK segments (3,480.9 km), and the ladder plus a ±10% count guard print on every rebuild so the filter is auditable rather than asserted. L = 100 m — the smallest round value at or above the 81.9 m median block face, so a street point stands for at most one block face, the same spatial unit a PLUTO lot frontage occupies; splitting per geometry PART (not per segment) yields 50,199 points, MN 13,472 / BK 36,727. It is a `frame` column on `analysis.address`, not a sibling table, because a street midpoint is not a new grain (D61): within 50 m of a lot it reproduces that lot's lead 93.1% of the time at a median gap_score difference of 0.000, and a sibling table would fork every UPDATE-only annotation layer into two code paths; `loci street-frame` writes `data/interim/street_points.parquet` and `address-gaps` consumes it, delete-then-inserting BOTH frames in one write under one provenance stamp (`--no-street-frame` reproduces the pre-D84 screen). Clustering is WITHIN frame, ids namespaced `BK:bar:S12`, because clustering the union moved 429 of the 571 existing lot clusters and net-MERGED 12 — a point with zero residents becoming the bridge that makes two markets look like one is a dedup bug wearing a new costume. The non-filtering proof was re-run as D57/D61 ran it: every lot row's gap_score, lead, n_missing, cluster_id and homes_400m are byte-identical by md5 before and after, Σunits is unchanged at 2,074,244, all 571 lot clusters keep their id and membership, and reach×0.8 removes zero pairs in either frame. 4,874 street points (9.7%) have no lot within 100 m and are a different population, not more of the same — median gap_score 2.18 against the lot frame's 1.27, 6.33 missing categories against 1.89, 55% of them led by laundry — and they cluster into 586 street-only clusters, 369 with five or more members. The censored ones need no gate: 28 of those clusters have homes_400m 0 and therefore density 0, so D83's density ranking sorts Randall's Island, Floyd Bennett Field and Governors Island to the bottom for a stated reason rather than letting their `CAP_M/reach(laundry)` = 7.50 top an unfiltered list — reintroducing a gate would have been D75 in reverse. What stays pinned to `frame='lot'`: the supply baseline FIT universe (n unchanged at 281,842 — the apply is not pinned, a street point gets a real ratio against the lot-frame norm), the revenue calibration, leave-one-ZIP-out backtest AND apply (a units-0 point is not an observation of realised receipts; street revenue_* stay NULL, which is this model's word for "not modelled"), `loci recommend`'s area medians, validation/sample.py's income deciles, conveniences' per-address distribution, the webmap's neighbourhood roll-up, and — found during the build — the NTA character suppression denominator and roll-up, which would otherwise have moved every published D82 share with no building having changed. Operational: `transit-profile` now needs `--re-sweep` after any frame change (its own consistency check caught the stale entrance set), and `supply_baseline.yaml`'s `universe` string still reads "all addresses" until the next sanctioned `--fit-baseline` (its stored n = 281,842 already proves the lot universe). Webmap: street dots render hollow behind a default-off toggle, `clusters.json` carries frame (571 lot / 586 street). 33 tests; suite 1,137 passed / 1 deliberate skip.

**D85 — NYC DOT data: bi-annual counts as a source, camera registry, sidewalk counts.** *(2026-09-13, owner, GTM-157)*
Owner: "we need NYC DOT data, both the bi-annual and the camera data." Built: `staging.dot_pedestrian_count` (114 points × 37 rounds 2007-05…2026-05 × am/md/pm = 12,312 rows, exact vs the raw wide file; rounds are irregular — May 2019 only, Oct 2020 only, June 2024 — so trends use decimal years); `validate-pedestrian` now reads the table; registry role validation → control. Top on-street points 2026-05: W 34 St @ Broadway 37,109/round, Eighth Av @ W 44 35,488, Seventh Av @ W 32 34,465; Roosevelt Av @ Main St is the only riser in the top 10 (+121/yr); Midtown's negative 10-year slopes are largely the unrecovered 2020 level. `staging.dot_camera`: webcams.nyctmc.org/api/cameras verified — 969 cameras (MN 376, QN 208, BK 204, SI 100, BX 81), no auth, 352×240 JPEG ~23 KB, near-live (9 fetches 5 s apart → 9 different frames; 0 duplicates in 183 fetches at 10 s), `isOnline` is the string "true" on all 969 so it means published, not live. Per-address context on `analysis.address` (dot_point_id/m, latest am/md/pm, camera_id/m): straight-line EPSG:32618, a lower bound on the walk, never comparable to the network `*_400m` columns; median distance to the nearest count point MN 739 m / BK 1,416 m, to the nearest camera MN 234 m / BK 732 m; within 400 m of a camera MN 79.9% / BK 22.1%; camera density is DOT's arterial geography, not footfall. `loci sidewalk-count sample|schedule|stats|validate`: onnxruntime + YOLO11n (COCO person), `.pt` from ultralytics assets v8.3.0 sha256-pinned, exported to ONNX once (ultralytics publishes no `.onnx`), 0.027 s/frame CPU on the M4; counts only, frames never stored unless `--keep-frames` (`data/frames` gitignored; public imagery of identifiable people stays on the machine); `MAX_FRAMES_PER_RUN=720`; duplicate-frame skip makes a frozen camera one observation. First samples 2026-09-13 18:54–19:05, 61 frames each: 7 Ave @ 9 St pm_peak mean 0.94 / evening 1.12; Grand Army Plaza 1.91 / 1.82; 5 Ave @ 50 St 2.71 / 0.87 — Fifth Avenue busiest at 6 pm and empties after 7. `validate`: 15 cameras within 60 m of 14 count points, bridge midpoints excluded, per-counted-hour footing, N=0 today (Sunday vs weekday counts). Ruling: sidewalk counts are a shortlist-verification instrument (stock, not flow; per-camera field of view uncorrected; compare a camera with itself first), never a screen-wide layer; DOT counts are context and calibration. Why the shape: counts and cameras are new grains (tables); per-address context is columns per D61; the detector runs locally so no image ever leaves the machine.

**Addendum (2026-09-13, commit 089f1fe):** the DOT layer shipped on the webmap — count points sized by latest round with a trend arrow and a COVID-recovery caveat in the popup, and all 969 NYCTMC cameras with per-camera sidewalk-count summaries where sampled, off by default alongside the other context layers. The exported `webmap/data/dot.json` carried bare `NaN` tokens (from rounds with no comparable prior count for a trend) that `JSON.parse` rejects outright, silently breaking the whole map bundle rather than just the trend arrow; fixed by writing `null` for the incomparable case, with a test pinning the export to valid JSON so the failure mode cannot return unnoticed.

**D86 — Paid-data wishlist as registry entries.** *(2026-09-13, owner, GTM-159)*
Owner asked for a list of paid sources to integrate post-raise. Encoded as 35 `status: wishlist` registry entries (config with the code that reads it) with required cost/price_tier/price_note/license_note/closes_gap/priority/evidence/cost_basis, exempt from the $100 budget and the CONTEXT §3 mirror, mirrored instead by generated docs/PAID-SOURCES.md with a byte-identical drift check in `check-sources` (commit 9488a6d). Booked $301,565/yr (tier floors $213,200; verified subset $86,457); P1 $74,300: Advan Patterns Plus, Placer ($8k verified public order), SafeGraph Spend, CompStak, Google Places Insights, Data Axle API, LiveXYZ, Google Places Pro/Aggregate, Esri BA ($5,200 verified). Findings: license terms not price are binding — Dewey/Advan academic-only at any price, Claritas no derivatives, Placer no redistribution, Data Axle/Esri internal-use by default — so riders are a line item, not a rounding error; Google Places Insights holds monthly POI snapshots back to 2024-01 and could un-censor the 47.7% `backfill_censored` rows in the first-seen ledger (D79).
