Two non-purchases encoded as planned: NYC DOT VivaCity sensors (~100 sites incl. residential, announced 2026-06-04 — the ≥100 non-corridor points GTM-147 requires; a data-sharing ask, not a purchase) and Strava Metro (Loci does not qualify outside an agency contract). Why this shape: a wishlist that is not machine-checked rots; the registry already carries cost and bias per entry, so the wishlist is the same object with a status, not a new document.

**D87 — GTM strategy memo.** *(2026-09-13, owner, GTM-158/GTM-159)*
Owner asked for pricing, buyers, competitors, value prop, and whether anyone sells the model or the dataset. Investor-lens draft, then a contrarian red-team — five of seven attacks failed and were accepted — final at docs/GTM.md (commit 0d1012c), mirrored to the LOCI Notion page. Verified: Placer $31k signed public contract (civic band $8–27.5k), Buxton $35k model build + $15k/yr SCOUT, Esri BA $3,908–$8,367 list; Esri Retail MarketPlace discontinued (2017 data on 2010 geography, no replacement) so the industry's canonical retail-gap metric is unreplaced; NYC SBS already sources its 24,900-storefront count from LiveXYZ. Nobody sells address-grain city-wide daily-needs gap logic or the filings lifecycle; nearest substitutes are Placer void analysis (chain-level) and LiveXYZ (census). Rulings: ICP order brokers → lenders/feasibility shops (subcontracted $500–1,500 input into their $4,900 SBA study) → BID/SBS → 3–30-unit operators; chains are logos and the watchlist a lead list; the lead SKU is the $20k/quarter retainer (the only demonstrated willingness to pay); per-seat licence anchored on Reonomy $4,800 / GrowthFactor $2,400; the value prop is cost of search, not failure rates, and no decision value is claimed until the retrodiction test runs (GTM-158, Urgent — QUESTIONS T11). Moat is BBL reconciliation + operator, months not years, not a data monopoly; narrative is "consulting-funded data company"; sub-$1M NYC TAM makes the raise a bet on city #2 (E8). The memo carries the contrarian's three "we will not claim" sentences verbatim, and its pre-spend actions (license riders, a Places Insights trial, the VivaCity ask) are folded into D86's wishlist ticket (GTM-159).

**D91 — Site-revenue model v0.2: pool elasticity chosen by the gate (restaurant ε = 0.6; the unconstrained fit was degenerate at ε = 0), leakage anchored to the CBP size-band median instead of the EC mean, capacity ceiling from PLUTO retail area, site-level competition multiplier capped at 1.0; restaurant skill rises to ρ 0.82 and every level falls 3–5×; the v0.2 number is a floor for above-typical operators.** *(2026-09-14, owner, GTM-160)*

Owner, after the v0 model (D81) overshot Lion's Milk at 104 Roebling: "why would we send him something we know is wrong" → "build the fix. unless there is a difference in avg price, I wouldn't expect more than a modest difference between different cafés." Causes of the overshoot, ranked and quantified: revenue scaling linearly with the local spend pool (elasticity 1 by construction; the "scaled to your block" row multiplied the borough average by 2.6); λ anchored to the Economic Census MEAN receipts, which large operators pull up (Kings cafés mean $675,905 vs CBP band median $575,589; restaurants $925,896 vs $262,924); no capacity term; survivorship in the anchor; the ZIP-level agglomeration sign (γ < 0) applied at an address, turning 28 neighbouring cafés into a ×6.7 multiplier. Built (data-scientist, Opus; src/loci/model/revenue.py v0.2, v0 reproducible behind model_version; sql/025_revenue_capacity.sql): (1) ε per category on a 0–1 grid refit inside every leave-one-ZIP-out fold jointly with β, γ and a price-index exponent δ — the unconstrained argmax was DEGENERATE, ε = 0 in seven of nine categories, deleting the spend pool and leaving λ·(incumbent density)^−γ, i.e. retail predicting retail, the rejected D1 thesis; the cross-category placebo caught it (every category's model predicted every other's employment equally well). So ε is chosen by the gate: refit at each ε, ship the smallest that passes, floored at 0.3 (the owner's range and published elasticities). Restaurant passes at 0.6/0.7/0.8, ships 0.6 (placebo margin crosses zero between 0.5 and 0.6) — the backtest refuses the owner's 0.3–0.5. δ adds no skill for restaurant (ships 0); a little for bar, convenience, grocery, nails, pharmacy, which still fail. (2) λ calibrated so the predicted county MEDIAN matches the CBP size-band median × EC revenue per employee; λ_median/λ_mean restaurant 0.31 Kings / 0.77 NY. (3) revenue_cap_p50 = PLUTO retailarea (÷ the Storefront Registry count when a lot files several storefronts) × a per-category $/sq ft band; p50 = min(model, cap); capacity_bound flag; binds on 62% of MN lot rows, 1% Gowanus core, 0.2% BK. (4) γ stays free in the fit (its ranking skill) but the site-level multiplier is capped at 1.0 — neighbours may not raise a site above corridor-neutral; the ZIP-level sign does not transfer to an address. Two bugs fixed on the way: λ had been fitted on the uncapped share and applied to the capped one (Manhattan median restaurant $130k against a $1.67M anchor); numpy nan**0 == 1 gave the ε = 0 candidate a larger evaluation sample than every other. Result: gate unchanged — restaurant alone passes (ρ_oos 0.763 → 0.819, R² 0.68, beats county-average 0.573 and homes-only 0.629, placebo own 0.823 vs bar 0.818); nine categories fail in both versions. Levels: 104 Roebling restaurant p50 $2,494,887 → $494,500 (cap $700,000, not bound); 376 Graham (El Punto Cubano Express, confirmed by the owner's contact as the former Lion's Milk owner's new restaurant, opened ~2026-07) $1,282,676 → $369,429 (688 sq ft demise); Gowanus core restaurant $1,673,663 → $453,650, rent ceiling $11,158 → $3,024/mo, grade stays C. Decomposition: the median anchor is the largest correction (0.41× alone); capacity 0.28×/0.38× alone but binds neither p50 once the others land; ε 0.80×/0.97×; γ cap 0.81×/0.97× net. Standing caveat, now on the owner-facing pages: the median anchor describes a two-to-three-person shop, so for a 1,000 sq ft café running 62 hours with a full crew the v0.2 number is a FLOOR, not an estimate; the size term the model lacks is headcount, which only an operator's answer supplies. Pre-registration record for Lion's Milk (docs/recommendations/predictions/lions-milk-2026-09-13.md) now holds, all timestamped before any figure from the owner: raw v0 model $1.29M–$2.42M; judgment $650k–$1.25M central $900k; bottom-up menu × daypart traffic $1.35M–$1.69M (traffic is the exposure; the menu has no breakfast plates); cost-side read $933k–$1.04M (break-even $739k, $906k with a $90k owner draw; rent the least sensitive line; tip credit worth $62k–$74k); rent $8k–$12k/mo; v0.2 model $400k–$574k; owner evidence: "made more money during COVID" (consistent with a labour-fixed-cost-bound café). 376 Graham memo: block side-by-side with Roebling (same 4,000 homes, $142k vs $213k income, commuter-origin stop with a resident evening, weekends weak), levers ranked (resident evening, delivery to the 800 m ring, beer-and-wine not a bar, no café line, small fixed base), pre-registered bottom-up $871k–$1.16M settled / $653k–$867k ramp, rent $3.5k–$6.5k. Two owner-facing artifact pages published (Lion's Milk, El Punto). Suite 1,221 passed / 1 skipped; commit 3d2a936. Dedup defect ticketed as GTM-153 (a café with a DOHMH permit counted twice; category-assignment rule before per-category dedup).

**D88 — Retrodiction: the screen ranks retail streets; no decision value claimed.** *(2026-09-14, owner, GTM-158)*

Owner: "run the retrodiction." Design and results exactly as in docs/retrodiction-2026-09.md and the statistician review: cohort 12,572 dated openings 2023–24 MN+BK (81.5% food; dates are record-creation/licence dates, D80 lead 221–259 d); business-level survival NOT identified (every source is a current-state extract; Foursquare fetched open-only; pipeline is_open ⇔ ever opened; KM/Cox refused against a 48-event floor); entry retrodiction — screen frozen 2023-01-01 from the first-seen ledger, 12,000 lot addresses × 6 categories, logit with category FE, NTA-clustered SEs, NTA-blocked folds: AUC 0.866 [0.851, 0.881] vs 0.854 for the same model without the score (the honest lift +0.013; the +0.048 vs homes-only was a straw man the permutation null itself exposed), spatially structured placebo p95 0.8545, CD-blocked +0.014, calibration monotone; sign: log(1+supply_ratio_t0) +1.54 pooled, positive in every category, +1.17 [0.87, 1.47] after NTA fixed effects and other-category supply — own-category thickness predicts own-category entry within a neighborhood net of general density, i.e. agglomeration or herding, not unmet demand; restaurant own-category-gap −0.89 [−1.44, −0.33] survives Bonferroni (no restaurant within 400 m in 2023 → less likely to get one); LL157 go-dark (premises occupied 2022-12-31 → vacant 2024-12-31; strict n=12,713 / 1,074 events; attrition-as-event 16,163 / 4,524): AUC 0.549 vs 0.535, 0.544 vs 0.539; the score's sign flips with the outcome definition (+0.42 / −0.20) — a null; Foursquare re-pull without the open-only filter (b340d91): 228,455 NYC closures, 13,643 ledger rows gain closed_on, cohort 109 events, implied S(24) 0.994 vs a true ~0.75–0.80 → ~3% ascertainment, categorically non-random, excluded from the hazard gate; 54,190 pre-ledger closures are a future panel. Ruling — record the statistician's sentence verbatim: "The frozen 2023-01-01 screen predicts where 2023-24 openings landed out of sample (NTA-blocked AUC 0.866 vs 0.854 for the same model without the score, and above a spatially structured placebo at p95 0.8545), with a positive supply coefficient that survives NTA fixed effects and conditioning on other-category density (+1.17 [0.87, 1.47]) — so the screen ranks retail streets, not unserved demand, and Loci may claim cost of search only; decision value remains unclaimed because the one survival-adjacent outcome we can test returns a null and the business-level outcome remains untested rather than absent." Consequence: docs/GTM.md's value proposition stays cost-of-search; the recommend card's economics grades are unaffected; D1's reverse-causality worry is now measured, not assumed. Why the shape: the survival test was refused rather than printed because a 100%-survivor cohort would have produced S(24)=1.000 — the extract, not New York.

**D89 — Recommendations ledger: track time-to-fill and fit.** *(2026-09-14, owner, GTM-162)*

Owner: "start to track recommendations so that we can see how long it takes for the free market to fill those gaps and if they do it well (with our proposed solution)." Built (1c659a5, sql/026): analysis.recommendation (append-only by code invariant, card_hash idempotency; issued_on, anchor, category, proposed_solution, format_hint, grade, supply ratio / homes / gap score at issue, status open|filled|withdrawn|expired), analysis.recommendation_outcome per monthly snapshot (match kind opened|in_pipeline|none from the first-seen ledger and the filings pipeline, same category, 400 m straight-line, first-seen ≥ issued_on; days_to_fill; solution_match_score under a documented rubric — category 0.50 required, format 0.20, corroboration 0.15, independent 0.10, still_open 0.05; unavailable ≠ zero), views recommendation_latest and category summary (share still open at 12 months NULL until exposed); `loci recommend --record`, `loci recommendations backfill|check|report`; make chains-refresh ends with the check (order load-bearing). Backfilled honest history: D73 laundry lead issued 2026-09-10, withdrawn 2026-09-11 (0.94×); D74 Gowanus card as issued 2026-09-11 (13 D / 2 C — hardware and tailor C; restaurant recorded D because its C came after D81 and is not back-dated); the three D73 thin leads with solution and format hint. First check 2026-09: 15 rows all 'none' — the instrument, not the market (ledger left-censored before 2026-10; pipeline entries pre-dating issue excluded as the anti-reverse-causality choice). Owner decisions pending (GTM-162): radius, expiry, re-issue policy, rubric weights, pre-existing build-outs as context. Why the shape: a recommendation is a new grain (table), outcomes are monthly observations that persist ('none' is stored, not skipped), and honesty requires the first rows to be the cards actually issued.

**D90 — GTM-48 coverage validation on the address frame: P3 survives at 7.6% [6.5–8.8], no income gradient, and fitness's headline hole rate was a Google type-map defect — owner rules on all three follow-ups plus cross-category dedup.** *(2026-09-14, owner, GTM-48/GTM-112/GTM-173/GTM-174/GTM-175)*

GTM-48 ran on the address frame (docs/coverage-validation-2026-09.md is the source of truth for every number here — read it, cite it): 5,633 address×category rows (5,522 planned + 111 kept-and-flagged from a killed first attempt), seed 20260902, 20 per stratum, 649 m radius (D53), 14 mapped categories (clinic stays unmapped, D30); 5,985 calls ≈ $190, ledger 8,963/9,113 after the run (budget raised in `.env` from 5,000 to cover it). Design-weighted, respecting the 137 sampling strata: **7.6% [6.5–8.8] of MN+BK MISSING flags are coverage holes (~40k of 523k)** — the naive equal-weight pooled estimate of 10.5% was wrong, an artifact of pooling rows across strata of very different size, and the statistician's review (Opus) caught it along with two other defects in the first pass: row-level rather than stratum-level inference, and the wrong Google-type width for fitness. Income gradient is **not** distinguishable from zero once errors respect the 137 strata (stratum-clustered p=0.087, tract-clustered p=0.104, permutation p=0.141); only hardware shows a category-level income contrast surviving Benjamini-Hochberg correction. Holes track anchor absence, not income: anchored categories sit at ≤2%; tailor_repair 37.5% (a floor — no anchor exists for it); hair_barber 34.5% without beauty_salon in scope (44.5% with it); hardware 12.0% (a floor). **Fitness's apparent 35.6% hole rate was entirely sports_club and marina Google returns (yacht clubs, tennis clubs) — 0 of 180 sampled gym hits — a Google type-map defect inherited from D50/M6, not missing supply**; fitness is restored to the other anchored categories' range once GOOGLE_TYPES drops those two types.

Owner ruled 2026-09-14, "yes to all 4": **(1)** demote tailor_repair and hair_barber from headline coverage claims on the D30 precedent (the same treatment clinic got when its Google type could not validate it) — RULED, not pending; **(2)** adopt the numeric G9 Wilson-upper coverage-grade ladder — Wilson upper bound ≤10% → A, ≤25% → B, else → C — which on these rates grades grocery and hardware B and hair_barber/tailor_repair/clinic C, everything else A (QUESTIONS D22, GTM-174) — RULED; **(3)** remove sports_club and marina from fitness's GOOGLE_TYPES, validator-only — it changes what the validator counts as a fitness hit, not loci's own fitness category or its POI adapters (QUESTIONS M14, GTM-173) — RULED; **(4)** run cross-category name+distance dedup before the existing per-category dedup, GTM-153 proper — the fix for the Lion's Milk defect flagged inline in D91 (a DOHMH restaurant and an Overture cafe_bakery listing 11 m apart, both open, both counted) (QUESTIONS M15, GTM-175) — RULED; implementation of all four and the resulting supply re-fit/re-run are recorded in a later session-log entry, not this one. **Two items remain genuinely open, not part of this ruling:** whether unresolved co-located POI pairs (2,527 groups / 7,597 POIs; collapsing would remove 4,270 more; default OFF) should collapse or count as-is (QUESTIONS D24), and the three `categories.yaml` anchor-vs-adapter contradictions from Next-actions item 21 (bank NAICS 522110 vs credit unions; hardware 444140 vs home centers; clinic) — these concern the ZBP anchor measure, not the Google calls, and stay unresolved.

Also found and ticketed: the validator's `est_cost` printout ignores calls already spent on the ledger — it printed an estimated $16.70 for this run when the true incremental cost, given the ledger's prior state, was closer to $177, a regression of the same class of bug GTM-127 fixed once already for a different validator estimate (GTM-171).

Also this session: **GTM-112's category-expansion fail-closed checklist shipped** (commit 766d9a2, docs/CATEGORY-EXPANSION.md — 12 gates with cited thresholds, a dentist dry-run — plus tests/test_category_registry.py). Its findings are folded in here because they bear on the same coverage-grade question: G9 had no numeric threshold before ruling (2) above; clinic can never reach coverage grade A because it has no Google type (D30); `loci check-categories` does not exist yet; the `supply.py` anchor-fallback failing OPEN (rather than closed) on a missing anchor is deliberate, and the checklist records the refusal as a requirement rather than an oversight.

Why the shape: this is the P3 validation the charter's acceptance criteria gate on (CONTEXT.md §1.4) — it had to be re-done once the statistician found the pooling and inference defects, and the ruling on its three follow-ups plus the independently-flagged dedup defect belonged in one sitting rather than three.

- Statistician review (scratchpad statistician_retrodiction.md): corrected the headline comparator from homes-only (+0.048) to the honest no-score refit (+0.0126); added NTA-FE and other-category-supply conditioning (+1.17 [0.87, 1.47]); flagged the LL157 rejection as wrong and specified the go-dark test that unlocked a real (if null) survival-adjacent outcome.
- Recommendations ledger (D89, commit 1c659a5): analysis.recommendation + recommendation_outcome shipped with the monthly fill-check rubric; first check all 'none' by construction (left-censored before 2026-10).
- Linear: GTM-158 moved to Done with the verdict comment; GTM-161 (survival ascertainment) and GTM-162 (recommendations owner decisions) opened, both E2 · Access Engine.
- Coordination: three peer sessions active this window — D90 reserved by abenmayor-cf (GTM-157 camera sampling), D91 shipped by abenmayor-84 (site-revenue model v0.2).

**D92 — Four layers: modeled, realized, scored, learned — and the forecast ledger.** *(2026-09-14, owner, GTM-163)*

Owner framing: "predicted/modeled (not what the world reflects but what it could) vs realized/actual." Ruling: the warehouse and the docs name four layers — MODELED (the screen: gap score, supply ratio, demand proxies, character, recommendations, forecasts), REALIZED (the first-seen ledger, closures, filings lifecycle, chain snapshots, DOT counts, camera frames), SCORED (retrodiction, recommendation fill checks, forecast outcomes — where modeled meets realized on a schedule), LEARNED (FINDINGS.md, carrying capacity, portability, planner verdicts — what holds, what travels). Built (e578470): analysis.forecast / forecast_run / forecast_outcome + views (sql/028), model 0.1.0+f7d190df, vintages 2023-01 and 2026-09 (4,227,630 rows each), 2023-01 scored at 12 months AUC 0.899 vs 0.860 no-score, Brier 0.095, pooled calibration gap 0.12 hiding per-category level errors; own_gap_flag −0.71 [−0.98, −0.45]; no NTA surprise survives Bonferroni; the map's Off/Modeled/Realized/Surprise control; `make chains-refresh` issues and scores monthly; the ledger forecasts ENTRY, never viability.

Also record the legality-vs-herding test as the D88 addendum (from the statistician memo): +1.18 [0.88, 1.49] after NTA FE + other-category supply + present-day legal-capacity controls; +1.40 [1.05, 1.75] inside the legal set; legality sets the level (16% vs 72%) but absorbs 0.0002 of the +0.0126 lift while other-category supply absorbs ~48% — reading: "the screen ranks sites inside the legal retail set; it does not merely rediscover where retail is legal, and it still measures co-location, never unmet demand"; caveats: retail_area_400m partly post-treatment, legality variables present-day PLUTO; docs/GTM.md updated (069f0f4).

Why: naming the layers makes the scoreboard the product's compounding asset and keeps "what could be" from being sold as "what is".

**D93 — The learned layer: findings register, carrying capacity, portability audit, planner review.** *(2026-09-14, owner, GTM-164)*

Owner: "backtesting and improvement of the model as well as research; what did the model learn; what can other cities learn from NYC; how many small businesses can a certain amount of people support; density vs revenue; run things by urban planners; critical inputs to expand to new cities." Built: docs/FINDINGS.md (a9efca7; 14 findings with decision ids, grades, portability, overturn conditions; five most portable: agglomeration sign, legality-vs-herding decomposition, filings lifecycle ordering, ledger design, honesty-grade architecture; two most likely overturned: the +0.0126 entry lift, restaurant revenue v0.2); carrying capacity (`loci capacity`, docs/carrying-capacity-2026-09.md): gated fits — only grocery (flattens ~16,104 residents in 400 m) and convenience saturate, seven accelerate (open shed), six proportional; per-1k-residents at p50 density: restaurant 3.85, grocery 1.68, hair 1.39, nails 1.00; CBP-vs-POI ratio per category (clinic 0.94× … fitness 7.42×) is the portability parameter; NYC vs 107 metros with NYC held out: tailor 11.3×, laundry 3.4×, nails 2.2× above, restaurant/cafe/clinic on the curve, bar 0.60× below; nearest metros by dense population LA, Chicago, Philadelphia; D70 (growth) and this (levels) agree on 2/15 — different questions; D91's capacity ceiling binds 62% of Manhattan vs 7% of MN+BK; portability audit (`loci gen-portability`, docs/PORTABILITY.md drift-checked; registry portability/feeds/degrades_to on all 43 sources): universal 7 / national 8 / state 6 / city_open_data 15 / city_unique 6; minimum input set per grade — "act" needs a paid economics input in every city including NYC; the load-bearing portable input is a permit STATUS date; Chicago/LA/Philadelphia reach D today, C at best, none has a storefront registry; every calibrated constant is NYC-fitted; planner review (df5383d): protocol + three packets, verdicts enter QUESTIONS.md and the decision log; Notion mirrors for FINDINGS.md and the packets.

Why: research outputs that are not registered with grades and overturn conditions rot; the register is the learned layer's ledger.

**D94 — Co-located POIs and the closure gate.** *(2026-09-14, owner rule: "any time we have 2 businesses in the same address, we should do a check if one of them closed down"; owner then ruled "keep the closure gate on and re-fit the baseline"; commit a67f03e)*

Built: a tri-state predicate `poi_is_open` (open|closed|unknown) in `src/loci/model/poi_presence.py` — closed only on a source-published closure (Foursquare `closed_on` via `analysis.poi_first_seen`; DCWP `active_basis` out_of_business/unable_to_locate/no_evidence_of_activity; DOHMH closed_at_last_inspection; SLA/DOS licence expiry < today), open only on a published active status fresher than 731 days, else unknown; D79 preserved throughout (absence is never a closure; `last_inspection_date` is never a first-seen date; ledger rows are never deleted). `analysis.poi_colocation` (sql/029, a view not a table) groups same-category exact-coordinate POIs — `staging.poi` carries no `address_id`, so coordinate stands in for the building geocode. Gate wired into `score/supply.py`: `GATE_CLOSED=True` removes evidenced-closed POIs from the canonical supply set; `COLLAPSE_UNRESOLVED=False` — unresolved same-coordinate pairs count as-is and are flagged, since two restaurants in one large building can legitimately share a coordinate. `loci colocation` report added at cli.py EOF; 47 tests.

Real data (all boroughs, principled set): 140,235 canonical POIs → 2,982 removed (2.13%) — laundry 1,020, restaurant 635, fitness 391, grocery 272, hair 170, bank 118, bar 90, cafe 84, hardware 50, nails 48, clinic 40, childcare 32, pharmacy 19, convenience 9, tailor 4. Same-category coordinate groups: 4,530 (12,497 POIs, 8.91% of canonical) — 506 one_closed, 34 all_closed, 1,463 both_open, 2,527 unresolved (7,597 POIs); collapsing the unresolved groups would remove a further 4,270 (3.11%) but was NOT applied (QUESTIONS D24 stays open). Restaurant groups: 2,257, 52% resolved, half by a published DOHMH verdict.

Two named cases the gate honestly does not catch: Okozushi at 376 Graham (DOHMH last inspected 384 days ago, no published closure, so "open" under the rule — needs a fourth "stale" state, GTM-176) and the Lion's Milk double count (DOHMH restaurant vs Overture cafe_bakery, 11 m apart, different categories, so outside same-category colocation entirely — fixed downstream by D101, not by this gate).

DCWP verification (why laundry stays in, not a licence-lapse artifact): every laundry POI comes from the DCWP INSPECTIONS feed (jzhd-m6uv) — the licence roster contributes zero laundries, since its 6 "Laundries" licences expired 2023-12 — so Out of Business (1,289) / Unable to Locate (12) / No Evidence of Activity (27) is an inspector's field verdict, not a licence lapse; aggregator twins are uninformative for laundromats (0.6% of closed carry a twin vs 1.7% of open, versus 30% for closed DOHMH restaurants); the storefront registry has no laundry activity code; median 530 days since verdict with no re-inspection. Ruling: keep.

Baseline re-fit: supply_hash 767b28674e30 → 9a11a2f5b9e7 (n 281,842 unchanged); every baseline median moved under 1%; the supply-ratio apply touched no screen column. Screen re-run #1 (canonical order incl. character, openings, dot-context): identity exact (Σn_missing 645,015 → 656,387), gap addresses 224,993 → 227,892, laundry-led gaps +6,261 (52,064), bar −1,145, cafe −997, convenience −857; lead_censored unchanged (BK 14,245 / MN 705); top-50 clusters Jaccard 0.30 vs before, driven by laundry-led clusters entering; suite 1,520 passed. age-fit apply refused the stale curve (fitted on 767b2867) — refit deferred to re-run #2 (D101).

Why: co-located POIs are usually succession or a stale record, not two live supply points; counting both inflates supply and hides gaps; the fail-safe is the tri-state predicate so a missing measurement never becomes a fabricated closure.

**D95 — Charter: the four layers.** *(2026-09-14, owner)*

The wording naming the four layers (MODELED / REALIZED / SCORED / LEARNED) was added to
CONTEXT.md §4 (subsection 4.7) at the owner's approval. Why: it makes every future claim
declare whether it is modeled, realized, scored or learned, so "what could be" is never sold
as "what is" (D87/D88).

**D96 — Forecast re-issue on the gated supply; supply hash in the model version; retention rule.** *(2026-09-14, owner ruling: "keep the closure gate on and re-fit the baseline"; commit ce68d53, GTM-163)*

Consequence of D94's ruling for the forecast ledger: `model_version` now hashes `supply_hash` + the closure-gate flag alongside the existing feature list/fit-window/horizon/radius/support-floor terms (0.1.1), so a supply re-fit changes the version by construction even when the model form itself did not change; the supply hash a run was fit on is stamped onto `analysis.forecast_run.supply_hash`. `loci forecast issue` refuses to reuse an existing `(month, version)` without `--force`, printing the supply hash first. `loci forecast prune [--keep-vintages 3]` deletes prediction rows only — the newest 3 vintages per `model_version` are kept, never a vintage with an open horizon or no scored outcome, never `forecast_run`/`forecast_outcome` (the ledger itself); dry run by default, real deletion only with `LOCI_FORECAST_PRUNE_REAL=1`. Verified empirically against DuckDB 1.5: neither `CHECKPOINT` nor `VACUUM` shrinks the `.duckdb` file on disk — only an offline rebuild would (out of scope for a routine prune). Full design: docs/chains-process.md "The supply-set identity is part of the version" and "Retention".

Re-issued the 2026-09 vintage as `0.1.1+3cd0e269` on supply `a7c531ebca17` at 22:47, after screen re-run #2 (D101): 4,227,630 rows, `ships=true`, scored nothing new (horizon still open). `webmap/data/forecast.json` re-exported from it (commit ef9517e).

Discrepancy found after issuing: one further `poi_status` change (Windclimb, unknown→closed, the D98 closure evidence) landed after re-run #2 was declared final and moved the live supply hash to `467cd5969200`, so the vintage just issued is already one hash behind. Re-run #3 onto that hash finished at ~23:10 (supply_hash `467cd5969200` on every row and in the baseline); a peer began re-issuing the 2026-09 vintage on it at close of this session — **re-issue running at close, result to be appended** (as of this write, `analysis.forecast_run` for 2026-09 still shows only `0.1.1+3cd0e269` on `a7c531ebca17`; the next session should record the new `model_version` once it lands, and mark GTM-181 Done). Group rule adopted (also recorded in D101): a "final" signal freezes `poi_status` writes too, the same way an in-progress re-run freezes the supply hash — this is the D94/D101 failure mode repeating one layer up.

Also recorded here — one definition of "still open" (commit e695d52): retrodiction's cohort closure events, the recommendations rubric's `still_open` component, and the webmap's realized-closure layer all now read `poi_is_open` (D94's tri-state predicate) instead of raw `closed_on`/`last_seen_month` logic; an adoption test greps the three modules for any remaining raw logic. Cohort closure events move 109 → 190 under the predicate (Foursquare `closed_on` + DCWP `out_of_business` + DOHMH status + lapsed SLA/DOS), still ~5% of true closures (D88 stands); `unknown` is never an event and never a zero in any of the three readers.

Addendum (abenmayor-cf, 2026-09-15): the 2026-09 vintage was re-issued on the final supply — analysis.forecast_run model_version 0.1.1+f1cb6628, supply_hash 467cd5969200, issued 2026-09-14 23:27, 4,227,630 rows (the 0.1.1+3cd0e269 vintage on a7c531ebca17 stays as history); forecast score, forecast-export and citibike export ran with it (map data commit 5bbb39e). After abenmayor-84's revenue v0.2 re-apply on 467cd5969200, `loci export-webmap` re-exported the map: 148 files, 121.3 MB, ageFit curves and screen supply both stamped 467cd5969200 (commit 4b6841f). GTM-181 done.

**D97 — Commercial legality is a label, never a gate.** *(2026-09-14, owner)*

Decided: analysis.address gains PLUTO columns zonedist1, overlay1, overlay2, landuse, ownertype, histdist, landmark (extending the address grain, no new table) and a derived legality verdict: commercial | grandfathered | ineligible. Ineligible = (R district with no C1/C2 overlay, OR park/open-space land use, OR institutional owner type) AND no open commercial POI at the address. An open commercial POI makes the address grandfathered and eligible. Ineligible addresses stay in the universe and stay scored (D75 holds); they are excluded from the recommendation ledger and top-N lists, and gap markers render greyed with a "not commercially zoned" tag. Historic district and landmark are card labels only and never exclude (D82 holds).

Why: a friend of the owner saw gap markers on Washington Square North rowhouses and MacDougal Alley (R6, no overlay) where no as-of-right commerce exists; investigation showed the map draws gap markers with no legality check at all, that East 8th St markers nearby are legitimately C1-7, that NYU parcels are institutional rather than illegal, and that the Greenwich Village Historic District restricts facades, not use, so landmarks are the wrong exclusion signal. The open-POI clause exists because much of the Village and brownstone Brooklyn trades as legal non-conforming use in R districts.

**D98 — Closure evidence sources and precedence.** *(2026-09-14, owner)*

Decided: two new positive-evidence closure sources join poi_status: Google Places business_status CLOSED_PERMANENTLY (via the existing budget-capped proxy) and first-party or news web evidence. Every evidence row stores verdict, url, source name, evidence date, retrieval time and query; verdicts auto-apply with that provenance. Between conflicting sources the newest evidence date wins. Checks run on demand for every "unknown" POI in a generated report's supply set, inside that report's cap, and via a budgeted `loci verify-closures` batch command; never citywide unbudgeted. The webmap export must read the closure-gated supply view.

Why: Windclimb at 74 Richardson St (a soup café, not a climbing gym) is carried only by Overture and Foursquare, so the tri-state predicate correctly returns "unknown" and cannot close it, yet a first-party web post states the restaurant was sold (now D's Grab and Go). D79 (absence is never evidence) is preserved because these are presence-of-evidence sources. The export finding matters because webmap_export.py still reads the ungated analysis.poi_supply, so even a "closed" verdict would not remove a marker today.

**D99 — Address search uses NYC GeoSearch snapped to Loci.** *(2026-09-14, owner)*

Decided: the webmap gets a search box backed by NYC Planning Labs GeoSearch (free, keyless, returns BBL); results snap to the nearest Loci address row within 50 m and open its card; out-of-universe addresses are refused with "not in Loci coverage" and get no report.

Why: keyless and city-official beats Google autocomplete's per-session cost and vendor tie; snapping guarantees search lands only on scored data; refusing out-of-universe addresses avoids degraded half-reports.

**D100 — On-demand allocator report.** *(2026-09-14, owner)*

Decided: `loci report <address>` and a "Generate report" button on the address card produce, asynchronously, a markdown memo under docs/recommendations/ rendered in a map side panel. Reader is a capital allocator. Four mandatory sections: category call + confidence with falsification test; supply with open/closed/unknown status and basis; demand + catchment economics; legality, web-sourced rents/leases, risk, exit. Sources are the warehouse, Google Places and web search; prose is one Claude API call against a fixed outline, with no owner edit step. Hard cap default $1.00 per address enforced in code before any paid call, 30-day cache, --dry-run and --no-cache flags, spend ledger. Acceptance is the investor agent reviewing three reports plus call-counting tests proving cap and cache.

Why: the El Punto page proved the format's value but was hand-written from scratchpad scripts and not reproducible; an allocator reader needs the trade, risk and exit, not a data card; the cap and cache follow the standing "enforce spend budgets in code" rule.

**D101 — Cross-category name+distance dedup.** *(2026-09-14/15, owner ruling 4 "yes to all 4"; category-precedence ruling "go with B"; commit 13f0fec, sql/032, 19 tests)*

`score/dedup.py` is now ONE global union-find. Cross-source pairs of different category merge iff their distinctive name cores are equal and non-empty and NOT (both carry trade words whose sets are disjoint), within MATCH_METERS 40 m; same-source cross-category merging stays off by default (CROSS_CATEGORY_SAME_SOURCE=False; 1,741 such pairs are the DOS booth pattern). Audit: the loose names_match rule measured 0.870 precision [0.790, 0.922] on 100 hand-labelled merges (~2,340 wrong of 18,023 candidates); candidates were scored and the shipped rule measured 0.970 [0.915, 0.990] on a fresh 100 (~390 wrong of 12,928), recall ~0.84 — precision bought with recall deliberately. A 25 m cap was tested and rejected (true merges median 15.8 m, 28% beyond 20 m; the cap lowered precision). Merges: 12,928; clusters 227,548 → 219,133 (−3.7%).

CATEGORY_PRECEDENCE = "finer_food" (owner: DOHMH "restaurant" is a permit class, not a retail category; where the anchor says restaurant and an aggregator member says cafe_bakery or bar, the finer category wins, with DOHMH counted as corroboration, not overridden): canonical counts move restaurant 84,065 → 79,028, cafe_bakery 19,344 → 19,150, bar 12,516 → 12,294, hair 15,601 → 13,714, nails 24,009 → 23,905, grocery 15,504 → 14,998, convenience 7,891 → 7,650; relabels restaurant→bar 4,209, restaurant→cafe 1,981, hair→nails 2,365, grocery→convenience 459. Variant A (anchor-wins) was measured and rejected: bar→restaurant 4,986, cafe→restaurant 1,687. sql/032 redefines `analysis.poi_supply` to read the cluster's category (d.category) and join `category_anchor` on it; `poi_supply_status`/`poi_colocation` follow.

Ledger keys (category is part of the key): under variant B, 12,011 keys gone, 3,596 minted (3,514 same-canonical relabels + 82 canonical changes from transitive bridges); abenmayor-88 ran the key migration (sql/035, commit 00eeca3) before the snapshot — 8,134 merged groups, 0 fake openings, 0 vanished-without-map, ledger 219,133 = clusters (recorded fully in their D103). Lion's Milk is now one cluster (62729), category cafe_bakery, with the DOHMH row canonical — the double count named in D94 is fixed. Consequence worth a look: cafe_bakery now QUALIFIES as an anchored category (has DOHMH members, coverage 1.507), so its principled set is now registry-backed (gated cafe 8,932 of 19,150) — flagged as a QUESTIONS entry, not decided.

Screen re-run #2 (after `loci dedup`, the key migration, a poi-snapshot by 88, `supply-ratio --fit-baseline`, canonical order, `age-fit fit` + apply): supply_hash 9a11a2f5b9e7 → a7c531ebca17; baseline medians vs gate-only: bar +13.5%, cafe +17.9%, nails +5.2%, hair −15.3%, restaurant −14.2%; identity exact (643,670); gap addresses 227,892 → 223,107; Σgap_score 497,559 → 485,856 (−2.35%); lead-gap deltas: bar −7,327, cafe −987, convenience −428, laundry +2,716, restaurant +205, hair_barber lead 0 → 158; lead_censored BK 14,422 / MN 720; age-fit refit: bar pass (dispersion 3.61×), childcare pass (3.25×), pharmacy refused (F2b CI [−0.441, +0.750]); applied to 281,840 lot rows; all UPDATE-only layers ran full; top-50 Jaccard 0.28 vs gate-only (cluster ids renumber per run — caveat).

Incident: the first attempt of re-run #2 halted at age-fit apply because the live supply hash moved mid-run (7132ff7c1d57 → a7c531ebca17) — abenmayor-29's `verify-closures` (28 Google Places calls, $0.90, 112 evidence rows on 28 POIs, 2 closed verdicts) changed poi_status at 22:08; the hash guard worked as designed. Re-run restarted with a hash check at three points, all a7c531ebca17. Structural finding: `db.init_schema` applies every `*.sql` on disk in sorted order INCLUDING uncommitted files, so a peer's uncommitted migration goes live on any session's next write connection — abenmayor-88 will propose a HEAD-tracked-only guard as an owner decision (recorded as a QUESTIONS entry, not decided here; ticketed GTM-179). Rule adopted by the four sessions active this window: any write that can change poi_status or the supply set must be announced first, because it moves the supply hash under a running re-run.

Second hash move, same night: abenmayor-29's Windclimb verdict (the closure evidence named in D98 — the 74 Richardson St soup café carried only by Overture/Foursquare, resolved by a first-party web post) landed AFTER re-run #2 closed out, moving the live supply hash again to a7c531ebca17 → 467cd5969200. A third canonical re-run onto 467cd5969200 is in progress; its numbers (identity check, gap-address delta, lead-gap deltas, age-fit refit) will follow in a small follow-up edit to this entry once it finishes, not a new decision id. Group rule extended after this second move: a "final" signal (an evidence verdict marked final, not just recorded) freezes `poi_status` writes too, the same way an in-progress re-run freezes the supply hash — a verdict landing mid-re-run is exactly the D94/D101 failure mode repeating one layer up, this time on closure evidence rather than dedup.

Why: DOHMH's "restaurant" permit class was silently absorbing genuinely finer categories (cafe, bar), the same defect D91 flagged inline for Lion's Milk; cross-source, cross-category dedup with a name-core+trade-word rule closes it without over-merging distinct trade names that happen to sit close together.

Re-run #3 (2026-09-15, to 467cd5969200 after abenmayor-29's Windclimb verdict) completed with the hash guard equal at all three checks: supply_baseline.yaml re-fit (every median moved 0.00% — one POI has no material effect), street-frame, address-gaps (223 s), address-demand, pipeline, storefronts, age-fit fit (bar and childcare pass, pharmacy refused on F2b, as before) and apply (281,840 lot rows), supply-ratio, address-access, transit-profile, citibike address-measures (the fifth UPDATE-only layer, bike_run_at null = 0 on 281,842 lot rows), address-character, storefront-pipeline openings, dot-counts address-context; supply_hash 467cd5969200 on all 332,041 rows; identity exact; gap addresses 223,107 and Σgap_score 485,855 and every per-category lead-gap count identical to re-run #2. The run surfaced a latent defect (fixed, commit f8142e0): storefront_pipeline.ensure_schema applied sql/020 alone, which re-creates the VIEW analysis.poi_first_seen in its 020 form without closed_on/closed_src/is_closed, so every canonical run ending in `storefront-pipeline openings` silently stripped the columns the closure gate (sql/029) reads and broke supply_hash and two live tests; it now re-applies 027 after 020, the same pattern as poi_presence.ensure_schema. Rule: a module that applies a single migration file must re-apply the newest definition of every view that file touches (GTM-182 carries the follow-ups). Full suite after the fix: 194 passed on the affected files; earlier full run 1,773 passed / 7 skipped.

**D102 — Citi Bike phase 1 (GTM-166).** *(2026-09-14, owner: "yes absolutely. make sure we dont forget to finish this"; commits f7f3d29, ac914da)*

Built: `staging.citibike_station_month`, 1,400,477 rows, the full 44-month panel 2023-01…2026-08, 2,611 docks, 151,909,792 starts / 151,566,696 ends, member share 81.7%; dockless trips, cross-river ids (Jersey City/Hoboken leak into the NY file and are excluded by id pattern) and operator depots excluded and counted separately; start−end gap +0.23%. The completeness check distinguishes an interior outage day (2026-02-23, a system-wide zero from a storm, kept in the divisor — owner call still pending, see Next actions) from a truncated monthly file, which is refused. Pre-2021 data is refused outright: no station-id crosswalk exists before the 2021-02 schema. Seasonality: 45k (Feb 2026) → 183k (Jun 2026) starts per weekday; docks grew +20.9% 2023-01→2025-08.

Address grain (live, computed after screen re-run #2): `bike_starts_400m` / `bike_ends_400m` / `bike_evening_ends_share_400m` / `bike_casual_share_400m` on all 281,842 MN+BK lot addresses — 149,364 sit within 400 m of a dock, and 132,478 of those read a true, persisted zero rather than a NULL; 590,953 address×dock pairs stored in `analysis.address_bike_station` (`--re-sweep` is mandatory after any address-gaps rebuild, same rule as transit). Coverage: 46.9% of BK addresses sit within 400 m of a dock vs 34.9% near a subway entrance, so Brooklyn's non-zero foot-traffic coverage rises 34.9% → 60.2% once bike is added (63,205 BK addresses have a dock but no entrance). Gowanus core bike starts read p50 265 vs East Village 1,286 — the REVERSE of the transit ordering, so bike is evidence bike is not a restatement of transit coverage, not a second read of the same signal.

DOT validation: ρ +0.684 all-camera / +0.275 Brooklyn-only; per-window off-diagonal correlation ties the diagonal, so — same verdict as transit (D76) — this is card context only, never a grade and never a denominator. Licence: NYCBS data-use policy permits derived measures, forbids redistributing the raw dataset. Map: `webmap/data/bike.json` (2,491 docks); registry entry `citibike_tripdata`. GTM-166 marked **Done**; phases 2 (OD leakage, GTM-167) and 3 (activity growth + Divvy portability adapter, GTM-168) stay open.

Owner calls still outstanding: whether the 2026-02-23 storm day stays in the per-weekday divisor (QUESTIONS, below); whether bike dayparts extend `analysis.address_transit_profile` or get a sibling table (two builders would then write one DELETE scope — watch-table-proliferation caution applies).

Why: the owner asked for this explicitly and flagged the risk of it being dropped ("make sure we dont forget to finish this") — it is the first free, city-official, non-transit foot-traffic signal, and the Gowanus/East Village reversal is the first evidence any of Loci's access measures are NOT just a restatement of the transit signal.

**D103 — First-seen ledger key migration for the category-precedence dedup.** *(2026-09-14, following D101's owner ruling; commit 00eeca3, sql/035)*

Because category is part of `location_key`, D101's dedup (variant B, finer-food-category wins) re-minted keys wherever a cluster's canonical category changed: 12,011 keys went absent, 4,538 sat unresolved, collapsing to 8,134 merged groups. Without a migration the next monthly snapshot would have recorded every one of those as a fake opening or a fake disappearance, permanently, in the first-seen ledger — the exact fabrication D79 exists to forbid.

Built: `analysis.poi_key_map` and `loci poi-keys plan|apply|guard`, with three routes — relabel by `poi_id` (the source's own stable id carried the merge), relabel by location (a 40 m link rule), and merge into the surviving cluster keeping the earliest `first_seen` and latest `last_seen`; unmapped keys are never deleted. The snapshot guard refuses to run when absent keys exceed 0.5% AND ≥ 25 keys have no map entry (no-op on an empty ledger or a fixture without `poi_dedup`); `make chains-refresh` now runs the guard before every snapshot.

Applied and verified against a pre-migration copy of the warehouse: fake openings 0, vanished-without-map 0, first_seen moved later 0; ledger 227,548 → 219,133 rows = the post-dedup cluster count exactly; the snapshot carried all 219,133 by key with 0 minted and 0 gone. Dependents rewritten: `chains.brand_location` 11,189 rows (PK-collapsed 7,390, 92,008 → 84,618), `staging.poi_closure` 18 rows.

Incident: the agent's scratch verification run executed against the LIVE warehouse rather than a copy, because the write path ignored `$LOCI_DB` while the read path honoured it — fixed in the same commit; the result was independently re-verified afterward and a rollback copy of the pre-migration state was kept.

Caveats (ticketed as GTM-182, low priority — none break the migration's own guarantees above): `n_months_seen` is a max, not a history, so a merged key's month-count undercounts the true seen-span across its merged clusters; conflicting closures keep the latest `closed_on`, so a merge between two clusters with different closure verdicts silently drops the earlier one; 3,880 `gov_filing` rows folded to an earlier `source_date` (earliest wins), so `gov_filing` counts are not conserved across the migration; `sql/015`'s comment still reads `location_key = cluster_id`, stale now that category is part of the key.

Second migration-application bug found the same night (commit f8142e0, a peer): `storefront_pipeline.ensure_schema` applied sql/020 alone and re-created the VIEW `analysis.poi_first_seen` WITHOUT `closed_on`/`closed_src`/`is_closed` — so every canonical re-run ending in `storefront-pipeline openings` silently stripped the closure columns the D94 supply gate (sql/029) reads, breaking `supply_hash` and two live tests. Fixed by re-applying sql/027 after 020. Same root cause as the `init_schema` uncommitted-migration issue (D101, QUESTIONS D25, GTM-179): a schema-application path that doesn't track which migrations a VIEW/table actually depends on. Two independent instances of this class of bug in one session is itself a signal the fix belongs at the `init_schema`/`ensure_schema` level, not per-caller.

Why: a dedup rule that changes what a canonical key MEANS must migrate every downstream reader of that key or it silently corrupts the one ledger the project depends on never to fabricate an event — this is the same discipline D79 established, applied to a second kind of key change (category reassignment, not just cross-source merging).

**D104 — Legality rule corrected (grandfathering test: PLUTO retail evidence OR a commercial-category POI within 20 m whose poi_status <> 'closed'; commercial zoning always qualifies, institutional ownership and park landuse can only disqualify non-commercial zones).** *(2026-09-14, owner-ruled via contrarian review, supersedes the open-POI wording in D97; GTM-169, commit 3cd0e269)*

The legality rule (D97) as first built required an evidenced-OPEN POI within the buffer to call an address "grandfathered," and since 74% of POIs carry status unknown, "ineligible" was substantially a map of thin POI corroboration — violating D79 in the exclusionary direction. Three further defects in the first build: (1) the east-west pre-filter pad omitted `cos(latitude)` and reached 15 m, not 20; (2) institutional ownership was tested before commercial zoning, marking 1,151 NYCHA/HPD lots in C districts ineligible; (3) street rows with no BBL fell through to commercial by default. Also retracted: a builder claim that DuckDB `ST_Distance_Sphere` is broken; it takes `(lat, lon)` exactly as D16 documents, and the module now pins the flipped call against its haversine.

Ruling (owner, after contrarian review of model/address_legality.py): grandfathered = PLUTO retail evidence on the lot (retailarea > 0 OR bldgclass LIKE 'K%' OR 'S%') OR a commercial-category POI within 20 m whose `poi_status <> 'closed'` (open or unknown). Commercial zoning (C-prefix district or C1/C2 overlay) is decided FIRST and always yields commercial; institutional ownertype and park landuse can only make a non-commercially-zoned address ineligible. A fourth verdict 'unknown' applies when no PLUTO lot exists (the 50,199 street-midpoint rows); unknown is never excluded from recommendations and never greyed. Radius stays 20 m (typical lot width 6–8 m; 30 m spans three lots); the sensitivity table is recorded in `src/loci/model/address_legality.py`. Top-N and recs read paths filter ineligible at read time, not only at ledger insert.

Distribution after apply on 2026-09-14 (supply_hash 467cd5969200): commercial 49,036 (14.8%), grandfathered 19,102 (5.8%), ineligible 213,522 (64.3%), unknown 50,381 (15.2%).

Why: as first built the rule required an evidenced-open POI, and since 74% of POIs carry status unknown, "ineligible" was substantially a map of thin POI corroboration, violating D79 in the exclusionary direction (only 857 grandfathered citywide; 41 and 31 Carmine St, 35 Bedford St, 223 Franklin St and 156 Henry St, all real R-district storefronts, read ineligible). The three further defects were calculation bugs. Also retracted: a false builder claim about `ST_Distance_Sphere`.

### 2026-09-09 — Session 15 (executive session on Fable): density-shock investigation (QUESTIONS T7), development pipeline layer (D62)
- Owner's question (2026-09-09): "why don't we know when people are moving into each gap address?" revealed a four-agent stack: (a) research agent scanned for storefront opening/closing time series (none exists, Increment-2 work); (b) data-engineer assessed DCP Housing Database and DOB CO sources (available, recent, 436 jobs with CO post-DCP-cutoff); (c) developer built the pipeline layer (D62); (d) investor reviewed timing signals and lag findings from O5.
- Kill check on T7 framing: no opening/closing time series in staging.poi (only Foursquare opened_on, 2010 spike is launch date; SLA 3,205 recent-only records; DOHMH/DOS/SNAP/DCWP carry no date fields). Foursquare's opened_on is record-creation date, not store opening. LODES panel issue: registry describes "annual 2002–2023" but analysis.hex_panel has only three vintages (2002, 2013, 2023), cannot resolve <5-year lag.
- Investor answer (scratchpad 2026-09-09): lag is rational landlord optionality. A 300-unit project COing 2025-Q4 will NOT have retail open until Q3 2026 by design (landlord waits for residents, retail tenant underwriting requires lease-up, 12–18 month pre-opening). Value of delay ~$4–5M per project (foregone rent for 12 mo, marginal above the building's base value) paid mostly by the residential side (lower net operating income) and recovered via retail premium only in the top tier. Not a friction, not a timing window — a rational tradeoff. Fit: only asset owners running portfolio bets on retail density (e.g. Equity Commonwealth or Cousins operating as a developer) see the option. Developer-side amenity-retail analysis is a separate (Axis 1 investability) product; T7's causal framing is deferred.
- Owner reframing (2026-09-09, plain language): "I want to know which gap addresses are about to gain residents so I can offer a storefront lease nearby to the incoming demand." This is timing annotation, not causation. D62 builds it: nearest large project (≥50 units), stage (permitted/partial-CO/complete), and CO date sit on every address; the webmap shows which gaps are 12–24 months out from a density shock; the investor's three-decade portfolio rule applies.
- D62 built (data-engineer + developer agents): DCP Housing Database (br6q-ssj3, 109k jobs on MN+BK) joined to DOB CO feeds (bs8b-p36w + pkdm-hqz6, 436 jobs freshened post-DCP-cutoff). One new table analysis.dev_pipeline (job grain), 12 UPDATE columns on analysis.address. Webmap overlay "Development pipeline" (net_units ≥50, four stage bands, sized 50–99/100–299/300+, hollow ring for temporary CO). Data dir 42→50.6MB, suite 333→364. GTM-129 reframed to address level 2026-09-09; D62 built same day.
- T7 status: exploratory only, marked open. Timing catalysts from O5 (permit→construction 5–9yr, demographic 10–20yr market-rate-only). The contrarian caveats (2026-09-09, Opus notes) stand: no identified opening/closing series; license date ≠ store opening; flat count, more profitable unfalsifiable by the data Loci has; underwriting heuristic (permit→bid, CO+18mo→demand, 50–60%leased→signal) is provisional, unvalidated.
- Nothing committed at the time of writing.

### 2026-09-02 — Session 8e: the distance table
- Owner asked whether every data point is stored and whether a graph DB would help. Answer:
  POIs yes, distances no — `hex_access` kept counts at three thresholds and threw the Dijkstra
  results away. Graph DB: no; the graph is a road network and the only operation is
  shortest-path, which scipy does in C in seconds (D9 stands).
- **`analysis.hex_poi_distance`** — every (hex, canonical business) pair within 30 min with
  its network distance. 16.2M rows, ~3 min via `loci score` (now implemented; `make nyc`
  runs dedup → score → gaps). `hex_access` is derived from it in SQL; regression check
  matched the previous build row-for-row. `gaps`, `gaps-sweep`, `spacing` read the table;
  the nearest-of-category query answers in 60 ms instead of a 2-minute graph run.
- **Size:** the file hit 2 GB. Measured: the table is 453 MB as parquet and 533 MB in DuckDB
  without a key, 1,640 MB with the (h3_index, poi_id) primary key, which also made the load
  7× slower. Dropped the key — uniqueness holds by construction — and swapped the table in
  place. DuckDB does not shrink a file, so `data/loci.duckdb` stays ~2 GB until recreated;
  a fresh `make nyc` lands near 600 MB.
- 39 tests pass.

### 2026-09-02 — Session 8c: Foursquare in, ghosts out
- Owner accepted the Hugging Face gate and supplied `HF_TOKEN` (in `.env`; it is also in the
  session transcript — rotate it when convenient). NYC bbox extract cached in <1 min:
  821k rows → `data/raw/fsq_places_nyc.parquet`.

  restaurant / bar / cafe families, leaf labels elsewhere. Dropped on purpose: Deli (8.7k, a
  restaurant leaf that is usually a bodega in NYC — SNAP and DOHMH already anchor both sides),
  ATM, Doctor's Office (23k solo practices; would quintuple `clinic`), specialist physicians,
  contractor leaves. 191k rows map.
- **Ghost check, the important finding.** Foursquare's dates are strings; `date_closed` is
  almost never set. Corroboration by any other source, by `date_refreshed` year: 2012 → 5%,
  2019 → 11%, 2024 → 29%, 2025 → 38%, 2026 → 55%. Venues that closed simply stop being
  refreshed. Gate `LOCI_FSQ_MIN_REFRESHED=2024-01-01`: 109k of 191k mapped rows load, 54k
  canonical after dedup (299k → 224k, 25% collapsed).
- **Gap screen with everything in (SNAP + SLA + fresh FSQ): 726 hexes.** any-missing:
  bank 285, hardware 245, fitness 138, bar 93, clinic 91, childcare 60, laundry 31,
  pharmacy 21, convenience 17, grocery 2. The UNGATED Foursquare load had shown 468 hexes
  (hardware 165, fitness 79) — that drop was ghosts, not coverage. Real Foursquare effect:
  hardware 270→245, fitness 154→138, clinic 148→91.
- **Screen-design sensitivity, for the owner:** a category becomes 'expected' at 80%
  prevalence. Bank sits at 0.829 and hardware at 0.844 now; adding Foursquare pushed bank
  over the line, so 285 bank-missing hexes appeared that were not gaps yesterday. The
  threshold is a knife-edge for tier-4 categories — worth a sensitivity sweep (0.75/0.80/0.85)
  before publishing a top-N list. Filed on QUESTIONS.md M4; H-D10 asks whether Foursquare's
  'Medical Center' and 'Gym and Studio' mean what the bundle means.
- GTM-15 Done. 37 tests pass; `make check` passes. Webmap JSON still needs re-export.

**D105 — Ground-truth instrument: Interceptor-driven browser sessions against the owner's real logged-in Chrome, first use = verifying the recommendation ledger's 15 open anchors against what is physically at each address.** *(2026-09-14, session 27, abenmayor-cc; ticket GTM-183)*

Owner chose Interceptor (agent-driven CLI over the owner's real logged-in Chrome, github.com/Hacker-Valley-Media/Interceptor) as the tool for Loci's first ground-truth checks on ranked addresses. Owner chose the 15 ledger anchors as the subjects and "existing evidence table + one new observation table" as the evidence home.

Owner 2026-09-14: first session checks the four 2026-09-14 report addresses plus the Gowanus centroid; rows being added by a parallel agent (QUESTIONS D35).

Built: sql/036 (`analysis.address_observation`, `analysis.address_observation_miss`), `src/loci/model/ground_truth.py`, a `loci ground-truth` cli.py sub-app, `tests/test_ground_truth.py` (20 tests), `docs/ground-truth-protocol.md`. Grains: `address_observation` = one observed storefront per anchor per session, with a NULL-name 'vacant' row written when nothing is observed so absence is recorded, not inferred; the miss view = open, same-category, no `poi_presence` match by `name_key` within 40 m, with empty `name_key`s excluded so the tokenizer's blind spot is not reported as the model's. Match is any-category within 40 m; the miss view is deliberately same-category and narrower, so a mis-typed POI reads as a category error, not as missing supply.

Rules: human-supervised, human-paced session only — same footing as the BizQuest precedent (commit fe59eb2), never unattended. D79 preserved: a vacant/unknown observation writes no evidence row; 'closed' is written only from an explicit Maps label; a human reading "Open" off a Maps card may write 'open', unlike `web_rules.py`, which never emits open because a search hit not saying closed is not evidence of trading. `address_observation` is NOT wired into the screen, forecast, or grades — an instrument that scores the screen must not feed it.

Why path (b) on the evidence source: DuckDB 1.5.5 rejects `DROP`/`ADD CONSTRAINT` on a `CHECK` (probed, `NotImplementedException`), and `db.init_schema` applies every `*.sql` on disk (GTM-179/182), so widening the shared evidence table's `CHECK` by a copy-swap would execute inside peer sessions mid-write. Evidence rows instead use `source='web'`, `domain_class='maps_ui'` — `domain_class` already carries source kind and `poi_evidence.basis()` renders it with zero changes.

Finding: all 15 open ledger recs share one anchor (the 2026-09-11 Gowanus bbox centroid, `anchor_address_id NULL`), so a session against them is one look producing 15 category verdicts, not 15 address checks — recorded in QUESTIONS D35, now answered (owner: add the four report addresses as subjects).

Tests: 80 passed across test_ground_truth/test_poi_evidence/test_recommendation; 227 passed across the init_schema neighbours. Interceptor NOT installed. Warehouse writes this session: four `loci recommendations add` rows (376 Graham Ave, 379 Broome St, 4 East 8th St, 545 Sackett St; issued_by abenmayor-cc) — no closure evidence, no poi_status write by intent.

Addendum 2026-09-15 (hash incident, logged at the hash steward's request; cross-referenced from the re-baseline entry): creating sql/036 at ~23:47 on 2026-09-14 moved the live supply hash 467cd5969200 → d993b3802c6a even though 036 names no poi_status or evidence object. `db.init_schema` sweeps every `*.sql` on disk on any write connection; the next write connection was this session's four ledger adds, and the sweep's poi_status re-render flushed closure evidence already in the warehouse, flipping nine POIs to closed under every artefact stamped that night (baseline, address rows, age-fit, revenue calibration, forecast vintage, map). 036 was announced to peers and written last, but the ledger adds ran on top of it before the steward could take the first write — that ordering was the gap. Rule adopted: a new `sql/*.sql` file is itself a hash-moving event; announce it like a poi_status write, create it last, and the hash steward makes the first write connection after it. The four ledger rows carry supply_ratio_at_issue from reports fitted on 467cd5969200; the steward flags them if the morning re-baseline moves those ratios. Re-baseline is the steward's first action on 2026-09-15.

Addendum 2 (2026-09-15, after the re-baseline, D106 commit 084ca55): the steward's flag on the four 2026-09-14 ledger rows was verified. `supply_ratio_at_issue` is defined as the card's `supply_ratio_vs_base` (recommendation_ledger.py:528 ← recommend.py:541, the area median of `analysis.address_category.supply_ratio_vs_base`, the D73 count-based ratio), not `analysis.address_gaps.{cat}_ratio`, which is the distance ratio nearest_m / reach. Three rows were correct under that definition (4 East 8th and 545 Sackett convenience 0.0 = zero open principled POIs within reach, a real reading with the reports' own status-coverage caveat; 376 Graham bank 0.36 = area median, address value 0.35). The 379 Broome convenience row held the distance ratio 0.4908 where the count ratio is 0.309: a data-entry error by the agent that added it, not a hash move. Fixed 2026-09-15, ledger-only and announced: r-20260914-379-broome-street--convenience-6045f4 withdrawn with the reason recorded, re-issued as r-20260914-379-broome-street--convenience-401904 (supply_ratio_at_issue 0.309, issued_on kept 2026-09-14, solution text now labels both measures). Lesson: two measures in this codebase are both called "supply ratio" in prose; the ledger holds the count-based one, and the reports print both. Manifest regenerated: data/ground_truth/manifest-2026-09-15.json, 19 anchors.

Why: the ledger's monthly outcome check (D89) matches only against Foursquare first-seen and storefront filings, so nothing today can contradict a recommendation with what is physically at the anchor. The load-bearing output is `analysis.address_observation_miss`: an open storefront of the recommended category that the supply model does not hold is a direct hit on P3 (coverage-gap validity). A confirmed gap at the anchor does not by itself confirm the 400 m catchment gap; the protocol lists that and four other threats.

**D106 — One quiet-warehouse re-baseline instead of a fourth chase: every stamped artefact moved to supply d993b3802c6a in a single pass.** *(2026-09-15, owner: "continue"; abenmayor-cf)*

Decided: after the fourth hash drift of the day (D105: 467cd5969200 → d993b3802c6a, nine POIs flipped closed when sql/036's creation forced a poi_status re-render), all sessions were frozen and the hash steward ran the whole re-stamp chain once, including the peers' steps: `supply-ratio --fit-baseline` (77 s, every median moved 0.000%), street-frame, address-gaps (215 s), address-demand, pipeline, storefronts, `age-fit fit` (bar and childcare pass; pharmacy refused, Brooklyn 65+ CI includes 0) and apply, supply-ratio, address-access, transit-profile, citibike address-measures, address-character (345 s), storefront-pipeline openings (208 s), dot-counts address-context, `address-legality build` (3,449 s — the long pole; commercial 49,036 / grandfathered 19,102 / ineligible 213,522 / unknown 50,381), `revenue fit` (345 s; restaurant alone passes, ε 0.4, β 1.25, γ −0.5, ρ_oos 0.829) and `revenue --boroughs MN,BK` (223 s), `forecast issue --month 2026-09 --force` (1,050 s; model_version 0.1.1+ae3eadb3, supply_hash d993b3802c6a, 4,227,630 rows, AUC 0.8768 vs 0.8513 no-score; earlier vintages kept as history), forecast-export, citibike export, `export-webmap` (148 files, 123.6 MB, meta.json stamps d993b3802c6a for screen and ageFit). Hash guard equal at six checks. Result: the gap screen is byte-identical to re-run #3 (223,107 gap addresses, Σgap_score 485,854.61, Σgap_score_fit 401,798.02, every lead-gap count unchanged, lead_censored BK 14,422 / MN 720); only supply_hash and legality (previously NULL) changed on analysis.address; suite 1,876 passed / 1 skipped; commit 17900c2. abenmayor-cc is verifying its four 2026-09-14 ledger rows against d993b3802c6a (the re-run check compared a count-based ratio with the rows' distance ratio, so no move is established); outcome logged under D105. Why: three canonical re-runs earlier in the night were each chased by another session's write (a closure verdict, a key migration, a migration file's sweep); the fix was not a fourth chase but a freeze of every session followed by one pass that also ran the absent peers' steps (revenue, forecast) so nothing ended a hash behind. Rules confirmed: a 'final' signal freezes poi_status writes, migrations AND view re-renders; the steward runs the first write after any new sql/*.sql; peers' re-stamp steps are part of the canonical order, not separate follow-ups. Cross-reference D105 for the cause and D94/D101 for the two supply changes this re-stamps.

**D107 — First ground-truth session: 12 of 19 recommendation anchors have open supply of the recommended category inside 400 m; five warehouse POIs found Permanently closed; the instrument stays a verifier and is not wired into the screen.** *(2026-09-15, session 27 continued, abenmayor-cc; ticket GTM-183 Done, follow-ups GTM-185)*

Setup: Interceptor Browser 1.0.1 installed (pkg SHA256 verified, Developer ID Hacker Valley Media LLC, notarized); first extension install landed in Arc because the Web Store link followed the OS default browser — reinstalled in Chrome; one Chrome context. 57 page loads, human-paced (3 s waits), zero timeouts, dedicated tab group loci-ground-truth, owner present throughout.

Protocol change from the live probe: the coordinate pin URL lists no businesses; the reads that work are the category-nearby search (`/maps/search/<term>/@lat,lon,18z`), the address panel ("At this place"), and Street View. Manifest now carries nearby_url (per-category search term table), address_url (MapPLUTO street address by BBL, fallback rec area_label, then neighborhood), area_kind; write is atomic. Commit f579c08.

Results (run gt-2026-09-15-session1): verdicts supply_missed 12, confirmed_gap 7, inconclusive 0. Address anchors: 376 Graham bank — Cross County Savings Bank open 122 m away (cleanest falsification; building holds Thee Brooklyn Barber + El Punto Cubano, open); 379 Broome convenience — two open stores ≤150 m (confirms the pre-registered no-gap conclusion); 4 East 8th convenience — four open stores ≤300 m (the 0.0 ratio was a status-coverage hole); 545 Sackett convenience — nearest open store 407 m, but the parcel is a fenced construction lot, not a retail site. Gowanus centroid: 9 of 15 categories have open supply inside 400 m; bank/hair/pharmacy/hardware are gaps at 524–732 m; convenience (410 m) and laundry (405 m) sit within measurement error of the radius.

**D109 — Chain watchlist admission: two tiers, a candidate predicate, exclusions, incumbents kept off the lead list.** *(2026-09-15, owner, GTM-186–191)*

The list had no intake: all 123 rows arrived in one seeded payload on
2026-09-13 (misread as 121 while a peer was mid-edit) and nothing had been
added since — until two more rows (All'Antico Vinaio, Super Burrito) landed
the same way on 2026-09-15, which is what forced the question. Owner ruled:
a brand enters the mechanical CANDIDATE pool at `locations_total >= 5` OR
`detect.flag_for` (≥3 new in 12m, or ≥2 new of ≤8), after aliasing, with
`n_sources >= 2`, not in an excluded class, and at least one movement signal
(opening, press hit, or filing) in 12 months — "5+" alone was rejected as
the primary gate because on the 2026-09-13 snapshot it would have admitted
2,341 unlisted brands including banks and gas stations while missing 244
flagged sub-5 brands (150 of them two-sourced) that `flag_for` exists to
catch, and 41 of the 123 already-admitted brands have no detect row at all
(nothing licenses a climbing gym) — so the count is a floor, not the
predicate. Excluding banks and health-system practice lines and requiring
12-month movement narrows the 2,341 to 697, the honest size of the backlog.
National incumbents (100+ locations: Dunkin', Starbucks, Chase) are admitted
rather than excluded, but with `sales_role: incumbent`, sorted out of the
broker lead-list default order and reserved for the D19 "brand X opening
nearby" recommend-card signal — a broker doesn't want "Dunkin' is
expanding," the card genuinely does. Hard exclusions, each with a reason:
banks, health-system practice lines, agent networks (MoneyGram/Western
Union — counters inside bodegas, not storefronts, and the fastest-"growing"
name in the first raw detect run), wireless carriers, parking, ATMs,
government/postal, and fuel-branded convenience. Contraction-tier brands
(Rite Aid, Walgreens) stay on the list as `sales_role: contraction` — a
pharmacy net-closure is the supply event that made a laundromat chain's
expansion possible, which is a lead in the other direction. First-run
backlog: the owner will triage the top 100 unlisted candidates by
`locations_new_12m` by hand; the rest waits for the 2026-10 snapshot delta
rather than a multi-session sweep of all 697. Quarterly re-verification is
the only path to `confidence: verified` — as of today 0 of 123 admitted rows
carry a `last_verified` date and 0 are verified, so the first quarterly pass
is the first time anyone here will have counted a store rather than read a
number off a seeded payload.

Caveat on every count above: they were read on the 2026-09-13 detect
snapshot. A 2026-09-15 rebuild under the fixed normalizer and the
post-D101/D103 deduped ledger moved the base materially (20,989 detected
brands vs 26,151, 660 flagged vs 436, 2,241 at 5+ locations vs 2,403) — the
snapshot shrank roughly a fifth on dedup alone, which is itself the reason
aliasing is C1's precondition and not an afterthought: the same rebuild
caught "Raising Cane's Chicken Fingers" (17 locations) still splitting from
"Raising Cane's" (14) as one chain the 2026-09-15 rebuild detects at 20 locations / 7 new in 12m once aliased (the dedup collapses the overlap rather than summing 17+14). All'Antico
Vinaio's two seeded rows also collapsed to one key at 10 locations / 0 new
12m under the fix. Treat 2,341 / 244 / 697 as illustrations of scale on the
2026-09-13 snapshot, not as the current backlog — re-derive them on the
first real `loci chains candidates` run (T1) against the rebuilt base.

Two questions remain open and are not part of this ruling: what evidence
promotes a row to `confidence: verified` beyond a store-locator count, and
whether to request a RetailStat quote now or wait for revenue (QUESTIONS
D46/D47).

Owner rulings, all on the recommended option: (R1) the revenue model's λ stays untouched — it is a six-term identification constant (true leakage, commuter inflow, non-household demand, CEX mapping error, unit-to-household gap, POI-vs-EC count gap) that an OD matrix cannot decompose, so wiring bike leakage into it would silently move every shipped revenue number; OD leakage ships as a table, a view and card measures only, with the Huff denominator as the entry point if it ever graduates (QUESTIONS D42). (R2) the ticket's literal "AM/PM share > 1" residential-dock rule is arithmetically backwards — measured, it types 59 of 2,611 docks residential because the 15–19 window carries more volume everywhere — replaced by the weekday start:end ratio-of-ratios (am_starts/am_ends)/(pm_starts/pm_ends), >1.2 residential, <0.8 destination, else mixed, <200 trips in the station-month unknown; on the whole panel residential docks originate 50.3% of trips, destination 33.2%, mixed 15.6%, unknown 0.9%. (R3) NTA-grain card values are stamped on every address in the NTA, labelled neighbourhood-wide, NULL never 0 where the NTA has no residential dock or too few trips.

Built: sql/037_citibike_od.sql — analysis.bike_od_leakage at (origin_nta, destination_nta, month, day_type, daypart, origin_type) with trips/member_trips/round_trips stored separately, view analysis.bike_od_leakage_evening (window = evening any day OR any daypart Saturday/Sunday, counted once, weekday pm_peak excluded as the commute home, share partitioned by origin × month), nullable bike_od_* columns on analysis.address and bike_od_supplied_share on analysis.address_category (filled only where ratio > 1, D39). Modules sources/cities/nyc/citibike_od.py (imports phase 1's SQL helpers, never redefines them; month is the unit of idempotence; a trip is dated by started_at so conservation against phase 1 holds in every station-month cell, shortfall 0.19–0.49% = the dockless/JC ends) and model/bike_od.py (dock→NTA via python h3 res-9 → analysis.hex, the same rule addresses use — NOT the DuckDB h3_latlng_to_cell, whose BIGINT never matches the VARCHAR h3_index and joins zero rows silently). CLI: loci citibike od-ingest / od-stats / od-measures / od-validate. 70 tests in tests/test_citibike_od.py and tests/test_bike_od.py.

Ingest: 44 contiguous months 2023-01..2026-08, 133 origin → 133 destination NTAs, 151,489,130 NY-dock-to-NY-dock trips, 2.25% round, ~5.24M rows, 14,439 distinct NTA pairs, 0 unmapped docks in every month; ~6 s per month, ~4.5 min for the panel (all 33 archives already on disk).

Red-team (contrarian, before the first measures write) found the supplied-share as first built was a Manhattan indicator: POIs per lot-frame address put 27 of 110 NTAs above median in all 15 categories and 36 in none (86.6% identical indicator across category pairs; MN 0.90 vs BK 0.13), and phantom NTAs (BX0401: 1 lot address, 728 open POIs) sat inside the measurable universe reading 100% supplied. Fixed before any write: density is POIs per 1,000 residential units; an NTA enters the universe only with ≥500 lot-frame addresses; the outside-universe NULL threshold tightened 0.5 → 0.2 and the dropped share stored as bike_od_outside_share; the 200-trip floor now applies to the supplied-share denominator; needs_rebuild watches address_category and keys on the live supply hash (bike_od_supply_hash), not the window label; the card names the denominator ("of the trips that leave here for a Manhattan or Brooklyn neighbourhood we can measure (X% went elsewhere)"), labels Queens/Bronx destinations by name, and the report carries the monthly IQR of top-share (3.1% at the median NTA, 6.6% at p90 — the pooled 12-month window is summer-weighted, Aug ≈ 2.5× Feb).

Measures (window 2025-09..2026-08): 124 origin NTAs above the trip floor, 10,479 pairs, 79 NTAs in the measurable universe; median out-of-NTA share 84.5%; 22.3% of outbound trips land outside the universe, which drops 74 origin NTAs from supplied_share (QUESTIONS D44) plus 2 under the floor; 332,041 MN+BK addresses stamped, 200,976 carry a destination, 131,065 NULL; 226,063 address_category rows carry bike_od_supplied_share (per-category medians: restaurant 0.806, pharmacy 0.428, bank 0.252). Card readings: Gowanus BK0601 — 31% of evening/weekend trips from residential docks stay, 69% leave; Bushwick BK0401 — 15% stay, 85% leave.

Validation (D76 graduation): destination-NTA evening/weekend inflow vs DOT PM/evening pedestrian counts, 43 NTAs over 76 count points: ρ +0.327 against the +0.50 bar; the density baseline (POIs per 1,000 units vs DOT) is +0.005, so the flow beats it. Category placebo: every category beats the null baseline (excess +0.211 to +0.824) — but the null collapsed to 0.6% once density was per-unit (only 2 NTAs are above median in all 15 categories), so that gate is unfalsifiable as built and proves nothing (QUESTIONS D43). **Verdict: CONTEXT ONLY.** Enters no gap_score, no supply_ratio_vs_base, no grade, no λ. The card says "riders," never "residents" (caveat 1).

Migration hazard handled: 037 was developed as 037_citibike_od.sql.draft (invisible to init_schema's *.sql glob), tests applied the draft's text to a temporary DuckDB, and the rename was announced to abenmayor-cc and abenmayor-61 and made only after abenmayor-cc's ground-truth writes completed. This is a working answer to QUESTIONS D25 (QUESTIONS D45). My writes did not move the supply hash: it is ba944e18c57b before and after (moved by D107, not D108). The baseline-hash drift test in tests/test_supply_ratio.py stays red until the re-baseline D107 assigns.

Earlier the same session: 035434b — citibike-ingest now reports system-wide zero-trip dates and states they stay in the weekday divisor (D28 surfaced, not resolved).

Phase 3 (GTM-168) is now unblocked on phase 2; it still waits on the entry-retrodiction panel re-run.

**D108 addendum (2026-09-15, session 28, abenmayor-db) — owner rulings on QUESTIONS D44 and D28.** D44: report every origin NTA's bike_od_supplied_share; the 0.2 outside-universe NULL cut (which dropped 74 of 124 origins) is removed on the D75 no-eligibility-gate footing, and bike_od_outside_share is always stored and printed on both card lines. The 200-trip floor on the inside-universe denominator stays: that is a no-denominator rule, not a gate. Measures re-run (commit 82db230): 332,041 addresses stamped, 200,976 with a destination, 242,772 address_category rows now carry supplied_share (was 226,063); per origin NTA the outside share is 27.2% at the median, 93.5% at the 90th percentile, 98.0% at worst; 17 origins are NULL under the trip floor. D28: the 2026-02-23 system-wide zero-trip day stays in the per-weekday divisor (an average weekday that month really included a day the docks were shut; dropping it would select on the outcome) — the behaviour 035434b already prints, no code change.
