**D110 — Chain watchlist tier 3 `watch`: pre-chain operators at one or two locations with an intent signal, internal-only until they graduate; Tavily budget 60 → 68.** *(2026-09-15, owner, GTM-192)*

The D109 candidate predicate starts at five locations or `flag_for`'s "2 new of ≤8", so by
construction it meets a brand only after it has expanded; the owner asked for the earlier
read — "identify chains before they grow. Thinking bathhouse, perhaps Mink" (QUESTIONS D48).
Tier 3 is `watch`: an operator with **one or two open MN+BK locations** plus **at least one
intent signal in 12 months**, not in a D109 excluded class. It must read
`analysis.poi_first_seen`, not `chains.brand_latest` — `detect.MIN_LOCATIONS = 2` means a
one-site operator has no brand row at all — keyed on `chains.normalize.brand_key` so a row
graduates to CANDIDATE without changing key, and explicitly **not** on
`poi_first_seen.name_key`, which is token-sorted while `storefront_filing.business_name_key`
is not. No category gate, for the same reason D109 admits null-`loci_category` rows and a
sharper one: Bathhouse's own POI rows split across `nails_beauty` and `restaurant`, so any
category filter would admit or drop the owner's headline example by coin-flip;
`pipeline_coverage` (GTM-189) carries `real`/`structural_zero` beside the count so a zero
for a spa reads as unmeasured, not as nothing coming. A `watch` row is **internal only**
(owner ruling): it never reaches the broker lead list or a D19 card until it graduates,
because the evidence behind it is one filing or one headline and putting a single-site
operator in front of a broker as a growing chain on that basis would cost the list its
credibility — the pool below has a named false-positive tail that makes the risk concrete.
Graduation is the D109 predicate firing, which is also the moment the row becomes externally
visible, with `watched_since` retained so the tier's lead time becomes measurable rather than
claimed; expiry is 18 months without a new signal, set against D80's own clock (fit-out →
first-inspection p75 = 378 days, so a 12-month expiry would drop operators still mid-build).
The retrospective is the reason the tier is scoped to signals (a), (b), (e) and a new (f),
and not (d):
**Bathhouse** is already a D109 candidate today (`locations_total` 5, flagged "2 new of only
5") but that is half artefact — three physical sites fragmented into five clusters, and one
of the two "new" locations is the **2019** Williamsburg site re-dated by its 2026-03-24
DOHMH row — and it fired in 2026-09, **32 months after** the Flatiron site opened in January
2024. At December 2023 the warehouse could see **none** of the D48 signals: the earliest
Flatiron filing is a DOHMH inspection dated 2024-04-02, three months *post*-opening, and the
deeper reason is structural — in `staging.storefront_filing` every feed except DOHMH begins
**2024-09-13** (SLA pending: 1,542 rows, 2024-10-01 onward), because they are live-status
snapshots with no history, so signal (a) can never be back-tested before late 2024 and can
only accrue forward. `chains.press_hits` is likewise a 45-day queue (443 rows, 2026-07-30 →
2026-09-12), so signal (b) is unarchived rather than absent; the Crain's Flatiron-expansion
piece that would have given ~12 months of warning was published before Loci was collecting.
The $35M Imaginary Ventures round (May 2026) post-dates the second opening by two years and
is no help for it. Where the tier would have won is the **third** site: the 540 Atlantic Ave
`liquor_application` is dated 2026-02-04 against a 2026-05-01 activation — **86 days** of
warning, and seven months ahead of D109. **Mink is Mink Padel** (owner, West Harlem, 439 W
127th St, Manhattan's first public padel courts, two outdoor courts in the courtyard of
Janus Property's converted Mink Building, opened 2025) **and Loci holds nothing on it** —
zero rows in `poi_first_seen`, `staging.poi`, `storefront_filing`, `storefront_pipeline` and
`press_hits`; the nearest ledger POI to the address is a barber shop 70 m away. That is the
more important half of the retrospective, because it is not a gap that accrual fixes: a
padel club with no food or drink enters no feed Loci reads. **Padel Haus**, by contrast, is
in the warehouse at three BBLs — but only through its café (DOHMH "Juice, Smoothies, Fruit
Salads" 2023-08-09; `liquor_active` 2026-03-10 / 05-08 / 05-12), i.e. the filings see the
bar, not the sport. And where a premium operator's landlord does file, the filing carries
the **landlord's** name: 429 W 127th St has DOB fit-outs under "Janus Property Company"
(2025-10-28, 2025-11-26 "convert the use and occupancy of the first floor"), and 418 W 127th
a 2024-11-12 fence-and-gate fit-out with **no business name at all** — no brand-key rule can
recover "Mink Padel" from either. Signal (e) also cannot fire, because "Mink" is the
building's name, not an operator family. One route does work and is new to this design:
the only filing in the warehouse that names padel is **"Padel State"** (73 West St BK, DOB
`fitout_filing` 2025-07-25, not yet open, null category) found by its work description —
*"temporary installation of Prefabricated Assembly Padel Ball Courts"* — so tier 3 adds a
signal (f), a monthly derived scan of fit-out free text, which is the only in-warehouse
route to premium categories and costs nothing. Pool size for
signal (a) alone, measured today: 40,691 brand keys have one or two open MN+BK locations in
the five filing-real categories; **168** of them (0.41%) have a 12-month filing more than
150 m from every existing site, of which **22 have not yet opened** — all 22 entered at
`liquor_application`, so the SLA pending feed is the whole workhorse. That number is a floor
with a false-positive tail, not an estimate: name-key matching across filings and POIs is
lossy (D80 mapped 20.6% of filings to a category; franchisee and operating-company filings
are invisible to the brand key), 19 rows were dropped for missing coordinates, the 150 m
rule is a proxy because the ledger carries lon/lat and not BBL, and the observed false
positives are named — CulinArt (a contract caterer), Watches of Switzerland (a retailer's
bar licence), FLORIDITA 4159 LLC, and generic keys like "el malecon restaurant" matching
19.9 km away — and, per the Mink Padel finding, the pool is food-and-drink **by
construction**: all 22 are liquor applications, so no gym, spa, climbing wall or padel club
can appear in it at any threshold, the same blind spot D109 records when it notes that 41 of
123 admitted brands have no detect row because nothing licenses a climbing gym. Budget:
(a), the "Brand II" hint for (e) and the new fit-out-text scan (f) are derived monthly
inside the existing filings pipeline at no new spend; (b) needs a new Tavily
`query_kind = 'watch'`, and the owner **rejected re-splitting the code-enforced ceiling and
ruled a raise instead — `research.MAX_QUERIES` 60 → 68**, keeping GTM-191's 12/24/24 intact
and adding 8 watch queries. The re-split would have funded tier 3 by stretching the
admitted-brand re-verification cycle from five months to seven, and D109 already records
that 0 of 123 admitted rows are `verified` or carry a `last_verified` date, so it would have
slowed the one process that has never yet completed once; the cost of the ruling instead is
explicit and small, 8 Tavily credits a month (~13%), still enforced in code, with discovery
first in `build_plan` so truncation eats watch triage before discovery. Signal (d),
expansion-role job postings, is dropped: no free source, highest noise, lowest yield. Human
cost ~30 minutes a month on top of D109's 45–60. Left open deliberately: whether tier 3
should accept categories the filings cannot see at all — if it does, (b), (c) and (f) are
its only routes and the honest expectation is a handful of rows a year, not a pipeline; the
recommendation is to accept them and make `structural_zero` render as "no filing coverage
for this category" rather than a silent zero. Not built.

**D111 — Citi Bike phase 3: activity growth adds nothing to entry prediction; CONTEXT ONLY; reader proven portable to Divvy (GTM-168).** *(2026-09-15, session 28, abenmayor-db; ticket GTM-168 Done)*

Owner rulings (all on the recommended option): (R1) back-ingest 2021-02..2022-12 and run two retrodiction vintages — t0 = 2023-01 (growth on a COVID-recovery base, the only line-for-line comparison with D88) and t0 = 2025-01 (clean growth, right-censored outcome); (R2) the feature enters the 2026-10 forecast vintage as model 0.2.0 only if every gate passes, otherwise CONTEXT ONLY (D76); (R3) Divvy scope = parameterise the reader per system, prove one Chicago month, write the PORTABILITY entry; (R4) the ship criterion is ratified by the statistician before any fit — the pre-registration (P0–P12) is in the run's report and reproduced in docs/retrodiction-2026-09.md's D111 section: one primary test (pooled 6 categories, t0 = 2023-01, member-only, direct, NTA-blocked CV), baseline FULL + docks_added_24m, identical rows and folds, floor = max(+0.005, placebo-Δ p95) with the CD-clustered two-sided 95% CI lower limit > +0.002, NTA-block-donation placebo run BEFORE the real fit, fold-seed stability (Δ > 0 in ≥ 18/20), 2025-01 confirmatory with a joint-resample compatibility CI, BH q = 0.10 over the declared family, Moran's I and effective-n diagnostics, co-movement language only.

Built (commit 692fde8; sql/038 landed after peer clearance, the D108 .draft pattern): analysis.address_bike_growth at address × asof_month — 12m-over-12m log-ratio of weekday MEMBER throughput within the 400 m reachable dock set, on a BALANCED dock set only (first_month ≤ M−23, last_month ≥ M; a dock born inside the window contributes to neither window), NULL below a 0.5 balanced share, detrended against the system (bike_growth_12m_rel is the feature), docks_added_24m stored as a separate control; CLI citibike growth-measures / growth-stats; validation/retrodiction.py D111 growth test with CLI flags on retrodiction run; sources/cities/lyft_bikeshare.py SYSTEMS (nyc_citibike, chicago_divvy) with nyc/citibike.py reduced to a binding over it and every NYC SQL string proven byte-identical; citibike divvy-probe. 69 new tests; suite 2,033 passed (the one failure is D107's baseline-hash drift test).

Back-ingest: 2021-02..2022-12 in two sittings (paused once for abenmayor-61's owner-waiting query — the ingest holds an exclusive DuckDB lock that refuses even read-only connections); panel now 67 contiguous months 2021-02..2026-08. Growth measures: asof 2023-01 — 1,224 of 2,684 docks balanced (81.8% of system activity), 626 born inside the window and excluded, 87,203 of 281,842 lot addresses carry a value; asof 2025-01 — 1,808 balanced (89.0%), 468 excluded, 116,188 carry a value; validation zero failures.

Confirmatory vintage 2025-01 (runtime 542 s): P2 attrition 59.0% of panel rows (4,917 of 12,000 addresses carry growth; retained addresses are denser and more retail — std diff +0.95 retail_index, +1.57 log_homes), so every claim is about the DOCK-MATURE CORE; effective n 2,668 distinct dock sets; feature Moran's I +0.81, across-NTA +0.30 (CD blocking binds). FULL 0.8755, baseline FULL+docks_added 0.8756, +growth 0.8756: Δ-AUC +0.0000; placebo Δ p95 +0.0002 so the floor is the absolute +0.005; CD-clustered CI [−0.0005, +0.0006]; fold-seed Δ positive 1/20; auxiliary R² 0.052 (the feature carries independent variation — it is simply uninformative); residual Moran's I 0.683 → 0.683; coefficient −0.42 [−1.29, +0.44]; all six per-category Δs "not a finding" under BH. Ascertainment rule truncated nothing (0 months).

Primary vintage 2023-01 (runtime 107 s; cohort 10,495; the model's own score lift reproduces D88 — entry AUC 0.8674 vs 0.8518 without the score, +0.0155): P2 attrition 69.3% of panel rows (3,678 of 12,000 addresses carry growth — the 2021–22 balanced dock set is smaller), effective n 2,058 distinct dock sets. FULL 0.8314, baseline FULL+docks_added 0.8312, +growth 0.8309: Δ-AUC −0.0004; placebo Δ p95 +0.0007 (floor stays the absolute +0.005); CD-clustered CI [−0.0020, +0.0013]; fold-seed Δ positive 10/20; auxiliary R² 0.285; residualised Δ +0.0001 (flagged: larger than the direct lift, itself a red flag under P6); residual Moran's I 0.679 → 0.680; coefficient +0.67 [−0.16, +1.50] — indistinguishable from zero, and opposite in sign to the confirmatory vintage's −0.42. Ascertainment truncated nothing.

Verdict: CONTEXT ONLY on both vintages — P3 fails (Δ below the floor, CI lower limit below +0.002), P8 fails (10/20 and 1/20 positive seeds), P7 moot. The Citi Bike activity series enters no grade, no supply ratio, no forecast (FEATURE_LIST and MODEL_SEMVER unchanged at 0.1.1). Failure ships as a finding (P12): the change in bike activity before t0 co-moves with nothing the entry model does not already carry — consistent with D88 (the screen ranks retail streets) and with phases 1–2 (levels and flows are card context only).

Portability (R3): one real Divvy month (2025-06: 678,904 trips, 386,811 member / 292,093 casual, dockless ends 156,772 = 23.1% REPORTED under a per-system gate, not dropped) landed as 13,600 station-month rows in a scratch DB with the day_type × daypart partition summing exactly; a latent CRLF header bug in the shared extractor (only Chicago's files trigger it) fixed; docs/PORTABILITY.md §8 (generated from src/loci/portability.py) says portable = the trip-file reader and the station-month grain travel, NOT Loci in Chicago (no address frame, walk graph, PLUTO, supply set → no address measures, no growth feature, no claim about Chicago retail).

Reporting quibbles to fix (QUESTIONS D50): the verdict text assumes the run is the primary vintage even when --asof is the confirmatory one; the lagged-growth pre-trend (P9) column was not built for the 2025-01 run, moot for a null contemporaneous effect but required before any positive result could ship; the run stamps supply hash d993b3802c6a from the baseline table rather than the live ba944e18c57b — provenance should read the live hash.

Citi Bike plan (owner 2026-09-14, "make sure we don't forget to finish this") is COMPLETE: phase 1 D102, phase 2 D108, phase 3 D111. GTM-168 Done.

**D112 — address-legality long pole removed (3,449 s → 3 s) and the canonical order re-baselined on supply ba944e18c57b (GTM-169; D107's owed re-baseline).** *(2026-09-15, session 28, abenmayor-db; ticket GTM-169)*

Long pole: EXPLAIN on the live warehouse showed the open-POI match in model/address_legality.open_poi_match_sql running as a NESTED_LOOP_JOIN — four inequality predicates and no equality, and DuckDB costed analysis.poi_supply_status (a four-CTE view over 3.8M poi_dedup rows) at ~1 row, so it evaluated ~6.8e10 address × POI pairs. Fix (commit c36cd18): both sides floor to 0.001° cells, the POI side fans to its 3×3 neighbourhood as a MATERIALIZED CTE (without MATERIALIZED DuckDB inlines the view nine times and is slower than the loop: 561 s vs 296 s on a 50k sample), the join is on cell equality, and the original bbox-pad + haversine predicates follow unchanged; assert_grid_covers_pad() refuses a cell narrower than the pad. Equivalence proven read-only before any write: EXCEPT ALL both ways is (0, 0) on the full universe (57,106 match rows) and at 2k/50k samples; the reference SQL is kept as open_poi_match_reference_sql and pinned by five new tests (bucket-boundary fixture, no duplicate fan-out, grid-covers-pad at 40.4–45.0N, loud failure, MATERIALIZED regression). Bare SELECT: 178 s → 1.0 s on the full frame. Standalone `address-legality build` after the fix: ~4 s wall-clock; inside the canonical order (step 15, after address-gaps wiped the columns): 3 s. sql/031's "~90 s" comment corrected. Option (a), a spatial R-tree, was rejected: no persisted index and D104 pins this module off ST_Distance_Sphere.

Re-baseline (this session's runner, per-step timings, the D106 order plus the D108/D111 re-sweeps after step 11): 01 fit-baseline 78 s (332,041 address rows, 4,980,615 address_category rows; median drift: not printed); 02 street-frame 1 s; 03 address-gaps 221 s (332,041 scored points: 281,842 lot + 50,199 street); 04 address-demand 4 s; 05 pipeline 57 s; 06 storefronts 73 s; 07a age-fit fit 6 s — bar and childcare pass and are written, pharmacy refused by its own F2b criterion exactly as D69/D71/D106 (the refusal exits 1 by design; the runner first treated it as fatal and was resumed from 07b); 07b age-fit apply 5 s; 08 supply-ratio 73 s; 09 address-access 46 s; 10 transit-profile 43 s; 11 citibike address-measures 3 s (590,953 station rows, 149,364 addresses measured); 11b od-measures --re-sweep 3 s (332,041 stamped, 200,976 with a destination, 242,772 address_category rows — identical to D108-addendum counts); 11c growth-measures --re-sweep 3 s (87,203 / 116,188 values at 2023-01 / 2025-01 — identical to D111); 12 character 68 s (D106: 345 s); 13 openings 48 s (D106: 208 s); 14 dot-context 2 s; 15 legality 3 s (D106: 3,449 s); 16 revenue fit 149 s (D106: 345 s); 17 revenue 82 s (D106: 223 s); 18 forecast issue 2026-09 --force 292 s (D106: 1,050 s; 4,227,630 rows issued, fit 360,000 rows / 12,000 addresses / 103 NTAs, blocked-CV AUC 0.8768, model_version 0.1.1+51bab17f); 19 forecast-export 16 s; 20 citibike export 3 s; 21 export-webmap 40 s (148 files, 123.6 MB, largest gaps/bar.json 15.93 MB). Total wall-clock 17:31:40 → 17:54:54 ≈ 23 min including the one-minute resume gap, against D106's ~98 min of timed steps.

Outcome: model/supply_baseline.yaml carries ba944e18c57b; analysis.address_gaps rows stamp ba944e18c57b; the forecast vintage is re-issued on it; webmap meta.json carries it; tests/test_supply_ratio.py::test_baseline_supply_hash_matches_the_live_poi_supply is green again (suite 2,139 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed at 18:12 — the drift test is green for the first time since D107). If tickets change, edit `src/loci/tickets.py` and re-run `loci gen-tickets` — then push the
delta. Never hand-edit `docs/TICKETS.md`. If a ticket is renamed, `make check` will flag every
question in `docs/QUESTIONS.md` that cited the old title.

When a question gets answered, update its **Status** and **Current answer** in
`docs/QUESTIONS.md` in the same session.
